# bts-sbom 저장소 분석 보고서 (코어 `bts_sbom` + 앱 계층, 분석 시점 코드명 `sbomplus` = 현재 `bts_sbom_app`)

- 분석일: 2026-11-17
- 분석 대상: `C:\firebaseprojects\beyondthesecurity-sbom` (HEAD `96f89c2`, Python 약 2.3만 줄, 표준 라이브러리 전용)
- 방법: 18개 분석가(코어 7 · sbomplus 6 · 테스트/CI/패키징 1 · 문서/데이터/증거 1 · 교차 관점 3[정직성 계약, 신뢰할 수 없는 입력 보안, 아키텍처])가 각자 담당 파일을 전부 읽고 결함을 보고했습니다. 중간 이상 결함마다 검증자 2명(코드 추적 / 실제 실행 재현)이 반박을 시도했고, 마지막에 누락 점검 에이전트가 빈틈을 확인했습니다. 총 239개 에이전트가 읽기 전용으로 돌았습니다.
- 결과: 결함 110건 검증 대상 중 105건 확인, 3건 반박, 2건 판정 갈림. 별도로 low/info 31건은 검증하지 않았습니다. 프로젝트 자체 주장 130개를 코드와 대조해 61개 성립 / 34개 부분 성립 / 34개 불성립 / 2개 확인 불가였습니다.
- 이 보고서는 코드를 수정하지 않았습니다. 본문의 경로는 분석 시점 기준 `sbomplus/...`이고, 작업 트리에서는 `bts_sbom_app/...`로 이름이 바뀌는 중입니다(아래 "분석 시 주의사항" 참고).

> 편집 메모(0.2.0 릴리스 세션): 본문 인용 중 옛 제품 표기는 "BTS-SBOM 앱 계층"으로 바꿨습니다. 코드 경로는 분석 시점 그대로(`sbomplus/...`)이며 현재는 `bts_sbom_app/...`입니다. 내용·판정은 수정하지 않았습니다.

## 1. 프로젝트 개요

| 구성 | 내용 |
|---|---|
| `bts_sbom/` (코어, v0.1.1) | SPDX 2.3 / CycloneDX 생성, OSV 취약점 스캔(온라인 + 오프라인 sqlite), OpenVEX 생성·소비, C/C++ 함수 수준 도달가능성, 타이포스쿼팅·의존성 혼동 탐지, 6개 규제 섹터 리포트(CRA · FDA 524B · UNECE R155/R156 · EO 14028 · 금융 OSS 가이드 · 로봇), 다수의 매니페스트/락파일 파서, 라이선스 도구 |
| `sbomplus/` (상위 계층) | 작업 복사본 격리, SBOM/VEX 생성, 라이선스 해소(출처 기록), OSV 발견 병합, 수정 계획, 승인형 패치 + 재스캔 + 되돌리기, 레거시 아키텍처 분석, EXAONE 제안(4단계 검증 + 결정적 폴백), 근거 기반 RAG 챗봇, 배치/루프 하네스, 인증 없는 HTTP 서버 |
| 그 외 | `tests/`, `docs/`, `specs/`(EARS 인수 기준), `knowledge/`(RAG 코퍼스), `evals/`, `evidence/`(배치/루프 실행 기록), `tutorials/`, `examples/`, CI 워크플로 2개, Dockerfile |

핵심 철학은 **정직성 계약**입니다: 근거가 없으면 통과가 아니다(`not_evidence`), "데이터 없음"은 "취약점 없음"이 아니다, VEX는 fail-closed, 도달가능성 UNKNOWN은 안전으로 취급하지 않는다, 분석 대상 저장소는 읽기만 하고 실행하지 않는다, 외부로 나가는 동작은 각각 명시 플래그가 필요하다.

**종합 평가**: 계약은 설계 의도에서 진짜입니다(판정 어휘, 공개 필드, fail-closed VEX, 오프라인 DB 신선도 처리 등이 여러 서브시스템에 일관되게 들어 있음). 테스트와 문서화 수준도 이 규모의 프로젝트치고 높습니다. 그러나 이 계약이 실제 코드에서 깨지는 경로가 여러 곳 확인됐고, 그중 일부는 CI 게이트가 통과해서는 안 될 때 통과하게 만듭니다.

## 2. 테스트 실측 (이 세션에서 직접 실행)

- `PYTHONUTF8=1`: 97개 전부 통과 (이후 작업 트리에서 107개 수집)
- 기본 cp949 콘솔: 95 통과 / 2 실패 (`test_cli_help`, `test_cli_sector_all_six_sectors`). em dash(U+2014) 인코딩 오류이며 `docs/CORE_ISSUES.md` #4에 이미 기록된 결함입니다. `ECU_Sentinel` 쪽 복사본에는 수정이 있으나 이 저장소로 포팅되지 않았습니다.
- 확인된 테스트 공백: `reachability.py`·`supply_chain.py`는 import 가능 여부만 검사, `batch.py`·`looplog.py`·`report.py`는 테스트 0.

## 3. 정직성 계약을 깨는 결함 (우선순위 높음)

1. **false-clean 스캔** — `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:753,774`: purl 없는 SBOM 컴포넌트가 OSV 생태계 `OSS-Fuzz`로 간주됩니다. OSV가 200/빈 응답을 주면 `data_status=complete`, `vulnerability_presence=none`, `gate_exit_code==0`, "all components authoritatively queried" 메시지가 나옵니다. 같은 파일 1176행 근처 주석이 이 응답이 무의미하다고 스스로 적고 있습니다.
2. **VEX 버전 붕괴** — `bts_sbom/vex.py:394-406`: 문장이 특정 버전 제품(`requests@9.9.9`)에 걸려 있어도 색인이 버전 제거 키로도 저장되고 조회도 버전 제거 키로 하므로, 아직 취약한 `requests@1.0.0` 발견이 `not_affected`로 suppress됩니다.
3. **CycloneDX VEX 검증 우회** — `bts_sbom/vex.py:514-577`: `load_vex_file()`이 CycloneDX 경로에서 `_validate_statement()`를 부르지 않아, 근거 없는 `not_affected`가 그대로 통과합니다(`bts-sbom vex --check`도 동일). `sbomplus/sectors.py:76`의 선박 프로파일 MAR-05도 근거 없는 `not_affected`로 critical/high를 닫고 `pass`를 냅니다.
4. **GIT 커밋 SHA가 "수정 버전"으로 채택** — `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py:424`, `remediation.py:62,95`, `osv_local_db.py:153`: range type을 거르지 않습니다. `docs/CORE_ISSUES.md` #1은 "v0.1.1에서 재확인 안 함"이라 적었는데 여전히 존재합니다. 오프라인 DB 쪽은 해시 문자열의 앞 숫자 때문에 "영향 없음"으로 잘못 판정되기도 합니다.
5. **EPSS/KEV 피드 장애가 측정값으로 위장** — `bts_sbom/_internal/epss_kev.py:93`: 장애 시 `{epss:0.0, kev:False}`가 `live-api`로 표기되고 오프라인 큐레이션 테이블로 폴백하지 않습니다. 확인 시 Log4Shell(CVE-2021-44228)이 epss 0.0 / KEV 아님으로 나왔습니다.
6. **루프 게이트가 탐지 붕괴를 통과시킴** — `sbomplus/loop.py:43-63`: 컴포넌트/취약점 수가 게이트 지표에 없고, 분모 0일 때 비율이 `None`이면 비교에서 빠집니다. 컴포넌트·취약점이 모두 0이 된 후보도 `pass: True`였습니다. 후보에 지표가 아예 없는 경우도 "회귀 없음"으로 처리됩니다(`loop.py:49`).
7. **섹터 NTIA 체크** — `bts_sbom/sectors.py:211`: 7개 요소 중 3개만 PASS(점수 76, B등급)여도 `fail_count==0`이면 `pass`로 판정합니다. 요구 제목은 "NTIA 최소 요소 충족"입니다.
8. **reachability 결과 덮어쓰기** — `bts_sbom/reachability.py:591`: 결과를 `cve_id`만 키로 저장해, 같은 CVE가 걸린 서로 다른 두 패키지의 판정이 서로를 덮어씁니다. 증명된 REACHABLE이 UNKNOWN으로 바뀝니다.

## 4. 보안: 신뢰할 수 없는 입력과 "원본은 바뀌지 않는다"

- **심볼릭 링크** — `sbomplus/workspace.py:121`: 로컬 폴더 소스는 `copytree(..., symlinks=True)`로 복사되어 실제 심볼릭 링크가 작업 복사본에 남습니다. 모듈 docstring("symlinks are not materialised")은 git clone 경로(`core.symlinks=false`)에서만 성립합니다. 링크된 외부 파일 내용 읽기와, `patch.apply()`를 통한 외부 파일 쓰기가 끝까지 재현됐습니다.
- **경로 검증 없음** — `sbomplus/patch.py:332`: `plan['files']`를 `os.path.join(root, rel)`로 그대로 씁니다. `../secret/requirements.txt` 같은 항목이 작업 복사본 밖의 파일을 덮어쓰는 것이 재현됐습니다. 이 쓰기는 `deliver.py`의 `--allow-origin-write` / `--allow-push` 게이트보다 먼저 일어나므로 "명시 플래그 없이는 원본 불변" 주장이 이 경로에서는 성립하지 않습니다. 또 `deliver.py`의 `inplace`는 `git apply --unsafe-paths`를 사용합니다.
- **승인 다이제스트 재사용** — `sbomplus/patch.py:301`: 해시가 (생태계, 컴포넌트, 현재 버전, 안전 버전)만 포함하고 대상 파일·검증 상태는 빠져 있습니다.
- **HTTP 서버** — `sbomplus/server.py`: Origin/Referer 검사가 없고 Content-Type을 무시해, `text/plain` 폼으로 다른 웹사이트가 로컬 서버에 요청을 보낼 수 있습니다(CSRF). 음수 `Content-Length`는 크기 제한을 무력화하고 워커 스레드를 멈추게 합니다.
- **커밋된 HEAD 서버의 승인 우회** — HEAD(`96f89c2`)의 `server.py`는 `approved:true`만으로 토큰 없이 패치를 적용합니다. 작업 트리의 미커밋 수정본은 이미 `approval_token`을 요구하도록 고쳐져 있어(검증자는 이 수정본을 읽고 "반박"했음), HEAD 기준으로만 실재하는 결함입니다.
- **챗봇 grounding** — `sbomplus/chat.py:30`: 인용문이 문서에 그대로 있는지만 검증하고 문서 내용의 사실성은 보지 않습니다. 의존성 키에 심은 문장이 "verified" 주장으로 통과합니다. 검증자 의견이 갈렸습니다(영향 범위 제한적).
- **chat 프로젝트 지정 오류** — `chat.py:141`: 존재하지 않는 프로젝트 id를 주면, 로드된 프로젝트가 하나일 때 그 프로젝트의 답을 확신 있게 돌려줍니다.
- 반박된 항목: `ssh://-oProxyCommand=...` 형태의 URL 주입은 `normalize_source()`가 걸러내지 않지만 git 자체가 `strange hostname ... blocked`로 거부해 실제 악용 불가(2017년 CVE-2017-1000117 수정)로 확인됐습니다.

## 5. 정확성과 견고성

- **Python 3.10에서 조용한 빈 결과**: `tomllib`는 3.11부터 stdlib입니다. 3.10에서는 `extra.py:89`(Cargo.lock), `pip.py:50`(poetry.lock), `extra.py:411`(pyproject.toml) 파서가 `[]`을 반환하고 폴백이 없습니다(같은 형식을 `cargo.py`는 정규식 폴백으로 처리). 선언된 최소 버전이자 CI 매트릭스(3.10)입니다. 실제 3.10에서 실행한 것이 아니라 `tomllib=None`을 주입해 재현했습니다.
- **SPDX 괄호 버그**: `strip("()")`가 문서화된 `bts_sbom/sbom.py:58` 외에 `bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py:84`에도 있습니다(문서화 안 됨, BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 우회책도 없음). `sbomplus/sbom.py:163`의 우회책은 괄호 개수가 같아지는 `(A OR B) AND (C OR D)`에서는 실패합니다.
- **CVSS v4.0**: `osvscanner.py:496`이 매크로벡터 최댓값 조회만 하고 심각도 거리 보간을 구현하지 않아, `VA:H` / `VA:L` / `VA:N`이 모두 9.8입니다. v3과 v4 중 큰 값을 쓰므로 부풀려진 점수가 심각도 집계로 전파됩니다.
- **파서**: requirements.txt는 extras(`pkg[x]==1`), `===`, `-r` 포함을 누락합니다. 깊게 중첩된 JSON은 `RecursionError`가 삼켜져 파일 전체가 빈 결과가 됩니다. 문법은 맞지만 구조가 틀린 lockfile 하나가 스캔 전체를 예외로 중단시킵니다(`osvscanner.py:885`). 디렉터리 제외가 부분 문자열 매칭이라 `vendors/`, `node_modules_old/`, `.github`도 건너뜁니다(`osvscanner.py:971`). Yarn Berry 락파일은 가짜 패키지를 만들어 냅니다.
- **버전 비교**: `sbomplus/versions.py`가 단일 문자 `a/b/m`을 사전릴리스로 취급합니다(`1.0.2a < 1.0.2`). 코어는 같은 버그를 이미 고쳤습니다. `fix_planner._highest()`는 Debian/RPM epoch를 무시합니다. `version_match`는 npm/cargo/go의 `==` 제약이 정확히 일치해도 항상 fail-closed입니다.
- **VEX**: 허용 목록에 없는 id(`MAL-…`, `DSA-…`)가 문서 전체 생성/파싱을 실패시킵니다(`vex.py:68`).
- **라이선스**: `Simplified BSD License`가 weak-copyleft로 분류됩니다(부분 문자열 `mpl`). `CC-BY-SA-4.0`은 두 모듈에서 서로 다르게 분류되고, `GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0`는 `license_categories`와 `license_utils`가 상반된 결론을 냅니다. `build_compat_matrix()`는 SSPL / CC-BY-NC 비호환을 영원히 못 잡습니다(현재 호출처는 없음). 라이선스 파일 스캐너는 UTF-16 LICENSE를 바이너리로 보고 버립니다.
- **도달가능성**: `#ifdef`로 비활성화된 코드, 전역 함수명과 같은 매개변수명 때문에 false REACHABLE이 납니다(과대보고 방향이라 보수적). 가상 호출·points-to는 도달가능성 판정 경로에서 사용되지 않습니다. `callgraph.py:544`는 템플릿이 없어도 호출 지점마다 전체 함수 집합을 훑어 함수 6천 개 입력에서 약 12초가 걸립니다.
- **패치**: 여러 매니페스트에 같은 핀이 있어도 첫 번째만 고치고 전체를 `applied`로 보고합니다(`patch.py:346`). 환경 마커가 붙은 서로 다른 핀을 모두 같은 버전으로 덮어씁니다(`patch.py:39`). UTF-8 BOM이 있는 파일의 첫 요구사항은 패치되지 않습니다. 롤백 실패 여부는 확인하지 않고 `regression_reverted`로 보고합니다(`pipeline.py:181`). 후반 라운드 예외가 앞 라운드의 이미 커밋된 결과를 `{}`로 지웁니다(`pipeline.py:113`).
- **아키텍처 분석**: git rename(`{a => b}/f.py`)과 비ASCII 파일명(한글)이 hotspot/coupling에서 조용히 빠집니다. `max_commits`로 이력이 잘려도 알리지 않습니다. Rust는 `mod`만 엣지로 만들어 `use` 순환을 못 잡는데 고지가 없습니다. 재귀 `depth()`는 긴 의존 체인에서 한계를 넘습니다.
- **CLI/파이프라인**: 잘못된 `sources_json` 경로가 "0개 프로젝트, 성공(종료 0)"이 됩니다. `batch`는 프로젝트 실패와 무관하게 항상 종료 0입니다. 단일 프로젝트 명령은 실행마다 정리되지 않는 임시 작업 폴더를 남깁니다.
- **RAG**: 2글자 한글 토큰의 빈도가 이중 집계되어 BM25가 부풀려집니다(`rag.py:58`). 임계값 11.0과 recall 수치는 이 토크나이저 위에서 측정된 값입니다.
- **라이선스 SBOM 쓰기**: `sbomplus/sbom.py:24`에서 purl이 없는 서로 다른 생태계의 같은 이름·버전 컴포넌트가 하나의 bom-ref로 합쳐집니다. `validate_cyclonedx()`는 `expression` 문자열을 파싱하지 않아 임의 문자열도 "valid"로 판정합니다.

## 6. 문서 주장과 현실 (불성립/부분 성립 주요 항목)

- README "sectors.py 42 tests": 코어 전체 테스트가 28개이고 섹터를 직접 다루는 것은 3~4개입니다.
- README `selfsbom` 예시 출력은 0.1.0, 실제는 0.1.1입니다. `bts_sbom/pyproject.toml`도 0.1.0으로 남아 있습니다. 루트 `CHANGELOG.md`에는 0.1.1 항목이 없습니다.
- `docs/SBOMPLUS.md`는 "코어 결함 3건"이라 쓰지만 `CORE_ISSUES.md`에는 4건이 있습니다. 또한 #2 결함은 문서에 적힌 위치 외에 `license_norm.py`에도 있어 "우회로 해결" 서술이 부분적입니다.
- `sbomplus/engine.py` docstring이 `tests/test_engine_contract.py`를 언급하지만 그런 파일은 없습니다. `osvscanner.py` docstring의 `_to_native`, `release_gate`도 코드에 없습니다. `_internal/spdx_sbom.py` docstring의 "benchmark/spdx_sbom에서 공식 파서로 검증"에 해당하는 디렉터리도 없고, 이 빌더의 출력은 저장소 어디에서도 검증되지 않습니다.
- `sbomplus/net.py`의 "모든 외부 호출은 여기를 거친다"는 `llm.py`가 독자 클라이언트를 쓰므로 불성립입니다. `llm.py:124`는 응답 본문을 키 마스킹 없이 `self.errors`에 저장합니다(현재 읽는 코드는 없음).
- `CONTRIBUTING.md`의 "ruff-clean": `bts_sbom/`와 `tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py`는 통과하지만 `sbomplus/`, `tests/sbomplus/`는 30건이 나오고 CI가 이 트리를 검사하지 않습니다.
- 루트 `SECURITY.md`는 `bts_sbom`만 범위로 적고 `sbomplus`의 공격 표면(서버, 외부 clone, EXAONE 키)을 다루지 않습니다.
- `pyproject.toml`: `knowledge/`와 `evals/`가 wheel에 포함되지 않습니다. 실제로 wheel을 빌드해 확인했고, 설치본에서는 `chat`/`serve`/`evalrag`가 오류 없이 빈 RAG 인덱스로 동작합니다.
- `sbomplus/arch.py` docstring의 "실행할 수 없는 분석은 모두 notes에 기록": Kotlin 엣지는 계산되는데 "분석 안 됨"으로 표기되고, Rust·C의 한계는 고지되지 않습니다.

**성립이 확인된 주요 주장** (이 보고서가 비판만 하는 것이 아니라는 점을 분명히 하기 위해)
- README/`SBOMPLUS.md`의 e2e 수치(2,478 컴포넌트, 1,292 취약점과 심각도 분포, 라이선스 해소율 0.990, 수정 계획 339/348, 재스캔 확인 515 / 추정 705, EXAONE 채택 57/5 = 0.919)는 `evidence/batch/latest.json`(= 최종 패스 `p15_final_verify`의 `summary.json`)과 정확히 일치합니다. 개별 프로젝트 JSON에서 처음부터 다시 합산한 것은 아닙니다.
- RAG 수치(골든 Recall@5 1.0, test 셋 recall 0.917 / 응답률 0.833)는 `evidence/loop/rag_history.json`과 일치합니다. 다만 5절의 한글 토큰 이중 집계 위에서 측정된 값입니다.
- `sbomplus/engine.py`가 코어를 import하는 유일한 지점이라는 주장은 성립합니다(`import bts_sbom` 구문은 `engine.py:36` 한 곳).
- EARS 인수 기준 68개는 `speclint`가 양방향 추적을 통과합니다(테스트 없는 AC 없음, 알 수 없는 AC를 가리키는 테스트 없음).
- 매니페스트가 없는 `iso14229`는 `empty_project` / `unknown`으로 보고되고 "취약점 없음"으로 보고되지 않습니다.
- `docs/CORE_ISSUES.md` #2의 `strip("()")` 서술과 sbomplus 테스트 수(HEAD 기준 69개)는 정확합니다.

## 7. 구조 평가

**강점**
- 재현 가능한 자동 테스트와, 증거(측정 퍼센트·건수)를 행마다 싣는 판정 구조.
- 코어는 `dependencies = []`가 실제로 유지됩니다.
- 비교적 안전한 clone 경로(훅 비활성, 서브모듈 미취득, `ext::` 거부, `core.symlinks=false`)와 읽기 전용 설계.
- 발견 결함과 우회책을 `CORE_ISSUES.md`에 정직하게 기록하는 습관, EARS 인수 기준과 테스트 연결(`speclint`).

**약점**
- 버전 비교, CVSS, 라이선스 분류가 코어 안팎에 2~3벌 복제되어 이미 값이 어긋났습니다(OSL-3.0, CC-BY-SA-4.0, 단일 문자 사전릴리스 등). 서로를 교차 검증하는 테스트가 없습니다.
- 구조가 다른 SPDX 빌더가 둘(`bts_sbom/sbom.py`의 다중 루트, `_internal/spdx_sbom.py`의 단일 루트)이고, NTIA의 "described product" 제외 휴리스틱은 단일 루트 가정이라 다중 루트 문서에서 비-리프 패키지를 분모에서 빼 버립니다(`ntia_check.py:165`).
- `sbom_from_resolver_graph`는 이름만으로 중복 제거해 다른 버전을 버리고 엉뚱한 버전으로 의존 엣지를 잇습니다(`sbom.py:195`).
- 패키징이 둘로 나뉘고(루트 / `bts_sbom/pyproject.toml`) 버전이 어긋나 있으며 데이터 디렉터리가 배포에 포함되지 않습니다.
- 일부 함수 복잡도가 매우 높습니다(예: `engine.normalize()` 순환 복잡도 약 80).
- 전역 상태: `net.STATS`는 프로젝트별로 초기화되지 않아 서버에서 누적/교차 오염됩니다. `arch`의 EOL 캐시는 한 번의 실패를 프로세스 끝까지 저장합니다.

## 8. 권장 수정 순서

1. 코어의 정직성 계약 위반 4건: `OSS-Fuzz` 기본값, VexIndex 버전 붕괴, CycloneDX VEX 검증, GIT range 필터링(+ 각각 회귀 테스트).
2. `workspace.py` 심볼릭 링크 처리와 `patch.apply()` 경로 포함 검사, 승인 다이제스트에 `files`·`verification` 포함.
3. Python 3.10: `tomllib` 폴백 추가하거나 최소 버전을 3.11로 올림. 파서 실패를 빈 결과가 아닌 명시적 `parse_failed`로 노출.
4. `strip("()")` 두 곳 수정 후 `sbomplus`의 우회책과 테스트 삭제.
5. `loop.gate()`: 컴포넌트/취약점 수와 "지표 누락 = 판정 불가" 규칙 추가. EPSS/KEV 장애 구분.
6. 문서·패키징 정합: 버전, CHANGELOG, 테스트 수, `SECURITY.md` 범위, wheel 데이터 포함 여부, `sbomplus` ruff를 CI에 포함.

## 9. 분석 시 주의사항과 한계

- **분석 도중 작업 트리가 바뀌었습니다.** 이 분석이 한 일이 아닙니다. 브랜치가 `feat/sbomplus-integration`에서 `main`으로 이동(fast-forward 병합)했고, `sbomplus/` → `bts_sbom_app/` 이름 변경이 스테이징되었으며, `console.py`와 `web/`(미추적)이 추가되고 `server.py`가 수정되었습니다(변경 경로 124개). 별도 worktree `_merge_bts_sbom_012`, `_rel_bts_sbom_012`(release/v0.2.0)도 있습니다. 본문의 `sbomplus/` 경로는 `bts_sbom_app/`로, 수정된 파일의 줄 번호는 달라졌을 수 있습니다.
- 저장소 루트에 빈 `__verify_tmp_out__/work/` 폴더가 분석 시간대에 생겼습니다. 어느 작업이 만들었는지 확인하지 못했고 삭제하지 않았습니다.
- 검증자가 틀린 경우가 있습니다. "cp949 콘솔에서 `sector` 출력이 크래시한다"는 반박됐지만 이 세션의 테스트 실행에서 재현됐습니다(위 2절).
- 검증자 2건(`patch.py:332` 추적, `workspace.py:43` 추적)은 안전 점검이 "미검토"로 표시했습니다. 내용에 인젝션 징후는 없었고 같은 결함을 다른 검증자가 실행해 확인했습니다.
- Python 3.10 결함은 모듈의 `tomllib`를 `None`으로 바꿔 재현한 것이며 실제 3.10 실행이 아닙니다.
- 직접 코드로 다시 확인한 항목: `strip("()")` 두 곳, `OSS-Fuzz` 기본값, `symlinks=True`, VexIndex 키 구조, `patch.py`의 경로 검증 부재, HEAD 서버의 토큰 처리.
- 누락 점검 에이전트의 지적: `LICENSE`, `bts_sbom/py.typed`, `summary.html`은 어느 분석가도 보지 않았고, `Dockerfile`의 보안 자세(베이스 이미지 고정, root 실행)는 평가되지 않았습니다. 같은 에이전트는 "e2e 수치를 원자료와 대조한 분석가가 없다"고 했으나, 문서/데이터 분석가가 `latest.json`·`summary.json`과의 일치를 확인했습니다(6절). 두 에이전트의 서술이 엇갈린 부분이고, 이 보고서는 분석가의 확인 기록을 따랐습니다.
- 테스트 수는 시점에 따라 다릅니다. 분석가는 HEAD 기준 sbomplus 69개 / 코어 28개를 확인했고, 작업 트리에는 새 테스트(`test_console_ui.py` 등)가 들어와 지금은 총 107개가 수집됩니다(분석 중 한 에이전트가 센 값은 106개). `_internal` 모듈 29개 중 4개(`pathutil.py`, `policy.py`, `remediation.py`, `version_match.py`)는 "각 파일 헤더가 출처를 적는다"는 `_internal/__init__.py`의 주장과 달리 해당 헤더가 없습니다. 두 `LICENSE` 파일은 바이트 단위로 같습니다.
- 분석 비용: 에이전트 239개, 도구 호출 약 3,700회, 약 61분.

---

# 부록 (자동 생성)

아래 부록은 워크플로 결과 JSON에서 생성했습니다. `상태`는 검증 결과(confirmed / refuted / disputed), `심각도`는 확인한 검증자의 최고 심각도(없으면 분석가 주장), `실행`은 확인한 검증자 중 코드를 실제로 실행한 수입니다.

| critical | confirmed | 2 | core-manifests | `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:50` | poetry.lock parsing is completely silent-empty on Python 3.10 |
| critical | confirmed | 2 | core-sbom-sector | `bts_sbom/sbom.py:58` | _valid_expression's strip("()") corrupts nested SPDX license expressions |
| critical | confirmed | 2 | core-vex-supply | `bts_sbom/vex.py:394` | VexIndex suppression ignores the product version it claims to key on |
| critical | confirmed | 2 | core-vuln-data-version | `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py:424` | A GIT-range commit SHA can become OsvVuln.fixed_version / remediation's fixed_suggestion, a fabricated non-installable 'safe version' |
| critical | confirmed | 1 | lens-honesty | `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:753` | SBOM components with no purl default to the OSV catch-all ecosystem 'OSS-Fuzz'; a meaningless 200/empty OSV answer for that ecosystem is then treated as a fully authoritative 'complete' + 'none' scan, producing a false verified-clean pass and CI gate_exit_code()==0 |

| critical | confirmed | 2 | lens-honesty | `sbomplus/workspace.py:121` | Local-folder ingestion materialises real OS symlinks inside the 'isolated' work copy, enabling arbitrary file read and, via patch.apply(), arbitrary file write outside the work copy |

## 부록 B. 심각도 critical/high 결함 상세

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py:89` — Cargo.lock parsing is completely silent-empty on Python 3.10 (the project's declared and CI-tested minimum version)

- 분석 영역: core-manifests (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: parse_cargo_lock (registered for 'cargo.lock') returns [] unconditionally when tomllib is None, with no fallback, unlike the sibling cargo.py which has a working regex-based fallback for the identical format.
- 재현 조건: On Python 3.10 (no stdlib tomllib; requires-python='>=3.10' and CI runs python-version 3.10 per .github/workflows/ci.yml:41), extra.parse_cargo_lock('[[package]]\nname="serde"\nversion="1.0.193"\n...') returns [] while cargo.parse_cargo_lock on the same text correctly returns the real package. The repo's own tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py::test_cargo_lock_direct_transitive_from_sibling_manifest (lines 575-597) would fail on the 3.10 CI legs as a result.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): extra.py:89-90 `if tomllib is None: return []` with no fallback — confirmed by reading and by execution. Simulated tomllib=None (Python 3.10 behavior): extra.parse_cargo_lock(text) -> [] while cargo.py's sibling parse_cargo_lock(text) -> real package via its regex fallback (cargo.py:51 `_cargo_lock_regex`). manifests/__init__.py:42 registers `'cargo.lock': extra.parse_cargo_lock`, and parse_file (lines 89-102) has no retry/fallback, so extra.py's version is what the pipeline actually calls. CI matrix (.github/workflows/ci.yml:41) runs python-version ["3.10","3.12"], and pyproject.toml:10 requires-python='>=3.10'; stdlib tomllib doesn't exist on 3.10. tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py:575 test_cargo_lock_direct_transitive_from_sibling_manifest asserts real parsed package data, which would fail (empty result) on the 3.10 CI legs. No guard/try-except/validator rescues this path.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified by reading and running code (no modifications to the repo; repro script lives in temp dir, no network, no third-party code executed). bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py:89-90 is `if tomllib is None: return []` with no fallback, unlike the sibling bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/cargo.py:43-51 which falls back to `_cargo_lock_regex`. Registry (manifests/__init__.py:42) maps 'cargo.lock' -> extra.parse_cargo_lock. pyproject.toml:10 declares requires-python='>=3.10'; .github/workflows/ci.yml:41 runs python-version ["3.10","3.12"]. On Python 3.10, tomllib doesn't exist in stdlib, so this path is live, not defensive/unreachable code. I simulated this by monkeypatching extra.tomllib=None (mirrors the real except-ModuleNotFoundError branch) on a live 3.12 interpreter and ran both functions directly: extra.parse_cargo_lock returned [] while cargo.parse_cargo_lock's regex fallback correctly returned the serde package. I also ran the exact scenario of tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py::test_cargo_lock_direct_transitive_from_sibling_manifest (lines 575-597) through manifests.parse_file with tomllib forced None: it returned [] instead of {"serde":"direct","rand":"transitive"}, reproducing a real test failure. All claims in the defect check out exactly.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:50` — poetry.lock parsing is completely silent-empty on Python 3.10

- 분석 영역: core-manifests (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: parse_poetry_lock returns [] unconditionally when tomllib is None, with no fallback; poetry.lock is documented as the source of 'exact versions for the WHOLE tree'.
- 재현 조건: Monkeypatching pip.tomllib=None (reproducing the exact branch real Python 3.10 takes) makes parse_poetry_lock('[[package]]\nname="requests"\nversion="2.31.0"\ngroups=["main"]\n') return [] instead of the real package; no error or note is produced anywhere.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:40-55: `parse_poetry_lock` does `if tomllib is None: return []` at line 50, no fallback, no logging. `tomllib` is set at line 9-11 via `try: import tomllib / except ModuleNotFoundError: tomllib = None` — real on Py3.10 (tomllib added 3.11). pyproject.toml requires-python ">=3.10" and CI matrix (.github/workflows/ci.yml, sbomplus_ci.yml) tests "3.10" — so this path is live on a supported, CI-tested interpreter. Checked caller chain: manifests/__init__.py routes "poetry.lock" directly to this function via `fn(text, path=path)` with no empty-result check/warning. Grepped tests/ — zero references to "poetry" or "tomllib"; no test exercises this branch. Executed the exact repro (monkeypatched `pipmod.tomllib=None`, called `parse_poetry_lock` on the given poetry.lock snippet) — confirmed it returns `[]`, silently dropping the "requests" package, no exception/log. Note cargo.py has an explicit regex fallback for the identical tomllib-None case, showing this silent-empty is an inconsistency/oversight, not an intended design. Partial mitigation exists only via pyproject.toml direct-dep parsing (inexact versions, no transitive tree), so not literally total blindness — hence "high" rather than "critical".
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified by reading bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:50-51 and by execution. Monkeypatching pip.tomllib=None (the real state on Python 3.10, where tomllib doesn't exist) and calling parse_poetry_lock('[[package]]\nname="requests"\nversion="2.31.0"\ngroups=["main"]\n') returned [] instead of the requests package (confirmed with normal tomllib present it correctly returns the DetectedPackage). No fallback parser (e.g. tomli backport), no logging, no exception/warning anywhere in pip.py or the dispatcher __init__.py. pyproject.toml ">=3.10" and CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml, sbomplus_ci.yml) explicitly test python-version 3.10, yet no test exercises poetry.lock content at all (tests dir has zero "poetry" hits), so this silent regression is untested on a supported, CI-matrixed interpreter.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/sbom.py:58` — _valid_expression's strip("()") corrupts nested SPDX license expressions

- 분석 영역: core-sbom-sector (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: expression_string() always wraps every And/Or node in its own parentheses; _valid_expression then calls .strip("()") on the whole string, which removes ALL leading/trailing '(' and ')' characters, not one matched outer pair. Any license expression that needs a nested group (mixing AND and OR, which requires parentheses by the SPDX grammar) gets one real closing or opening paren stripped off, producing an unbalanced, invalid string.
- 재현 조건: build_spdx_document(packages=[{'license': 'MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause)', ...}], []) stores licenseConcluded='MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause' (missing the closing paren) in the output document; calling validate_spdx() on that same just-generated document then flags it as invalid ('package X has a non-SPDX license value'), i.e. bts-sbom's own CLI (`bts-sbom sbom ...`) would abort with exit code 2 on a perfectly valid compound license.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Read bts_sbom/sbom.py:58 `expression_string(node).strip("()")` and _internal/license_spdx.py's expression_string (wraps every And/Or in one paren pair, line 272-275). For nested And(Or(...)), result is "(MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause))"; str.strip("()") removes ALL outer-set chars from each end (not one matched pair), stripping the leading "(" but TWO trailing ")" chars, leaving unbalanced "MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause". Executed build_spdx_document + validate_spdx directly: licenseConcluded became exactly that corrupted string, and validate_spdx (sbom.py:274-279) re-parsed it, failed, and emitted "package pkg1 has a non-SPDX license value" - reproducing the claimed CLI abort (cli.py:118-121, exit code 2) verbatim. No guard/try-except/default intervenes; _valid_expression is used both for writing and validating, so the bug is both real and self-inflicted exactly as described.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified bts_sbom/sbom.py:58: `expression_string(node).strip("()")`. license_spdx.py:272-275 wraps every And/Or node in its own parens, so a mixed expression renders as e.g. '(MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause))'. `.strip("()")` removes all leading/trailing paren chars, not one matched pair, yielding 'MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause' - unbalanced. Reproduced live: build_spdx_document with license 'MIT AND (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause)' stores exactly that truncated licenseConcluded, and validate_spdx() on the same doc then emits "package pkgX has a non-SPDX license value" - matching the trigger exactly. Any AND+OR mix (SPDX-required grouping) hits this; CLI would exit 2 on valid input.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/vex.py:394` — VexIndex suppression ignores the product version it claims to key on

- 분석 영역: core-vex-supply (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: VexIndex.__init__ indexes every statement under BOTH its literal product id and the version-stripped id (lines 394-395); status_for() then also tries both the literal and the version-stripped query product (line 406). The combination means a not_affected/fixed statement scoped to one specific package version matches a finding for ANY other version of that package, because the index collapses 'statement has version X' to the same key as 'statement has no version' whenever the query side is stripped.
- 재현 조건: An OpenVEX not_affected statement for pkg:pypi/requests@9.9.9 (CVE-2024-9999, justification vulnerable_code_not_present) causes VexIndex.status_for('CVE-2024-9999','pkg:pypi/requests@1.0.0') to return ('not_affected', 'vulnerable_code_not_present'), and vex.apply_to_findings() suppresses the finding for the actually-installed, still-vulnerable v1.0.0 -- confirmed by direct execution (python -c reproduction via bts_sbom.vex).
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified by direct execution. VexIndex.__init__ (vex.py:390-395) unconditionally stores each statement under both its literal product id and `_strip_version(pid)`, regardless of whether the statement actually had a version. status_for (vex.py:404-409) queries both the literal and `_strip_version(product)` keys. Built a not_affected statement for pkg:pypi/requests@9.9.9 (CVE-2024-9999) and queried status_for for @1.0.0 and @2.0.0: both returned ('not_affected','vulnerable_code_not_present'), and apply_to_findings suppressed a finding for requests@1.0.0. No guard, version-equality check, or try/except anywhere in __init__/status_for/apply_to_findings prevents this; policy.py's vex_evaluate caller documents version-stripped matching as intended but only for findings lacking version data, not as license to ignore a statement's own pinned version. This is a real VEX-suppression bypass: a not_affected/fixed claim for one version silently suppresses findings on any other version of that package.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Reproduced directly. bts_sbom/vex.py:393-395 indexes each statement under both (token, exact_pid) and (token, _strip_version(pid)). status_for (line 406) queries both (token, product) and (token, _strip_version(product)). For a not_affected statement on pkg:pypi/requests@9.9.9 (CVE-2024-9999), ran VexIndex.status_for('CVE-2024-9999','pkg:pypi/requests@1.0.0') -> ('not_affected','vulnerable_code_not_present'), and apply_to_findings() suppressed that finding, matching the reported trigger exactly. No version-equality guard exists anywhere in vex.py or the only other caller (bts_sbom/_internal/policy.py:453); the stripped-key collision is unconditional. This is a real false-negative: a VEX statement for one version silently suppresses detections for any other installed version of the same package.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py:424` — A GIT-range commit SHA can become OsvVuln.fixed_version / remediation's fixed_suggestion, a fabricated non-installable 'safe version'

- 분석 영역: core-vuln-data-version (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: osv_client.py's _parse_vulns() (loop at line 424, 'for range_item in affected.get("ranges", []) or []') and remediation.py's iter_fix_bounds()/suggest_fixes() (bts_sbom/_internal/remediation.py lines 62 and 95, same unfiltered 'for ri, rng in enumerate(aff.get("ranges", []) or [])') both collect every range's 'fixed' event with no check of rng/range_item.get('type'), so a GIT range's commit-hash 'fixed' value is treated exactly like an ECOSYSTEM range's semver 'fixed' value.
- 재현 조건: A record with only {"type":"GIT","events":[{"introduced":"0"},{"fixed":"9abcdef..."}]} under affected[].package={name:'left-pad',ecosystem:'npm'}: OsvClient()._parse_vulns(...) sets fixed_version to the literal SHA string, and remediation.suggest_fixes(vuln,'npm',current='1.0.0') returns fixed_suggestion == the SHA with constraint_source citing it as the real fix. Any downstream consumer (fix_planner.emit_requirements_patch, or a manifest bump) that treats fixed_suggestion/fixed_version as an installable version would pin a package to a git commit hash. Confirmed by executing both functions against a constructed OSV-shaped record; corroborated by sbomplus/engine.py's fixed_versions(), which explicitly documents 'GIT ranges (commit SHAs) are ignored' and whose own test fixtures (tests/sbomplus/conftest.py) deliberately attach a decoy GIT range+SHA to every advisory -- proof the project is aware this must be filtered, yet the core slice doesn't.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified by reading osv_client.py:424 and remediation.py:62/95 — neither loop checks range_item.get("type"), and "GIT" never appears in osv_client.py. Executed both against a GIT-only record {type:GIT, fixed: SHA}: OsvVuln.fixed_version == SHA, and suggest_fixes returned fixed_suggestion == SHA with constraint_source citing it. Worse: with a realistic mixed ECOSYSTEM+GIT record (the exact shape used in tests/sbomplus/conftest.py's decoy fixture), osv_client.py's fixed_version got overwritten to the SHA (last-match-wins, not even smallest-wins), discarding the correct "1.2.8". docs/CORE_ISSUES.md §1 documents this exact class of bug in v0.1.0 and claims remediation.py "addresses" it, but it doesn't for GIT-only or GIT-last records. fix_planner.py's _highest()/emit_requirements_patch would readily consume such a value into a manifest diff.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing. osv_client.py:424's range loop and remediation.py:62/95 loops collect every range's "fixed" event with no check of rng.get("type"), unlike sbomplus/engine.py:88 which explicitly does `if rg.get("type") not in ("ECOSYSTEM","SEMVER"): continue`. I ran the exact trigger (GIT-only range, fixed="9abcdef...", pkg left-pad/npm) via `python -I` against the real repo code (sys.path.insert, no network): OsvClient()._parse_vulns(...) set fixed_version to the literal SHA; remediation.suggest_fixes(vuln,"npm",current="1.0.0") returned fixed_suggestion == the SHA with constraint_source citing it as the fix. Traced reachability: cli.py:143-157 calls remediation_for_result by default (bts-sbom scan, unless --no-remediation) on osvscanner.py's raw OSV JSON (`entry["vulnerabilities"]=raw`, line 1208), so this is live, not dead code. fix_planner.py:61 (`getattr(v,"fixed_version")`) and emit_requirements_patch would propagate such a value if fed an OSV-derived fixed_version, though osv_client.py's own query_package/query_batch currently have no live caller elsewhere in the repo (library-only path) — remediation.py is the confirmed production-reachable one.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:753` — SBOM components with no purl default to the OSV catch-all ecosystem 'OSS-Fuzz'; a meaningless 200/empty OSV answer for that ecosystem is then treated as a fully authoritative 'complete' + 'none' scan, producing a false verified-clean pass and CI gate_exit_code()==0

- 분석 영역: lens-honesty (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: _parse_sbom (lines 753 and 774) sets `"ecosystem": eco or "OSS-Fuzz"` for any CycloneDX/SPDX component lacking a purl. The scan loop (lines 1176-1180) itself documents that an OSS-Fuzz 'answered' zero is MEANINGLESS ('OSV's catch-all that answers 200 with 0 vulns for arbitrary C/C++ names'), but only retries via the fw-distro fallback when the package carries osv_ecosystems candidates; otherwise the original 'queried_online' status is kept and flows unchanged into _completeness_block, which has no OSS-Fuzz-awareness at all.
- 재현 조건: Feed OsvScanner.scan() (offline=False) a CycloneDX/SPDX SBOM source containing one component with no purl, e.g. a generic/CMake/C library. Executed with a mocked OSV answer of (vulns=[], ok=True): the resulting query_status is 'queried_online' (an ANSWERED status), completeness.data_status=='complete', completeness.vulnerability_presence=='none', gate_exit_code(result)==0, OsvScanner._verified_clean(result) is True, and the table renderer prints 'No known vulnerabilities found (all components authoritatively queried).' This is directly reachable through discover()'s own SBOM auto-detection (`_is_sbom`) whenever a scanned directory tree contains a `*.cdx.json`/`*.spdx.json`/`bom.json` file, which sbomplus's engine.scan() also walks unchanged.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 아니오): Traced the full path. osvscanner.py:753/774 set ecosystem='OSS-Fuzz' when purl-less. normalize_ecosystem (line 114-120) leaves 'OSS-Fuzz' unchanged. In scan() (line 1167-1190), osv_eco='OSS-Fuzz' so _primary_meaningful=False (line 1179-1180), but the fw-distro retry only fires `if ... and p.get('osv_ecosystems')` (line 1181) - absent for a plain generic/CMake component, so raw=[], st='queried_online' (ANSWERED_STATUSES) stand unchanged; no 'scan_status' honesty flag is added (line 1216 guard skipped). _completeness_block (line 1257-1391) has zero OSS-Fuzz-awareness: counts['no_data_count'] stays 0, answered_all=True, presence='none' (line 1324), data_status='complete' (line 1377). gate_exit_code (line 379-380) returns 0; _verified_clean (line 1516-1517) returns True; _render_table (line 1530-1531) prints the 'authoritatively queried' message. discover() reaches this via _is_sbom/_parse_sbom unconditionally for any *.cdx.json/*.spdx.json/bom.json (lines 869-993). No guard, filter, or test anywhere blocks this.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Reproduced end-to-end with a local script (no network, _query_online mocked) against a CycloneDX SBOM component with no purl, loaded via sys.path.insert from C:/firebaseprojects/beyondthesecurity-sbom, run with python -I. _parse_sbom (osvscanner.py:753/774) sets ecosystem='OSS-Fuzz' for the purl-less component. With a mocked OSV answer (vulns=[], ok=True) and no osv_ecosystems candidates (normal SBOM path never sets them), the fw-distro-fallback guard at line 1181 is skipped, so query_status stays 'queried_online' (ANSWERED). _completeness_block (grep confirms zero 'OSS-Fuzz' references outside lines 753/774/1176/1180) has no ecosystem awareness: result was completeness.data_status='complete', vulnerability_presence='none', gate_exit_code(result)==0, OsvScanner._verified_clean(result)==True, and _render_table printed exactly 'No known vulnerabilities found (all components authoritatively queried).' Every claim in the defect matched observed behavior precisely.

### [critical / confirmed] `bts_sbom/reachability.py:591` — enrich_sca_vulns() keys reachability results only by cve_id, so two vulnerability entries for DIFFERENT packages that share one CVE id silently overwrite each other's verdict

- 분석 영역: lens-honesty (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: `by_cve = {r.cve_id: r for r in results}` (line 591) builds a dict keyed solely on cve_id, even though `results` has one entry per input vuln (which also carries package/version). The final loop (`r = by_cve.get(v.get("cve_id", ""))`, line 593) then assigns the SAME (last-processed) ReachabilityResult to every vuln dict sharing that cve_id, regardless of which package it actually describes.
- 재현 조건: Call enrich_sca_vulns(vulns, source_files) with two vuln dicts sharing cve_id='CVE-9999-0001' but different affected_functions: pkgA's function is reachable from main() (a real call path exists), pkgB's is not. Executed: with DISTINCT cve ids the engine correctly reports pkgA=REACHABLE (with the real call path) and pkgB=REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN; with the SAME shared cve id, BOTH entries come back REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN -- the genuinely REACHABLE verdict for pkgA is silently discarded and replaced by pkgB's weaker verdict purely because of key collision in the output dict.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Traced reachability.py:443-556: analyze() appends exactly one ReachabilityResult per vuln dict (package/version carried on the result, unused afterward). enrich_sca_vulns builds by_cve={r.cve_id: r for r in results} (line 591) then looks up by_cve.get(v.get("cve_id","")) (line 593) — no package/version in the key, so same-cve_id results from different packages collide and dict comprehension keeps only the last. No caller enforces unique cve_id per batch; no test covers this; no guard exists. Executed it directly: distinct cve ids gave pkgA=REACHABLE (path main->vulnFuncA), pkgB=REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN; identical shared cve_id gave BOTH pkgA and pkgB=REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN, silently discarding pkgA's genuine REACHABLE verdict. Exactly matches the claimed trigger.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Read bts_sbom/reachability.py:442-556: ReachabilityEngine.analyze() produces one ReachabilityResult per input vuln (carrying cve_id/package/version). Line 591's `by_cve = {r.cve_id: r for r in results}` collapses this to one entry per cve_id, discarding all but the last. Line 593's loop then assigns that single result to every vuln dict sharing the cve_id. Reproduced with a script in temp dir (sys.path.insert to repo, no network, no repo modification): two source files, funcA reachable from main(), funcB not. With distinct CVE ids: pkgA=REACHABLE (path ['main','funcA']), pkgB=REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN — correct. With both vulns sharing cve_id='CVE-9999-0001': BOTH entries came back REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN, i.e. pkgA's genuine REACHABLE verdict was silently overwritten by pkgB's weaker verdict purely from the dict-key collision. This directly violates the module's own documented "HONEST VERDICT CONTRACT" (REACHABLE must never be downgraded). No guard exists against cve_id collisions across packages, and such collisions are realistic in C/C++ SBOMs (shared vendored libraries across multiple components).

### [critical / confirmed] `sbomplus/workspace.py:121` — Local-folder ingestion materialises real OS symlinks inside the 'isolated' work copy, enabling arbitrary file read and, via patch.apply(), arbitrary file write outside the work copy

- 분석 영역: lens-honesty (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: prepare() for a local-folder source calls shutil.copytree(p, dest, ignore=_IGNORE, symlinks=True). With symlinks=True, shutil preserves symlinks AS symlinks in dest rather than dereferencing them, contradicting the module's own docstring ('symlinks are not materialised'). The git-clone path is actually protected (core.symlinks=false), but the documented local-folder input type is not.
- 재현 조건: Analyse a local folder containing e.g. `package.json -> C:/Users/victim/.ssh/some_file` (or any path the analyst's account can read/write). After workspace.prepare(), the work copy's package.json is a live OS symlink. Confirmed by execution: reading the copied file disclosed content of a file entirely outside the work copy; then calling patch.apply(root=<work copy>, plans=[...], approved=True) on a plan whose target file is that symlink wrote the upgraded dependency version straight into the victim file outside the work copy (demonstrated end-to-end with a JSON victim file).
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): workspace.py:121 `shutil.copytree(p, dest, ignore=_IGNORE, symlinks=True)` is the only guard for local-folder ingestion; no caller (cli.py:173, pipeline.py:63) or other code pre-validates/strips symlinks, and only the git-clone path sets `core.symlinks=false` (line 99). I reproduced end-to-end: created `srcfolder/package.json` as a real OS symlink to an outside victim JSON; `workspace.prepare(srcfolder, work_root)` produced a work copy whose `package.json` is `os.path.islink==True` pointing at the victim file; reading it disclosed victim content (arbitrary read). patch.py's `apply()` opens targets via plain `open(os.path.join(root, rel), ...)` with no `realpath`/containment check (confirmed absent repo-wide via grep for islink/readlink/realpath/O_NOFOLLOW — only workspace.py's two mentions, neither reachable from the local-folder path). Calling `patch.apply(root=dest, plans=[...], approved=True)` wrote the new version straight into the victim file outside the work copy, confirmed by re-reading it afterward (`1.0.0` -> `9.9.9`). Matches the docstring's broken promise and the trigger exactly.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Reproduced end-to-end by running real code (script in OS temp dir, repo read-only, no network). workspace.py:121 calls shutil.copytree(p, dest, ignore=_IGNORE, symlinks=True) for local-folder sources. This materializes a real OS symlink in the work copy (os.path.islink(copied)==True, target resolved to the victim file outside work_root) — contradicting the module docstring's "symlinks are not materialised" (workspace.py:5-6). Reading the copied file disclosed the victim file's content. Calling patch.apply(root=dest, plans=[...], approved=True) then opened the symlinked package.json for write, writing the upgraded version straight into the victim file outside the work copy (verified via diff of victim file before/after). Only the git-clone path sets core.symlinks=false (workspace.py:99); the local-folder path has no equivalent guard. Fully reachable via documented CLI/server/pipeline entry points that call workspace.prepare on local folders.

### [critical / confirmed] `sbomplus/patch.py:332` — apply() writes/reads outside the work-copy root with no path-containment check

- 분석 영역: plus-patch-deliver (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: _targets_for()/apply() use plan['files'] entries verbatim as root-relative paths (os.path.join(root, rel)) with no check that the result stays under root. A files entry containing '..' segments (reachable via a symlink in a locally-copied untrusted project - workspace.py copies local sources with shutil.copytree(..., symlinks=True) - or via a cross-directory manifest reference the core scanner may report, e.g. pip '-r ../x/requirements.txt') lets apply() read and overwrite an arbitrary file reachable by relative traversal from the work copy, defeating the project's central 'origin is never written without an explicit flag' guarantee, since this write happens entirely inside patch.py before deliver.py's allow_origin_write/allow_push gates are ever consulted. deliver.py's 'inplace' target compounds this: it runs `git apply --unsafe-paths` (deliver.py:71,77), explicitly disabling git's own built-in refusal of a path-escaping patch.
- 재현 조건: Call patch.apply(root, [plan], approved=True) with plan['files']=['../secret/requirements.txt'] (or any files path containing '..'); confirmed by execution: the sibling file ../secret/requirements.txt is overwritten (requests==2.19.1 -> requests==2.31.0) even though it is outside root and no flag was passed.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Confirmed by direct execution. sbomplus/patch.py _targets_for (lines 249-268) passes plan["files"] entries straight through unchanged, and apply() (line 332, os.path.join(root, rel)) reads then writes that path with zero containment check (no normpath/commonpath/abspath validation anywhere in patch.py, engine.py, pipeline.py, or deliver.py). pipeline.py:153 calls patchmod.apply(path, cur_plans, approved=True, ...) with plans built straight from engine.analyze() scan output - no sanitization of plan['files'] occurs anywhere before apply(). I reproduced the exact trigger: patch.apply(work_dir, [plan with files=['../secret/requirements.txt']], approved=True) overwrote a sibling file outside the work root, flipping requests==2.19.1 -> requests==2.31.0, with out['status']=='applied' (not blocked). This write happens entirely inside patch.py's own disk write (lines 371-373), before deliver.py's allow_origin_write/allow_push gates (deliver.py:62-79) are ever reached - those only guard the separate 'inplace'/'github' delivery paths, not the work-copy write. Also confirmed workspace.py:121 uses shutil.copytree(..., symlinks=True) for local-copy sources (git-clone sources get core.symlinks=false at workspace.py:99, but local copies do not), supporting the symlink-reachability claim as an additional vector. No guard, validator, or test anywhere neutralizes this; it is a real, currently-reachable CWE-22 arbitrary file write that defeats the project's stated "origin is never written without an explicit flag" guarantee.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Reproduced by execution (temp script, no repo modification, no network). With root=work, plan["files"]=["../secret/requirements.txt"], patch.apply(root,[plan],approved=True) read and overwrote the sibling file outside root: content changed requests==2.19.1 -> requests==2.31.0, status "applied", changed_files=["../secret/requirements.txt"]. Confirmed sbomplus/patch.py:249-268 (_targets_for) and :331-372 (apply) use plan["files"] verbatim via os.path.join(root, rel) with zero containment check (no normpath/commonpath/realpath test). Confirmed sbomplus/engine.py:194-199 (rel()) likewise builds "files" via os.path.relpath with no re-containment, so a scanner-reported source outside root propagates unchecked. Confirmed deliver.py:71,77 use `git apply --unsafe-paths`, and workspace.py:121 uses shutil.copytree(..., symlinks=True), exactly as cited. No guard exists anywhere in the call chain (pipeline.py -> patch.apply).

### [critical / confirmed] `sbomplus/loop.py:43` — Regression gate has no metric that catches a silent collapse of component/vulnerability detection

- 분석 영역: plus-pipeline-loop-cli (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: gate() only evaluates keys in HIGHER/LOWER that are non-None in BOTH base and candidate; batch.aggregate() guards every rate (license_resolved_rate, plan_verified_rate, fix_applied_rate, sbom_ref_rate's underlying ref_integrity) with `if X else None/1.0` when the denominator is 0, and components/vulns counts are not in HIGHER/LOWER at all. If a code change makes engine.analyze() legitimately return 0 components/0 vulns for every project without raising (pipeline.run_project reports no error in that case), aggregate() yields license_resolved_rate=None, plan_verified_rate=None, fix_applied_rate=None and sbom_ref_rate=1.0 (ref_integrity's 'rate':1.0 default when there are 0 affects to check), and cyclonedx/spdx stay 'valid' for an empty document. flatten()+gate() then see no metric that dropped, so decision='accept'.
- 재현 조건: Reproduced directly: constructed a base metrics dict (components=2000, vulns=1200, normal rates) and a candidate dict representing a total-detection-loss run (components=0, vulns=0, several rates None, sbom_ref_rate=1.0, cyclonedx/spdx_valid=1.0); loop.gate(base, cand) returned {'pass': True, 'failed': []}.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Traced every path: loop.gate() (loop.py:43-63) only diffs keys in HIGHER/LOWER (loop.py:21-23), which excludes 'components'/'vulns' even though flatten() (loop.py:31) carries them. batch.aggregate() (batch.py:40,44,49) sets license_resolved_rate/plan_verified_rate/fix_applied_rate to None via 'if X else None' when denominators (comps/ups/withfix) are 0; gate() skips any key where either side is None (loop.py:49-50). sbom.ref_integrity() (sbom.py:146) returns rate=1.0 when there are 0 affects, so sbom_ref_rate stays 1.0. pipeline.run_project() (pipeline.py:72-77) records no error when engine.analyze() returns normally with empty components/vulns lists. Reproduced directly: calling loop.gate(base, cand) with components 2000->0, vulns 1200->0, the None-ified rates, sbom_ref_rate=1.0, and arch_coverage/cyclonedx/spdx unchanged returned {'pass': True, 'failed': []}. No sanity/min-components guard exists anywhere in sbomplus. Existing test (test_regression_gate_blocks_drop_over_delta) only covers a non-None rate dropping, not this None/default-masking path.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 critical, 실행 예): Verified by reading sbomplus/loop.py:21-23 (HIGHER/LOWER omit components/vulns), batch.py:38-49 (license_resolved_rate, plan_verified_rate, fix_applied_rate →None when denominator 0), sbom.py:146 (ref_integrity rate defaults to 1.0 when 0 affects), and gate() at loop.py:47-62 which skips any key where base or cand is None. Reproduced directly: ran loop.gate(base, cand) with base=normal (2000 components/1200 vulns) and cand=total collapse (0 components/0 vulns, several rates None, sbom_ref_rate=1.0, cyclonedx/spdx valid). Actual output: {"pass": true, "failed": [], "deltas": {...no components/vulns key...}}. decision would be "accept" per run_pass line 160. Defect is real and exactly as described.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/license_categories.py:83` — license_categories.py and license_utils.py disagree on CC-BY-SA-4.0 and on GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0

- 분석 영역: core-licenses (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: license_categories._add(STRONG_COPYLEFT, ..., 'CC-BY-SA-4.0', 'CC-BY-SA-3.0') classifies CC-BY-SA-4.0 as strong-copyleft, while license_utils._LICENSE_MAP (line 73) classifies the same id as WEAK_COPYLEFT. Separately, license_utils._LICENSE_MAP (line 78) special-cases the full string 'gpl-2.0-only with classpath-exception-2.0' to WEAK_COPYLEFT, but license_categories.category() has no mechanism for WITH-exceptions at all (its documented scope is a single non-compound id) and license_norm.parse_compound_licenses()/is_copyleft() -- which is built on license_categories -- consequently reports is_copyleft_only=True for that exact common Java/OpenJDK license combination, the opposite conclusion from license_utils.classify_license() on the identical string.
- 재현 조건: license_categories.category('CC-BY-SA-4.0')=='strong-copyleft' vs license_utils.classify_license('CC-BY-SA-4.0')=='Weak Copyleft'. license_norm.parse_compound_licenses('GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0')['is_copyleft_only']==True vs license_utils.classify_license(same string)=='Weak Copyleft'. Both confirmed by execution.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Executed directly. license_categories.category('CC-BY-SA-4.0') (license_categories.py:83, _add(STRONG_COPYLEFT,...,'CC-BY-SA-4.0')) returns 'strong-copyleft'; license_utils.classify_license('CC-BY-SA-4.0') (license_utils.py:73) returns 'Weak Copyleft'. For 'GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0': license_norm.parse_compound_licenses strips the WITH exception via license_spdx's atom.raw (license_spdx.py _unary/raw only carries the base id, not the exception), so leaf_ids=['GPL-2.0-only'], category()='strong-copyleft', giving is_copyleft_only=True; license_utils.classify_license matches the full lowercased string as a special case (license_utils.py:78) returning 'Weak Copyleft' — opposite verdicts on the identical string, confirmed live. category() has no WITH-exception handling at all (docstring: single non-compound id only). Checked all callers (sca_report.py, license_scan.py, osvscanner.py, spdx_sbom.py, tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py): none reconcile the two classifiers or test this case; is_copyleft/parse_compound_licenses are unused elsewhere but are public API, so the bug is live and reachable by any caller.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 medium, 실행 예): Reproduced both claims by execution (sys.path.insert, no network, /tmp script): category('CC-BY-SA-4.0')=='strong-copyleft' (license_categories.py:83) vs classify_license('CC-BY-SA-4.0')=='Weak Copyleft' (license_utils.py:73). parse_compound_licenses('GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0') gives leaf_ids=['GPL-2.0-only'] (exception stripped, license_norm.py:150-157), category('GPL-2.0-only')=='strong-copyleft' so is_copyleft_only=True, while classify_license on the identical compound string hits the exact-string fast path (license_utils.py:456-457) returning 'Weak Copyleft'. license_categories.category() indeed has no WITH-exception mechanism. Mitigating: grep shows license_categories/license_norm's copyleft helpers (category/is_copyleft/is_copyleft_only/parse_compound_licenses) have zero callers outside license_norm.py itself — production code (sca_report.py, osvscanner.py, license_scan.py) only uses license_utils.classify_license. No tests cover this. So real but currently dead-path inconsistency.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/license_categories.py:94` — Naive substring fallback misclassifies 'Simplified BSD License' (and similar strings) as Weak Copyleft

- 분석 영역: core-licenses (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: category()'s _SUBSTRING fallback list checks `needle in low` with no word-boundary anchoring. The needle 'mpl' (meant to catch Mozilla Public License spellings) is a substring of the common word 'Simplified', so any license string containing it is caught by the 'mpl' rule before the later 'bsd' rule is ever reached. The identical needle exists in license_utils.py's parallel _SUBSTRING_RULES list (line 195), which is the one actually used by the production sca_report.py path via classify_license().
- 재현 조건: category('Simplified BSD License') and classify_license('Simplified BSD License') -- a common human-readable alias for the permissive BSD-2-Clause license (used in PyPI classifiers, package.json license fields, etc.) -- both return weak-copyleft / 'Weak Copyleft' instead of permissive. Also reproduced with 'Sample License' and 'Simple Public License v2.0'. Confirmed by execution.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing both paths. license_categories.py:94 `for needle, cat in _SUBSTRING: if needle in low:` iterates `_SUBSTRING` where `("mpl", WEAK_COPYLEFT)` (list index ~8) precedes `("bsd", PERMISSIVE)` (index ~17). "simplified" contains "mpl" as a substring (si-MPL-ified), so `category('Simplified BSD License')` returns weak-copyleft, verified: `weak-copyleft`. license_utils.py:195 has the identical ordering issue in `_SUBSTRING_RULES` (mpl at line 195, bsd at line 214); `classify_license()` at line 436 calls `_normalize()` (lowercase+strip parens only, no word-stripping) then falls through to this same substring loop at line 480-482 with no exact-match or word-boundary guard for non-SPDX strings. Executed: `classify_license('Simplified BSD License')` → `'Weak Copyleft'`, same for 'Sample License' and 'Simple Public License v2.0'. sca_report.py:36/410 confirmed to import and use `license_utils.classify_license`/`analyze_licenses` as the production path. No guard, try/except, or test prevents this; it is a genuine unanchored-substring bug reachable from production code.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and by execution. license_categories.py:90-104 and license_utils.py:190-221 both list ("mpl", WEAK_COPYLEFT) before the "bsd"/"mit" rules, and category()/classify_license() use plain `needle in low` substring checks with no word-boundary anchoring (license_categories.py:128-130, license_utils.py:480-482). "simplified" contains "mpl" at index 2-4. Ran a sandboxed script (sys.path.insert, python -I, /tmp, no network, no repo edits): category('Simplified BSD License')='weak-copyleft', classify_license('Simplified BSD License')='Weak Copyleft'; also reproduced for 'Sample License' and 'Simple Public License v2.0'. No exact-match guard in _LICENSE_MAP/_EXACT catches these aliases first, so the substring fallback is reached and mis-fires. 'Simplified BSD License' is a common PyPI/package.json alias for permissive BSD-2-Clause, so this is a realistic, reachable false-positive in the production license-compliance path (sca_report.py -> classify_license).

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py:84` — strip('()') corrupts nested parenthesized SPDX expressions written into the SBOM

- 분석 영역: core-licenses (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: normalize() computes `norm = expression_string(node).strip('()')`. Python's str.strip removes characters from BOTH ends repeatedly while they are in the given set, so when the AST is itself wrapped (e.g. a top-level And whose last term is a parenthesized Or) the closing paren of an INNER subexpression gets stripped along with the outer one, producing an unbalanced, syntactically invalid SPDX string. This is the exact bug class the project itself documents in docs/CORE_ISSUES.md #2 for a different function (bts_sbom.sbom._valid_expression), but an independent, undocumented instance exists here, in the path that feeds the actual SPDX SBOM document (spdx_sbom.py line 121 licenseDeclared, used by sca_report.py).
- 재현 조건: normalize('BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)') / to_spdx_field(same) returns 'BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT' (missing ')'), status reported as 'deprecated' (not flagged as broken); re-parsing that output with license_spdx.parse_expression raises SpdxParseError('unexpected end of expression'). No validator runs on the emitted spdx_sbom document in sca_report.py before it is included in the final report, so this corrupted value ships to the user for any dual/triple-licensed package with a nested AND/OR expression.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Traced and executed the exact path. license_norm.py:84 `expression_string(node).strip("()")`: for `BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)`, expression_string (license_spdx.py:268-276) renders `(BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT))` — the inner OR group's closing `)` sits adjacent to the outer And's closing `)`, so `.strip("()")` eats both. Ran it: `normalize()` returns normalized=`'BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT'`, status `deprecated`; `to_spdx_field` returns the same truncated string; re-parsing it raises `SpdxParseError: unexpected end of expression`, exactly as claimed. Confirmed spdx_sbom.py:121 feeds this into `licenseDeclared`, and sca_report.py (line 461-463, 553) includes `spdx_doc` unvalidated — `verify_packages` re-normalizes raw input (same bug, no re-parse check) and `ntia_check` never inspects license fields. The sibling `sbomplus.sbom`/`sbomplus.licenses` module (docs/CORE_ISSUES.md #2) has a restoration workaround and `validate_spdx`; this `bts_sbom._internal` path has neither — genuinely independent, unguarded instance.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py:84 (`norm = expression_string(node).strip("()")`) and license_spdx.py's expression_string, which wraps every And/Or node in its own parens. Ran actual repo code (sys.path.insert, no network): normalize('BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)') → normalized='BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT' (status 'deprecated', not 'invalid'/flagged). to_spdx_field returns the same truncated string. Traced the real pipeline: spdx_sbom.py:121 calls license_norm.to_spdx_field(p.get("license")) directly into licenseDeclared; built a full SPDX doc via spdx_sbom.build_spdx with this license and got licenseDeclared='BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT' in the actual document. Re-parsing it with license_spdx.parse_expression raises SpdxParseError('unexpected end of expression'), confirming it's invalid SPDX. Checked sca_report.py: spdx_doc is passed straight into the returned report dict with no validation step in between. verify_packages() also classifies this as 'deprecated' (counts['deprecated']=1), matching the claim that the corruption is mis-reported rather than flagged broken. Every element of the defect (file:line, trigger, output, re-parse failure, missing validator) reproduced exactly as described.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/license_scan.py:95` — Generic GPL-2.0-only signature preempts the dedicated Classpath-exception signature for its own target text

- 분석 영역: core-licenses (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: The nomos signature list's generic GPL-2.0-only entries (requiring only 'gnu general public license' + 'version 2', excluding lesser/affero/any-later) are listed and matched before the dedicated 'GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0' signature (line 156-157) that the comment explicitly says is for the 'common in Java' case. _detect_notice() returns on the first matching signature, so real Classpath-exception header text -- which virtually always also contains 'version 2' -- is classified as plain GPL-2.0-only (strong copyleft) before the Classpath signature is ever reached.
- 재현 조건: Feeding scan._detect_notice() a realistic OpenJDK-style header (GNU General Public License version 2 only + a 'Classpath' exception clause, paraphrased) returns ('GPL-2.0-only', 3, 'gnu general public license') instead of the Classpath-exception id. Confirmed by execution. This then flows into classify_license('GPL-2.0-only') == STRONG_COPYLEFT in _aggregate(), overstating the restrictiveness of a very common, intentionally-weakened license.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing. In _SIGNATURES (license_scan.py:95), ("GPL-2.0-only", ["gnu general public license","version 2"], ["lesser","affero","any later"]) precedes the Classpath signature at lines 156-157, and its exclusion list omits "classpath". _detect_notice (line 207-219) returns on first match. Ran _detect_notice() against a realistic OpenJDK Classpath-exception header: it returned ('GPL-2.0-only', 7, 'GNU General Public License') — never reaching the Classpath signature. classify_license confirms the downstream impact: classify_license('GPL-2.0-only') == 'Strong Copyleft' while classify_license('GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0') == 'Weak Copyleft' (license_utils.py:78 vs :81). No test, guard, or later-stage reconciliation covers this path (no test files reference license_scan/_detect_notice/classpath). The bug is real, unreachable-code is actually unreachable for this common pattern, and intent (comment says Classpath is "common in Java") contradicts the ordering.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing. In bts_sbom/_internal/license_scan.py the `_SIGNATURES` list has GPL-2.0-only (reqs: "gnu general public license"+"version 2"; excl: lesser/affero/any-later) at line 95, listed before the dedicated "GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0" signature at lines 156-157. `_detect_notice()` (line 207-219) iterates `_SIG_COMPILED` in order and returns on first match. I fed a realistic paraphrased OpenJDK-style header (GPL v2 only + "Classpath" exception clause, no "lesser"/"affero"/"any later version") into `_detect_notice()` directly from the repo path (no network, read-only): it returned `('GPL-2.0-only', 4, 'GNU General Public License')` — index 20 in the compiled list (the line-95 signature) — never reaching index 66 (the Classpath signature). `classify_license('GPL-2.0-only')` returned `'Strong Copyleft'`, confirming the downstream overstatement in `_aggregate()`. No guard exists; this is a genuine ordering bug, not a misreading.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/license_utils.py:822` — build_compat_matrix() can never flag single-license-flag restrictions (SSPL/CC-BY-NC/PolyForm/CERN-OHL-S)

- 분석 영역: core-licenses (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: In the `not set_b` branch of build_compat_matrix's pairwise loop, the inner `if ids[i] & set_a and ids[j] & set_a: continue` is followed unconditionally by a second bare `continue` on the next line, so the branch always continues to the next _INCOMPATIBLE_PAIRS entry regardless of outcome. found_incompat is therefore never set from a single-license-flag entry, and the final `if not found_incompat: matrix[i][j] = True` defaults the pair to compatible. analyze_licenses() (same file, lines 627-637) handles the identical _INCOMPATIBLE_PAIRS data correctly for this branch, so the two functions diverge.
- 재현 조건: build_compat_matrix(['SSPL-1.0','MIT']) returns matrix=[[True,True],[True,True]] and incompatible_pairs=[] -- SSPL-1.0's own-file-documented 'entire service stack disclosure obligation (stricter than AGPL)' is invisible in the matrix output; same for build_compat_matrix(['CC-BY-NC-4.0','Apache-2.0']). Confirmed by execution. Not currently called by any other file in the repo (grep found no caller), so the defect is latent rather than live, but it sits on a public, documented API.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 medium, 실행 예): Verified by reading license_utils.py:819-844: inside `if not set_b:` (single-flag entries like SSPL-1.0/CC-BY-NC/PolyForm/CERN-OHL-S, lines 143-186), the inner `if ids[i]&set_a and ids[j]&set_a: continue` is followed by an unconditional bare `continue` (line matching the reported 822 region) regardless of the inner condition's outcome. So this branch can never reach `matrix[i][j]=False`/`found_incompat=True`; `if not found_incompat: matrix[i][j]=True` always wins. Executed `build_compat_matrix(['SSPL-1.0','MIT'])` and `(['CC-BY-NC-4.0','Apache-2.0'])`: both return matrix all-True and `incompatible_pairs=[]`, exactly as claimed. `analyze_licenses` (lines 627-637) correctly flags presence via `matched = [...]`, confirming divergence is unintentional, not a guard. Repo-wide grep (including build/lib duplicate, byte-identical) shows zero callers/tests for `build_compat_matrix` — it is a real, reproducible logic bug but currently dead code.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and execution. At license_utils.py:822-827, inside `if not set_b:`, the inner `if ids[i] & set_a and ids[j] & set_a: continue` is followed by an unconditional `continue` — both branches fall through identically, so `found_incompat` is never set from single-flag entries (SSPL/CC-BY-NC/PolyForm/CERN-OHL-S at lines 148-152,158-162,181-186), and line 843's `if not found_incompat: matrix[i][j] = True` wrongly marks them compatible. Ran build_compat_matrix for all four families (SSPL-1.0+MIT, CC-BY-NC-4.0+Apache-2.0, CERN-OHL-S-2.0+MIT, PolyForm-Noncommercial+MIT) in an isolated Python (-I) with repo path inserted, no network: all returned matrix=[[True,True],[True,True]], incompatible_pairs=[]. analyze_licenses (lines 627-637) handles the same `not set_b` data correctly via a `matched` list. Grep confirms build_compat_matrix has zero callers/tests elsewhere in the repo — latent but real, on a public documented API.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py:411` — pyproject.toml ([tool.poetry] and PEP621 [project.dependencies]) parsing is completely silent-empty on Python 3.10

- 분석 영역: core-manifests (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: parse_pyproject_toml returns [] unconditionally when tomllib is None, with no fallback.
- 재현 조건: Monkeypatching extra.tomllib=None and calling parse_pyproject_toml on a pyproject.toml with `dependencies = ["requests==2.31.0"]` returns [] instead of the pinned requests dependency.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Confirmed by reading and execution. extra.py:30-33 imports tomllib in a bare try/except with no `tomli` backport fallback; bts_sbom/pyproject.toml declares `dependencies = []` (zero third-party deps) and `requires-python = ">=3.10"`, and CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:41) actually runs py3.10, where stdlib tomllib doesn't exist, so tomllib=None for real, not just via monkeypatch. extra.py:411-412 `if tomllib is None: return []` has no fallback; no caller (manifests/__init__.py:38) guards or warns. I ran parse_pyproject_toml with tomllib monkeypatched None against `dependencies = ["requests==2.31.0"]`: returned `[]` vs. the correct DetectedPackage when tomllib present. No test in tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py exercises this path. Same unguarded pattern also silently empties pip.py's parse_poetry_lock and extra.py's Cargo.toml direct-set lookup (lines 57-58, 89-92).
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading extra.py:411-412 and by executing code: with real tomllib, parse_pyproject_toml(pyproject_text) returned the requests==2.31.0 DetectedPackage; after `extra.tomllib = None` (simulating Python 3.10, which lacks stdlib tomllib), the identical call returned []. No tomli backport dependency exists anywhere in the repo (grep found none), and pyproject.toml declares requires-python>=3.10 with CI actually testing 3.10 (.github/workflows/ci.yml, sbomplus_ci.yml), so this is reachable in a real supported configuration, not hypothetical. Unlike cargo.py, which has a regex fallback when tomllib is unavailable, extra.py (and pip.py's parse_poetry_lock) has none — a silent, total feature loss for pyproject.toml/poetry.lock dependency detection on 3.10 with no error/log.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py:21` — Deeply-nested JSON silently empties the entire manifest via a swallowed RecursionError

- 분석 영역: core-manifests (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: json.loads(text) raises RecursionError on JSON nested past ~1500 container levels (well below any realistic file-size limit); the generic `except Exception: return []` swallows it, discarding every component in the file (not just the deep branch), with no error surfaced.
- 재현 조건: A 139KB, otherwise well-formed lockfileVersion-1-style package-lock.json with ~3000 levels of nested `dependencies` makes npm.parse() return [] with no exception. The same root cause (blanket except around json.loads/tomllib.loads) recurs in extra.py's parse_composer_lock (180-183), parse_pipfile_lock (197-200), parse_nuget_lock (269-272), parse_vcpkg_json (539-543), parse_pyproject_toml (413-416), pip.py's parse_poetry_lock (52-55) and cargo.py's tomllib paths. osvscanner.py's separate _probe_manifest_integrity re-parse catches this for 10 registered basenames (surfacing 'corrupt manifest'), but extra.py's vcpkg.json (not in that probe's table) and sbom.py's sbom_from_manifest() (calls parse_file with no probe at all) have no mitigation.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and by execution. npm.py:21-24 is `try: data=json.loads(text) except Exception: return []`; RecursionError subclasses Exception so it's swallowed. Reproduced directly: built a 139,968-byte lockfileVersion-1 package-lock.json with 3000 nested "dependencies" levels and called the real bts_sbom._internal.manifests.npm.parse() — it returned [] with no exception, matching the trigger exactly. Confirmed the same unguarded pattern at extra.py:180-183,197-200,269-272,539-543,413-416, pip.py:52-55, cargo.py. Verified osvscanner.py's _MANIFEST_FORMAT (line 189-195) lists exactly 10 basenames and _probe_manifest_integrity (208-226) pre-checks only those before parse_file, correctly surfacing "corrupt json manifest" there — but vcpkg.json is registered in manifests/__init__.py's parser table yet absent from _MANIFEST_FORMAT, and sbom.py:238 sbom_from_manifest() calls parse_file directly with zero probe. No file-size/recursion-limit guard exists anywhere in the codebase. Analyst's description, including the mitigation nuance, is accurate.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py:21-24 (`try: data = json.loads(text) except Exception: return []`) and by reproduction: in a temp script (sys.path to repo, no network, real npm.py), a lockfileVersion-1-style package-lock.json with 3000 nested `dependencies` levels (~148KB) made `json.loads` raise RecursionError('maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON object...'), caught by the bare `except Exception`, and npm.parse() returned `[]` silently with no exception surfaced — exactly matching the described trigger. Default sys.recursionlimit is 1000 (unmodified anywhere in repo, confirmed via grep). RecursionError is a RuntimeError subclass of Exception, so the catch is real, not a misreading. Also observed a related but distinct failure mode: at depth=1000, json.loads succeeds but the separate lockfileVersion-1 `walk()` recursion (outside the try/except, lines 52-59) raises an uncaught RecursionError — a crash rather than silent emptying, which the report didn't mention but doesn't contradict its core claim.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:13` — requirements.txt silently drops extras, '===' pins, and never follows -r includes

- 분석 영역: core-manifests (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: The pin regex's name group `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` cannot match a trailing `[extra]` bracket, so an extras line fails to match and is dropped entirely even though it is exactly pinned; the version group cannot match PEP440 '===' either; and any line starting with '-' (including '-r other.txt') is unconditionally skipped at line 32-33 with the included file's dependencies never read.
- 재현 조건: parse_requirements on a file containing `requests[security]==2.31.0`, `-r requirements-dev.txt` and `urllib3===1.26.18` alongside normal `==` lines drops all three (confirmed by execution: only the plain `==` lines appear in the output) while reporting the file as fully, successfully parsed.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py:13,32-34 and by execution. `_REQ_RE = r"^\s*([A-Za-z0-9._-]+)\s*==\s*([A-Za-z0-9._!+-]+)"` has no `[`/`]` in the name class, so `requests[security]==2.31.0` never matches (name group can't reach the `==`); `===` can't match either since the literal `==` consumes two of the three `=`, leaving a bare `=` the version class rejects. Line 32: `if not line or line.startswith("-"): continue` unconditionally drops `-r requirements-dev.txt`. Checked manifests/__init__.py and osvscanner.py: `_BY_NAME`/`supported_names()` match only the exact basename `requirements.txt`, so an included `requirements-dev.txt` is never separately discovered by the directory walker either — its deps are genuinely never read anywhere. No `_probe_manifest_integrity` or test covers `.txt` content validation, so parse_ok=True/component_count reflects only survivors, silently. Ran `parse_requirements` on a file with all three cases + one plain `==` line: output was exactly `[flask 2.3.0]`, count 1 — confirms all three described drops by actual execution.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by execution in /tmp/pip_defect_test/test_pip.py (sys.path.insert to repo, no network). Input with `requests[security]==2.31.0`, `-r requirements-dev.txt`, `urllib3===1.26.18`, `flask==2.3.3` yields only `flask 2.3.3` from parse_requirements. Direct regex test on pip.py:13 confirms `_REQ_RE.match` returns None for the extras line (`[` not in name class `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`) and for the `===` pin (second `=` breaks `\s*==\s*`, remaining `=1.26.18` fails version class `[A-Za-z0-9._!+-]+`). parse_requirements:32-33 unconditionally `continue`s on any `-`-prefixed line with no recursive call; grep of manifests/__init__.py and pip.py shows no other code path resolves `-r` includes or logs/warns on skipped lines, so drops are silent. All three sub-claims hold exactly as described.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py:83` — False REACHABLE: no preprocessor model, so #ifdef-disabled code is parsed as live

- 분석 영역: core-reachability (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: strip_noncode() removes comments and blanks string/char literal contents but has no concept of #ifdef/#if/#endif. Code inside a disabled preprocessor block is parsed exactly like always-compiled code, including any calls inside it, which get wired into the call graph as real edges.
- 재현 조건: C file with `void do_work(void) { #ifdef NEVER_DEFINED_FEATURE \n vulnerable_func(); \n #endif \n }` and `main(){ do_work(); }`, with NEVER_DEFINED_FEATURE undefined (so the real build never calls vulnerable_func). ReachabilityEngine.analyze() reports verdict=REACHABLE, path=['main','do_work','vulnerable_func'], unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[].
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 medium, 실행 예): Verified by execution. strip_noncode (callgraph.py:83-116) only strips // and /* */ comments and blanks string/char contents; it has zero `#if`/`#ifdef`/`#endif` awareness (confirmed via grep across callgraph.py). parse_functions/_extract_calls (callgraph.py:426-496) then regex-scan the raw brace-delimited body text for `name(` call sites with no macro evaluation, so text inside a disabled `#ifdef` block is textually identical to live code. I built the exact repro (do_work guarded by `#ifdef NEVER_DEFINED_FEATURE` calling vulnerable_func, main calling do_work) and ran ReachabilityEngine().analyze(): verdict=REACHABLE, reachable_via=['main','do_work','vulnerable_func'], unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[] — matching the report precisely. No guard, validator, or #ifdef-awareness exists anywhere in the module; reachability.py only footnotes "macro-generated or preprocessed-away calls are not visible" on UNREACHABLE verdicts, never on REACHABLE ones. Adjusting severity down from critical: this is a false-positive (conservative, safe-direction) over-approximation inherent to a non-preprocessor-aware, non-path-sensitive call-graph tool — it doesn't hide a real vulnerability, it over-flags dead code, causing wasted triage rather than missed risk.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read callgraph.py:83-116: strip_noncode() handles only `//`, `/* */`, and string/char literals — no #ifdef/#if/#endif logic exists anywhere in bts_sbom (grep for ifdef/preprocess across bts_sbom returned nothing). Reproduced live: wrote test.c with do_work() containing `#ifdef NEVER_DEFINED_FEATURE\n vulnerable_func();\n#endif` and main() calling do_work(), undefined macro. Ran ReachabilityEngine(entry_points={"main"}).analyze([path], vulns) via isolated Python (-I, sys.path.insert, no network). Output exactly matches the trigger: verdict=REACHABLE, reachable_via=['main','do_work','vulnerable_func'], unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[]. The module's own UNREACHABLE-path disclaimer (reachability.py:540-542) only warns about "preprocessed-away calls are not visible" (false negatives), never about disabled-block calls being wired in as false positives — so this failure mode is undocumented and unguarded.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py:481` — False REACHABLE: global-function-name resolution ignores C scoping/shadowing

- 분석 영역: core-reachability (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: _extract_calls resolves an unqualified call name against ONE global flat `defined` set of function names with no notion of local/parameter scope. A function parameter or local variable that merely shares a name with an unrelated global function is resolved to that global function.
- 재현 조건: C file where `void run(fn_t vulnerable_func) { vulnerable_func(); }` and `main` calls `run(safe_func)`. ReachabilityEngine.analyze() reports verdict=REACHABLE, detail='... reachable via: main -> run -> vulnerable_func', unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[] — i.e. full confidence — even though main never calls the real global vulnerable_func (it always calls safe_func through run's parameter).
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing. `_extract_calls` (callgraph.py:447-496) resolves an unqualified call name only against the global `defined` set of function names (line 481: `elif name in defined: callee = name`); `fn.params` is never consulted there (only used later at line 964 for arity-based indirect fallback). I ran the exact trigger: `run(fn_t vulnerable_func){ vulnerable_func(); }`, `main(){ run(safe_func); }`. CallGraph gave `run`'s call site callee="vulnerable_func", indirect=False — a parameter name silently rebound to the unrelated global. `ReachabilityEngine.analyze()` (reachability.py:232-237, 494-504) used that raw `cs.callee` for BFS adjacency, producing verdict=REACHABLE, detail="...reachable via: main -> run -> vulnerable_func", unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[] — exactly as claimed, full confidence, no hedge. No scope/shadow guard exists anywhere in callgraph.py.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Reproduced by execution. bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py:479-491 `_extract_calls` resolves the bare call name against the global `defined` set of function names and never checks `fn.params` (the function's own parameter names) for shadowing. With `void run(fn_t vulnerable_func){vulnerable_func();}` and `main(){run(safe_func);}` in one file, the call graph produced edge `run -> vulnerable_func` with CallSite.indirect=False instead of resolving through the parameter. Running `bts_sbom.reachability.ReachabilityEngine.analyze()` on this file gave verdict=REACHABLE, detail="... reachable via: main -> run -> vulnerable_func", unknown_reasons=[], assumptions=[] — exactly as the defect describes, even though main only ever reaches safe_func. The `collisions`/`callback_candidates` safety nets in reachability.py (same-name-across-files, address-taken/bare-arg registration) don't cover this single-file parameter-shadowing case, and nothing in the code or docs acknowledges this as an intended limitation.

### [high / confirmed] `bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py:544` — Unconditional O(functions x call_sites) scan makes CallGraph construction quadratic on any input

- 분석 영역: core-reachability (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
