Executed it directly: doc with one CRITICAL CVE + vex=[{"status":"not_affected","vulnerability":{"name":CVE}}] (no justification) → MAR-05 verdict="pass", 0 open findings. Confirmed bts_sbom/vex.py:139-153 `_validate_statement` enforces the OpenVEX justification requirement and is invoked by `VexIndex.__init__` (line 387, used for ingesting external VEX, fail-closed per `parse_document` docstring line 419), but sbomplus/sectors.py imports only `engine`, never `bts_sbom.vex`, so MAR-05 bypasses that validation entirely. The pipeline's own vex generator (sbom.openvex) never emits bare not_affected, but maritime_report/industry_reports are public functions that accept any externally-supplied VEX dict, so a vendor-supplied non-compliant VEX reaches this unguarded path. Downgrading slightly from "critical" since the module is explicitly a draft, non-conformity-assessment evidence tool.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading sbomplus/sectors.py:76 and executing it. The code is exactly `if s.get("status") in ("fixed", "not_affected"): closed.add(...)` with no check for `justification`/`impact_statement`. I ran `maritime_report(doc, vex, None)` (sys.path.insert, no network, python -I -B -X utf8) with one CRITICAL CVE and a VEX statement `{'status':'not_affected','vulnerability':{'name':CVE}}` (no justification key) — MAR-05 returned verdict='pass', detail '0 critical/high finding(s)...'. bts_sbom/vex.py:139-153 implements and VexError-raises exactly this fail-closed rule, but sectors.py bypasses it entirely (reads raw dict, never calls parse_document/_validate_statement). Mitigating factor: the only current caller, pipeline.py:99 via sbom.openvex(), never emits 'not_affected' (only fixed/affected), and the sector report isn't wired into any automated policy gate (it's explicitly a draft, non-certifying evidence report). But the function is a public API intended to accept externally-supplied VEX (per its own docstring), so the flaw is real and reachable by any realistic caller passing third-party/supplier VEX.

### [high / confirmed] `sbomplus/patch.py:39` — edit_requirements matches by canonical package name only, silently rewriting/downgrading unrelated marker- or platform-qualified pins of the same package

- 분석 영역: plus-patch-deliver (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: edit_requirements iterates every line of the file and rewrites any line whose canonicalized package name matches the target, regardless of which specific line's version actually corresponds to plan['current_version']. A requirements file with several environment-marker- or platform-qualified pins of the same package at different (already-safe, unrelated) versions has every matching line's '==' clause unconditionally forced to the new safe_version, including ones that never needed changing.
- 재현 조건: edit_requirements(text, 'requests', '2.31.0') on text = "requests==2.19.1 ; sys_platform == 'linux'\nrequests==5.0.0 ; sys_platform == 'darwin'\n". Confirmed by execution: both lines are rewritten to requests==2.31.0, silently downgrading the darwin-specific pin from 5.0.0 to 2.31.0 even though it was never vulnerable and had nothing to do with the plan.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and execution. patch.py:39 matches lines solely by canonical name; the version clause ("rest" holds any `; marker`) is rewritten unconditionally for every `==`/`===` clause at patch.py:51-52, with no comparison against plan['current_version'] (which is never even passed into edit_requirements - only name/new, see patch.py:281-283). Ran edit_requirements(text,'requests','2.31.0') on the exact trigger text: output was `requests==2.31.0 ; sys_platform == 'linux'\nrequests==2.31.0 ; sys_platform == 'darwin'\n`, confirming the darwin pin was silently downgraded 5.0.0→2.31.0. No caller in patch.py or test_patch_delivery.py guards against multiple same-name pins; tests only cover single-pin-per-name files. Not refuted.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read sbomplus/patch.py:30-71. The match guard at line 39 (`engine._canon("pypi", m.group("name")) != canon`) filters only by canonical package name; the per-line version is never compared against plan['current_version'] (which `_edit()` at line 283 never even passes into edit_requirements - only name/new are passed). For every matching line, line 51-52 unconditionally rewrites any `==`/`===` clause to `new`. Reproduced with `python -I` against the repo (no network, read-only): `edit_requirements("requests==2.19.1 ; sys_platform == 'linux'\nrequests==5.0.0 ; sys_platform == 'darwin'\n", "requests", "2.31.0")` returned n=2, rewriting both lines to `requests==2.31.0`, silently downgrading the darwin-specific 5.0.0 pin. No guard, marker-awareness, or blocked status exists for this case elsewhere in patch.py or its callers.

### [high / confirmed] `sbomplus/patch.py:301` — approval_digest() does not bind to target files or verification state, enabling a TOCTOU-style approval replay

- 분석 영역: plus-patch-deliver (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: approval_digest(plans) hashes only sorted (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version) tuples for status=='upgrade' plans. It ignores plan['files'] (which manifest(s) will actually be edited) and plan['verification'] (whether the candidate was OSV-confirmed clean). A token computed/approved against one plan object will be accepted by apply() against any other plan list that happens to share those four fields, even if the files it targets or its verification outcome differ completely.
- 재현 조건: patch.approval_digest([plan_a]) == patch.approval_digest([plan_b]) where plan_a has files=['requirements.txt'], verification.clean=True and plan_b (same ecosystem/component/current/safe version) has files=['unrelated/other_requirements.txt'], verification.clean=False with residual advisories. Confirmed by execution: both digests are identical (078c6000dfb9c284), so apply(root, [plan_b], approved=True, token=digest_from_plan_a) would be accepted.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and executing sbomplus/patch.py:300-303. approval_digest() hashes only (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version) for status=='upgrade' plans, ignoring plan['files'] and plan['verification']. Ran patch.approval_digest([plan_a]) vs ([plan_b]) with identical 4 fields but files=['requirements.txt']/verification.clean=True vs files=['unrelated/requirements.txt']/verification.clean=False (residual advisories): digests matched exactly. Then called apply(root, [plan_b], approved=True, token=digest_from_plan_a) — it was accepted (no digest_mismatch) and actually wrote 'foo==1.2.0' into unrelated/requirements.txt. apply()'s only_verified gate defaults False and is never set True anywhere in server.py/pipeline.py/cli.py, so verification is never enforced independently either. Architecturally (pipeline.py _patch_loop, bts_console.js line 458, cli.py --token) the digest is generated in one full pipeline run and consumed in a second independent re-run, a genuine TOCTOU window. No guard, default, or caller mitigates this.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read sbomplus/patch.py:301-303: approval_digest sorts only (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version) tuples for status=='upgrade' plans, never touching plan['files'] or plan['verification']. Reproduced with a script (sys.path.insert, no network): built plan_a (files=['requirements.txt'], verification.clean=True) and plan_b (files=['unrelated/other_requirements.txt'], verification.clean=False, same eco/component/versions) — approval_digest([plan_a]) == approval_digest([plan_b]) (both f933d41517463b3e), confirming identical hashes despite differing targets/verification. apply() (patch.py:309,316) recomputes digest from the plans actually passed and only compares token==digest, so a token obtained by reviewing plan_a's dry-run would silently pass for plan_b. Traced real usage: cli.py prints the digest from one scan; pipeline.py's _patch_loop (pipeline.py:139-153) reruns a fresh scan/verify before calling apply() with the stored token, so files/verification can legitimately change between review and apply while the digest stays stable — a genuine TOCTOU-style approval-binding gap, not a misreading.

### [high / confirmed] `sbomplus/patch.py:346` — apply() patches only the first manifest containing the vulnerable pin; duplicate declarations elsewhere stay vulnerable but are reported 'applied'

- 분석 영역: plus-patch-deliver (분석가 주장 심각도: critical, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: The per-plan loop over _targets_for()'s candidate files breaks as soon as ONE file is successfully edited (line 346), treating the candidate list as alternatives rather than as all the files that need the same fix. engine.normalize() legitimately accumulates every file that declares the same (ecosystem, name, version) into one component's 'files' list (e.g. requirements.txt + requirements-dev.txt both pinning requests==2.19.1, or several package.json files in a workspace monorepo), so plan['files'] commonly has more than one entry in real repositories. Only the first is ever rewritten; the rest keep the vulnerable pin, yet res['status']='applied' is reported for the whole component with no indication a second known-affected file was skipped. Because pipeline._patch_loop's regression check is scoped to changed_files only, the leftover vulnerability in the un-edited file is also invisible to the revert logic.
- 재현 조건: patch.apply(root, [plan], approved=True) with plan['files']=['requirements.txt','requirements-dev.txt'], both files containing 'requests==2.19.1'. Confirmed by execution: result status is 'applied', changed_files=['requirements.txt'] only; requirements.txt becomes requests==2.31.0 but requirements-dev.txt is left unchanged at requests==2.19.1.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and execution. patch.py:327-346: `_targets_for()` (line 252-268) appends ALL matching manifest files from `plan["files"]` to `targets`, not alternatives. The `for rel, kind in targets:` loop (331) sets `res["status"]="applied"` on the first successful edit and `break`s (346), never touching remaining targets. `remediate.py:38` confirms `plan["files"] = c["files"]`, and `engine.py:237-238` confirms a component's `files` list accumulates every manifest declaring the same (ecosystem, name, version) — e.g. requirements.txt + requirements-dev.txt both pinning requests==2.19.1 become one component with files=[both]. Reproduced by execution: `patch.apply('.', [plan], approved=True)` with `files=['requirements.txt','requirements-dev.txt']` both containing requests==2.19.1 returned status 'applied', changed_files=['requirements.txt'] only; requirements.txt became requests==2.31.0, requirements-dev.txt stayed at 2.19.1 with no skip indication. pipeline.py:171-174 confirms the regression/revert check is scoped to `changed_files`, so the leftover vulnerable file is invisible to that logic. No guard exists to prevent this.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading and execution. patch.py:331-346 loops over _targets_for(plan) candidates and breaks (line 346) on the first successful edit, treating alternatives rather than all required files. engine.py:221-238 keys components by (ecosystem, canon-name, version) and accumulates every declaring file into c['files']; remediate.py:38 copies that straight into plan['files']. Reproduced in /tmp/patch_repro: plan files=['requirements.txt','requirements-dev.txt'], both pinning requests==2.19.1. patch.apply() returned status='applied', changed_files=['requirements.txt'] only; requirements.txt became 2.31.0, requirements-dev.txt stayed 2.19.1, with no mention of the skipped file. Also confirmed pipeline.py:171-174: scoped() filters vulns to `edited=set(changed_files)`, so the leftover vuln in the un-edited file is invisible to the revert/regression logic.

### [high / confirmed] `sbomplus/cli.py:178` — A bad sources_json path silently becomes an empty, 'successful' batch/loop run

- 분석 영역: plus-pipeline-loop-cli (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: `srcs = read_json(a.sources_json, [])` uses util.read_json's default-on-(OSError,ValueError) behavior, so a missing file, a permission error, or invalid JSON for the user-supplied sources_json argument all silently resolve to `[]`. batch.run_batch([], ...) then aggregates an all-None/0 summary (projects=0) and, per the next finding, cli.py's batch branch still returns 0; for `loop`, decision becomes 'accept' (no e2e metric present to compare, RAG metrics unaffected) and cli returns 0 unless the RAG eval alone regresses.
- 재현 조건: Run `sbomplus batch typo_sources.jsno --tag t` (nonexistent/misspelled path): prints a '0 projects, 0 errors' summary and exits 0, giving no indication the path was wrong.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Verified by reading sbomplus/util.py:47-52 (read_json catches OSError/ValueError, returns default) and executing sbomplus/cli.py:178 live: `batch typo_sources.jsno --tag t` (nonexistent path) produced summary {"projects":0,"errors":0,"project_success_rate":null,...} and exit code 0, exactly as the trigger describes — no error field, no indication the path was bad. cli.py:184-186 unconditionally returns 0 for `batch` with no check on srcs emptiness, errors, or summary content. argparse's `sources_json` argument (cli.py:89) has no existence/type validation. For `loop`, traced loop.py's HIGHER/LOWER gating: with recs=[], project_success_rate/license_resolved_rate/sbom_ref_rate/plan_verified_rate/cyclonedx_valid_rate/spdx_valid_rate/arch_coverage all become None and are skipped in gate() (b or c is None → continue), so only rag/chat metrics (computed independently of srcs) can fail the gate — matching the claimed "decision becomes accept unless RAG eval alone regresses." No guard, validator, or test prevents this in either path.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 medium, 실행 예): Verified by reading util.read_json (sbomplus/util.py:47-52): any OSError/ValueError (missing file, bad perms, invalid JSON) returns the default. cli.py:178 calls read_json(a.sources_json, []), so a bad path yields srcs=[]. Reproduced live: ran cli.main(['batch','/tmp/this_path_does_not_exist_typo.jsno',...]) via a temp script against the real repo code (no network, offline/no-llm) - output showed summary.projects=0, summary.errors=0, and the process printed EXIT CODE: 0, with no error/warning about the missing sources_json path. This exactly matches the described trigger and 'silent empty success' behavior; code path confirmed at cli.py:178 and util.py:47-52.

### [high / confirmed] `sbomplus/pipeline.py:113` — An exception inside a later patch round discards all earlier rounds' already-applied, already-committed progress

- 분석 영역: plus-pipeline-loop-cli (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: The entire multi-round _patch_loop(...) call (lines 135-213) is wrapped as a single _Stages.run('patch', ...) stage whose default on exception is `{}` (line 113-114). _patch_loop itself has no internal try/except around any round. If round 1 succeeds (files edited and committed to the work-copy branch, as in SP-AC-17) and round 2 then raises (e.g. engine.analyze() on rescan, remediate.plan/verify, or patchmod.diff throwing on malformed input produced by the patch), the whole function's return value - including the round-1 'applied' entry, branch/commit identifiers, and rounds history - is replaced by {} and only a generic stage_errors['patch'] string survives. Because rec['patch'].get('status') is then None (not 'applied'), the delivery step (line 115) is skipped, so the real, already-made commit is never delivered/reported even though it exists on disk.
- 재현 조건: Round 1 of _patch_loop applies+commits a fix; round 2's engine.analyze(path, ...) rescan raises any exception (e.g. a transient OS/scanner error reading the just-edited manifest) -> rec['patch'] becomes {} instead of reflecting round 1's real, on-disk progress; this contradicts the module docstring's claim (lines 4-6) that 'a partial result is always visible instead of an exception that hides everything.'
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Read pipeline.py:111-213 and patch.py fully; no try/except exists anywhere inside _patch_loop, and patch.apply() with approved=True really writes files and git-commits to branch "sbomplus/auto-fix" (patch.py:371-385). I reproduced it directly: built a real git repo, ran _patch_loop(rounds=2) with a scanner that succeeds round 1 (applies+commits, confirmed via `git log`) then raises RuntimeError on round 2's engine.analyze() rescan (pipeline.py:166). The exception propagated all the way out of _patch_loop uncaught. Wrapping it exactly as pipeline.py:113-114 does (S.run("patch", lambda: _patch_loop(...), {})) produced rec["patch"] == {} with only stage_errors['patch'] set, so rec["patch"].get("status") is None — confirmed via actual execution that the delivery gate at line 115 is skipped, while the real commit and edited requirements.txt remain on disk/branch, invisible in the returned record. rounds defaults to 2 (line 46), so this is the default path, not a contrived edge case. No test (test_patch_delivery.py) covers this scenario and no guard exists.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Reproduced with a real temp git repo (repo itself untouched). Built rec/plans, monkeypatched engine.analyze to succeed on round-1 rescan but raise on round-2 rescan, then called S.run("patch", lambda: _patch_loop(...), {}) exactly as pipeline.py:113-114 does. Result: both round 1 and round 2 of patchmod.apply() wrote requirements.txt and created real git commits (2 commits beyond init, final content flask==3.0.0). Round 2's engine.analyze() raised; _patch_loop (sbomplus/pipeline.py:135-213) has no internal try/except around any round, so the exception propagated out of the whole function. _Stages.run's except (pipeline.py:29-31) caught it and returned the default {}, so rec['patch'] == {} despite two real, on-disk, committed rounds. rec['patch'].get('status') is None, so the delivery gate at pipeline.py:115 is skipped even though commits exist on the work-copy branch. Matches the described defect and contradicts the module docstring's partial-visibility claim (lines 4-6).

### [high / confirmed] `pyproject.toml:39` — sbomplus's knowledge/evals data directories are not shipped by the packaging config, but sbomplus/evals.py assumes they are installed as siblings

- 분석 영역: tests-ci-packaging (분석가 주장 심각도: high, 신뢰도: high)
- 설명: [tool.setuptools.packages.find] only includes Python packages (bts_sbom*, sbomplus*); knowledge/ and evals/ have no __init__.py and are not declared as package-data anywhere, so they are absent from a wheel/sdist built from this config. sbomplus/evals.py's repo_root()/build_index() compute the knowledge path as a sibling of the installed sbomplus package, and sbomplus/rag.py's load_dir() silently returns [] via os.walk on a nonexistent directory (no exception).
- 재현 조건: I built and installed a real (non-editable) wheel from this repo's root pyproject.toml into a temp target dir: bts_sbom and sbomplus landed in site-packages but knowledge/ and evals/ did not. Anyone who does a genuine `pip install bts-sbom` (as opposed to `pip install -e .` from inside the checked-out monorepo, which every test and CI job actually uses) and then runs `sbomplus chat`/`sbomplus serve`/`run_sbomplus.py evalrag` gets a silently empty RAG index and an ungrounded chatbot, with no error raised - contradicting the project's own 'absent evidence is never a pass' discipline.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 아니오): Verified by reading every link in the chain. pyproject.toml:37-39 `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]` include=["bts_sbom","bts_sbom.*","sbomplus","sbomplus.*"] only discovers Python packages; knowledge/ and evals/ (confirmed top-level siblings of sbomplus/, no __init__.py anywhere) aren't packages and are never matched. package-data (line 41-42) only maps bts_sbom->py.typed; no MANIFEST.in/setup.cfg exists to add them as data files. sbomplus/evals.py:11-12 repo_root()=dirname(dirname(__file__)) — in a real (non-editable) install this resolves to site-packages, not the repo; build_index (line 17) joins it with "knowledge". rag.py:218-232 load_dir does os.walk(root) which silently yields [] for a nonexistent dir, no exception. No downstream guard (Index class, cli.py `chat`/`evalrag`/`serve` paths) checks for an empty index. Confirmed further: both CI workflows (ci.yml:52, sbomplus_ci.yml:50) only ever run `pip install -e .`, an editable install whose site-packages link points back into the checked-out repo, so knowledge/evals resolve fine there and this gap is structurally invisible to CI/tests — exactly the asymmetry the analyst described.
- 검증자(confirmed, 심각도 high, 실행 예): Reproduced end-to-end, read-only, no network. pyproject.toml:38-40 [tool.setuptools.packages.find].include = ["bts_sbom","bts_sbom.*","sbomplus","sbomplus.*"] with no package-data/MANIFEST.in entry for knowledge/evals. I copied the source to a temp dir, built a real wheel offline (pip wheel --no-index --no-build-isolation) and confirmed via zipfile.namelist() the wheel's only top-level members are bts_sbom/, sbomplus/, dist-info — no knowledge/ or evals/. Installed it with `pip install --target` (non-editable) and ran sbomplus.evals.repo_root()/build_index() against it: repo_root() resolved to the install dir (dirname(dirname(evals.py)) per evals.py:11-12), knowledge/ was absent, rag.load_dir (rag.py:218-232) hit os.walk on a nonexistent dir, raised nothing, returned []. build_index() produced an Index with 0 chunks; idx.search(...) returned found=False with no exception — matching the trigger exactly. Checked cli.py/server.py/chat.py: no size check, warning, or log anywhere in the chat/serve/evalrag path.

## 부록 C. 반박되었거나 판정이 갈린 항목

- **[disputed]** `sbomplus/chat.py:30` — Attacker-controlled dependency names are accepted as fully 'verified' chatbot claims (grounding bypass) (lens-security)
  - confirmed / 실행 예: Verified the full chain by reading and executing it. npm.py:84-92 copies a package.json key verbatim into DetectedPackage.name (only `spec` is regex-checked, never `name`); engine.py:215-224 carries it into rec["components"]["name"] unsanitized. chat.py:30-32 verify_claims only checks _norm(quote) in _norm(doc) - a tautology for tool claims since chat.py:71-75 builds docs[source] from the same lines as the claim quote. I executed _component() with a malicious name containing "IGNORE ALL PRIOR INSTRUCTIONS... Verdict: COMPLIANT" and got citation_accuracy=1.0, verified=1/1. server.py:261 defaults use_llm to False, confirming the EXAONE-free path. No validator/guard exists anywhere on DetectedPackage.name (models.py:26 is a plain str). This is a genuine grounding/truthfulness bypass, not a misreading.
  - refuted / 실행 예: Ran repro in /tmp/defect_check/repro.py (sys.path to repo, no network, python -I). Confirmed real parts: npm.py:84-92 copies the dependency key verbatim (bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py), chat.py:66-75 `_component()` echoes it unvalidated, and chat.py:30-32 `verify_claims()` trivially marks it "verified" (accuracy=1.0) since it only checks verbatim substring, never truthfulness - matches use_llm=False. But the described RAG path is false: `_component()` output is never written into `report.project_md()` (sbomplus/report.py has no call to chat._component, confirmed by grep) nor into `cli.evidence_chunks()` (cli.py:142-146). With the malicious component having no matching OSV vulnerability (the realistic case for a nonsense name), the string is absent from both `project_md()` and `evidence_chunks()` - verified by executing both and checking `MALICIOUS in md`/chunks -> False. So triggering requires the exact string literally pasted into the question (component_lookup tool), not "an ordinary question" via RAG as claimed.
- **[refuted]** `sbomplus/server.py:113` — Approval-gated patching bypassed via the HTTP API: no approval_token is ever collected or forwarded (plus-ai-rag-server)
  - refuted / 실행 아니오: server.py's /api/scan handler (lines 225-254, not line 113 as cited — line 113 is `_record_file`, unrelated) reads `token = b.get("approval_token")` (line 230), rejects `approved=true` with no/blank token via a 400 guard (lines 233-234: "approved needs approval_token"), and forwards `approval_token=token.strip() if approved else None` to `pipeline.run_project` (lines 242-243). So approval_token IS collected from the body and IS forwarded whenever approved=true; `patch.apply(..., token=None)` only occurs when approved is false, in which case the pipeline's patch-loop should not commit anyway. The described bypass (POST with only approved:true applying/committing without a token) is blocked by the explicit validation at line 233-234 before the job even starts. The defect's core claim is factually wrong about what the handler reads/forwards.
  - refuted / 실행 예: Read server.py's /api/scan handler directly: line 230 reads `token = b.get("approval_token")`; lines 233-234 return HTTP 400 "approved needs approval_token..." if approved is true and token is not a non-empty string; line 242-243 forwards `approval_token=token.strip() if approved else None` into pipeline.run_project. So approval_token IS collected and forwarded, contradicting the claim. Reproduced live: started the real ThreadingHTTPServer via srv.make_handler on 127.0.0.1 with an ephemeral port (no repo changes, no network beyond loopback), POSTed exactly the defect's trigger `{"source": <existing local dir>, "patch": true, "approved": true}` with no approval_token, and got back `400 {"error": "approved needs approval_token: the digest of the plan you reviewed..."}`. The downstream `token is not None` no-op at patch.py:316 is real but unreachable via this path since server.py never lets a None token reach it when approved=true. Also the cited line server.py:113 is `_record_file`, unrelated to the handler (actual logic is ~225-246).
- **[refuted]** `bts_sbom/sectors.py:259` — Ordinary `sector` CLI output (not just --help) crashes on a cp949 console (core-sbom-sector)
  - refuted / 실행 예: Confirmed sectors.py:259 holds an em dash and that it propagates into sector_report() output (verified by running sector_report('cra', sbom-no-vex)). But the claimed "unhandled crash" is false: UnicodeEncodeError IS a subclass of ValueError (verified: issubclass(UnicodeEncodeError, ValueError) == True), and cli.py main() (line ~334) does `except ValueError as exc: print(...); return 2`. Actually ran `PYTHONIOENCODING=cp949 python -m bts_sbom sector --sector cra --sbom test.json`: it exits cleanly with code 2 and prints `bts-sbom: 'cp949' codec can't encode character '—' in position 335: illegal multibyte sequence` to stderr (ASCII-safe, since UnicodeEncodeError.__str__ escapes the char) — no traceback. This matches docs/CORE_ISSUES.md item 4, which already states `bts-sbom sector ...` exits 2 on cp949. Not a new unhandled-crash bug; it's a handled, already-documented graceful failure.
  - refuted / 실행 예: Ran the real CLI in this cp949-console environment (PYTHONPATH=repo python -I -m bts_sbom sector --sector cra --sbom sample.json, no --vex): it does fail, raising UnicodeEncodeError on the em dash from sectors.py:259's CRA-04 detail string, as claimed. But two supporting claims are wrong. (1) "crashes unhandled" is false: UnicodeEncodeError subclasses ValueError, so bts_sbom/cli.py:334-336's existing `except ValueError` catches it, prints "bts-sbom: ...cp949..." and returns exit 2 — handled, not an unhandled traceback. (2) docs/CORE_ISSUES.md:33-34 already explicitly states "bts-sbom sector ... exits 2 with 'cp949' codec can't encode character '—'", so this is not an undocumented gap limited to --help/cli.py as alleged. The encode issue is real but mischaracterized as new/unhandled when it is already documented and already gracefully caught.
- **[disputed]** `sbomplus/evals.py:17` — knowledge/ and evals/ resolved via installed-file-relative path, not shipped by the wheel that provides them (lens-architecture)
  - refuted / 실행 예: Verified the mechanics are real: evals.py:12 repo_root() is two dirname() calls above sbomplus/evals.py; evals.py:17/23 join 'knowledge'/'evals' onto it; pyproject.toml:37-44 only packages bts_sbom*/sbomplus*+py.typed+web assets, no MANIFEST.in/setup.py exists; knowledge/ and evals/*.json live at repo root (confirmed via ls), not under sbomplus/, so a wheel build omits them; rag.load_dir (rag.py:218-230) walks a missing dir silently (os.walk yields nothing, no exception) and util.read_json (util.py:47-52) returns its [] default on missing-file OSError. However docs/SBOMPLUS.md:131 explicitly documents this exact constraint: "RAG 코퍼스와 평가 데이터는... 레포 루트에 있어서, 소스 체크아웃에서 실행해야 챗봇·evalrag가 동작합니다. pip로 설치한 휠에는 들어 있지 않습니다." (chatbot/evalrag only work from a source checkout; not included in the pip wheel). This is a known, intended, author-documented limitation, not an undiscovered packaging gap — matching the "intended" refutation criterion even though the underlying silent-empty-result mechanism is accurately described.
  - confirmed / 실행 예: evals.py:11-12 repo_root() = dirname(dirname(abspath(__file__))), i.e. two levels above sbomplus/. pyproject.toml:37-44 packages.find includes only bts_sbom*/sbomplus* and package-data only py.typed + sbomplus/web/*; knowledge/ and evals/ are sibling top-level dirs (verified via ls) never declared, and setuptools package-data cannot capture data outside a package tree anyway. Reproduced live (temp script, sys.path.insert, no network, repo untouched): copied only sbomplus/+bts_sbom/ into a fake site-packages dir mirroring the declared install set, reimported evals — repo_root() resolved to that fake root, knowledge/ and evals/rag_golden.json were absent, build_index() returned 0 chunks (no exception), rag_eval() returned {'queries':0,...,'chunks':0}. Confirmed util.read_json (util.py:47-52) swallows OSError on missing file returning default, and rag.load_dir's os.walk (rag.py:218-232) silently yields nothing on a missing root. Matches the trigger description exactly: silent empty RAG/eval with no error on a wheel install.
- **[refuted]** `sbomplus/workspace.py:43` — SSH/git host authority is never validated, allowing classic ssh-option-injection-shaped URLs through to git clone (lens-security)
  - refuted / 실행 예: Read workspace.py:41-49 and :98-108. Confirmed normalize_source() does not parse the authority of ssh://\|git@\|file:// URLs (line 43) and passes `spec["url"]` verbatim to `git clone -- <url> <dest>` (line 104). But empirically ran `git clone -- "ssh://-oProxyCommand=touch /tmp/pwned/x" dest` with GIT_SSH_COMMAND pointed at an argv-logging script (git 2.52.0, this sandbox's installed git): git itself rejects the URL with `fatal: strange hostname '-oProxyCommand=touch ' blocked` and never invokes the ssh helper (no argv logged) — this is git's own fix for CVE-2017-1000117, present in every maintained git release since Aug 2017. `prepare()` then treats the nonzero rc as a normal clone failure (workspace.py:106-108). So the specific trigger does not reach ssh option injection; the described CVE-2017-1000117 shape is blocked by git itself, not by sbomplus, making the claimed impact unreachable in practice (would require a 9+ year unpatched git).
  - refuted / 실행 예: Confirmed normalize_source (workspace.py:43) doesn't validate authority: payload 'ssh://-oProxyCommand=.../x' is classified {"kind":"git","url":...} and reaches `git clone -- <url> dest` (line 104) verbatim. But actually running that clone (local, no network, git 2.52.0 installed) produced: "fatal: strange hostname '-oProxyCommand=touch ' blocked" — git itself rejects hostnames starting with '-' at the ssh-transport layer, a fix shipped for CVE-2017-1000117 since git 2.14.1 (Aug 2017). No command executed (marker file never created). Same result for git@ scp-like form. So the exploit chain described only works against git <2.14.1 (~9 years unmaintained); on any currently-supported git it's blocked upstream — a guard outside this file neutralizes it. Missing app-level validation is real but not a working RCE as claimed.

## 부록 E. 프로젝트 주장 점검 (불성립/부분 성립)

전체: fails 34, holds 61, partially 34, unverifiable 2

### core-sbom-sector

- **[fails]** "this module FABRICATES nothing. It only serializes packages that were actually discovered" and "relationships ... never inferred by name similarity" (honesty language in bts_sbom/_internal/sbom.py and bts_sbom/sbom.py docstrings) _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/sbom.py and bts_sbom/sbom.py module docstrings)_  
  근거: sbom_from_resolver_graph dedups resolver-graph nodes by name only (bts_sbom/sbom.py:195), so a graph with two differently-versioned nodes of the same name (e.g. lodash@3.10.1 and lodash@4.17.21) silently drops the second node entirely, and the surviving DEPENDS_ON edge meant for the dropped node gets reattached (via name-keyed ref_by_name) to the wrong, unrelated version. Reproduced: a 3-node graph (left-pad -> lodash@4.17.21, plus a decoy lodash@3.10.1) produces a 2-package document whose only DEPENDS_ON edge points left-pad at lodash@3.10.1 instead of the real target.
- **[partially]** sector_report() / NTIA reuse honesty: "absent evidence is never a pass", "0 fabricated passes", every verdict reflects what NTIA actually measured _(출처: bts_sbom/sectors.py module docstring (HONESTY DISCIPLINE) and README "Honesty discipline")_  
  근거: True for the not_evidence paths (VEX/eol absence) which were confirmed working. But _check_ntia_minimum (sectors.py:211-217) returns verdict 'pass' whenever fail_count==0, not when elements are actually satisfied. Constructed a CycloneDX doc where only 3 of 7 NTIA elements reach PASS (score 76, grade B) and the check still returns verdict='pass' with detail text '3/7 elements PASS (score 76.0, grade B)' — a self-contradictory pass label on materially incomplete evidence.
- **[partially]** NTIA supplier scoring "basis" reports "how many of the credited components rest on a supplier DERIVED from a package namespace ... so a reader can discount them" _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/ntia_check.py module docstring)_  
  근거: Holds for documents built via _internal/spdx_sbom.py or the CycloneDX builder (which write a 'supplier derived from ...' comment / supplier-basis property). Fails for the public sbom_from_resolver_graph -> build_spdx_document path: supplier_resolve.resolve() computes basis='npm-scope-namespace', derived=True internally (bts_sbom/sbom.py:214-219) but build_spdx_document (lines 120-126) only ever writes a bare 'Organization: <name>' string with no basis comment, so ntia_check._spdx_supplier_kind (comment-text heuristic) always classifies it 'named' instead of 'derived'. Reproduced: an npm-scoped package '@google/foo' yields supplier 'Organization: google' with no caveat, and NTIA reports the supplier element as 100% with basis '1 of 1 components' (no derived-namespace caveat).
- **[fails]** "Validity is proven in benchmark/spdx_sbom by round-tripping the output through the official spdx-tools parser + full validator." _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py module docstring)_  
  근거: No 'benchmark' directory of any kind exists anywhere in this repository (find . -iname '*benchmark*' returns nothing), and no code path in the repository calls bts_sbom.sbom.validate_spdx (or any other validator) on this builder's output — sca_report.py embeds spdx_sbom.build_spdx()'s result directly under an 'spdx_sbom' key with no validation step.
- **[partially]** Windows cross-platform support: "every text-mode file read/write in the package uses explicit UTF-8"; "Lockfiles produced on Windows (BOM, CRLF, backslash paths) parse identically to POSIX-produced ones" _(출처: bts_sbom/README.md "Platform support" table)_  
  근거: Holds for manifest/lockfile parsing (tested by test_utf8_bom_lockfiles_parse_on_every_os) and for every write path checked (cli.py _emit, sectors.py write_evidence use encoding='utf-8'). Fails for the CLI's own document-loading path: cli.py's _load_json (used for --sbom/--vex/--eol/--findings/--policy) opens with plain encoding='utf-8', not 'utf-8-sig'; reproduced a UTF-8-BOM-prefixed JSON file causing _load_json to raise (caught and reported as exit code 2, so it fails honestly, but it does not 'parse identically' to a non-BOM file as the README implies for Windows-produced files).

### core-scanner

- **[partially]** HONESTY DISCIPLINE (osvscanner.py docstring, lines 24-35): 'a package the scanner could NOT authoritatively query ... is NOT reported as clean ... so an empty vulnerability list never masquerades as a verified pass.' _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:24-35,289-391)_  
  근거: Holds inside osvscanner.py itself: _completeness_block/_verified_clean/gate_exit_code/sca_gate_check/_status_line all fold unqueried+offline_no_data+version_unresolved into data_status!=complete before any 'clean' claim (traced and confirmed by reading). But sca_report.py's separate _markdown() footer (line 251) only sums unqueried+offline_no_data, dropping version_unresolved, unlike osvscanner.py's own _status_line (line 1639-1640) which includes it - so the discipline is not uniformly enforced across this module's own two report builders.
- **[fails]** 'CVSS v4.0 base score ... FIRST.org CVSS 4.0 specification (2023): lookup-table algorithm' (osvscanner.py lines 410-411, 461-465) — implies parity with the official v4.0 scoring algorithm. _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:410-527)_  
  근거: Executed: CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:N/UI:N/VC:H/VI:H/VA:{H,L,N}/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N all score 9.8 via _cvss_v40_base_score, though the official FIRST algorithm's severity-distance interpolation (never implemented here) differentiates vectors within the same macrovector bucket. The code only performs the macrovector lookup (Table 33 max) and returns it unmodified, systematically returning the bucket ceiling instead of the true score for any non-worst-case vector.
- **[fails]** module docstring architecture table (osvscanner.py line 22): 'native OSV-Scanner JSON → _to_native [this module]'. _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:22)_  
  근거: grep across the whole repository finds zero definitions or references to a function named _to_native; the native result dict is built ad hoc inline inside scan() (lines 1142-1155, 1227-1236). Stale documentation.

### core-vuln-data-version

- **[fails]** Remediation and the OSV data layer never surface a git commit SHA as a safe/fixed version (project-wide honesty discipline named explicitly in this review's own brief). _(출처: task brief FOCUS text; implicit in remediation.py's 'minimal SAFE VERSION' / 'ADVISORY ONLY' framing)_  
  근거: Confirmed by execution: osv_local_db._vuln_affects, osv_client._parse_vulns and remediation.suggest_fixes/iter_fix_bounds all accept a GIT-range's commit-SHA 'fixed' event as a version bound (see defects above). sbomplus/engine.py's fixed_versions(), in the same repo, explicitly filters GIT ranges and documents why -- proving this is a known-necessary filter the core slice omits.
- **[partially]** Network failure is never read as clean (OSV client's data_status mapping). _(출처: task brief FOCUS text; osv_client.post_query's own docstring)_  
  근거: post_query()/query_package_raw() correctly return an explicit ok=False on failure (confirmed by execution) and the production path (osvscanner.py) uses exactly that. But the same class's query_package()/_post_with_retry() convenience methods discard ok and return [] on failure -- confirmed by execution to be indistinguishable from a genuine zero-vuln result -- so the guarantee holds only for the primitives the production scanner happens to use, not for the whole public OsvClient surface.
- **[fails]** 'no data is never reported as no vulnerability' extends to EPSS/KEV prioritization signals, i.e. a feed outage must not look like a measured zero. _(출처: task brief's stated honesty discipline, applied to epss_kev.py)_  
  근거: enrich_cves() returns a full, successful-looking {epss:0.0, kev:False} for every CVE even when fetch_epss/load_kev_set both failed outright (confirmed by execution with urlopen stubbed to raise); sca_report.py then labels this 'live-api' and never consults the offline curated table that has real data for the same CVE, confirmed by reading sca_report.py's _enrich_findings_epss_kev.
- **[partially]** README's public-API table: version_match.py delivers 'precise match verdicts with explanations' for pip/npm/cargo/go specifier syntax. _(출처: README.md line 125)_  
  근거: A wide battery of hand-run cases (PEP440 epoch/pre/post/dev, npm caret/tilde/x-range, go pseudo-versions, prerelease opt-in) all matched expected results, but the '==' operator for npm/cargo/go -- explicitly listed in the module's own _SEM_OP_RE regex as supported -- always fails closed even on an exact match (see defect above), so 'precise' does not hold for that documented syntax element.

### core-manifests

- **[fails]** README.md:12,187 / pyproject.toml:10 / bts_sbom/pyproject.toml:10 — "standard library only, Python >= 3.10" (zero third-party runtime deps, 3.10 is a fully supported minimum, exercised in the project's own 3-OS x {3.10,3.12} CI matrix, .github/workflows/ci.yml:41) _(출처: README.md, pyproject.toml, bts_sbom/pyproject.toml, .github/workflows/ci.yml)_  
  근거: tomllib was added to the stdlib only in Python 3.11; on 3.10 `tomllib = None` in pip.py, extra.py and cargo.py. Confirmed by execution (monkeypatching each module's `tomllib` to None, i.e. forcing exactly the branch real 3.10 takes): extra.parse_cargo_lock, pip.parse_poetry_lock and extra.parse_pyproject_toml all return [] unconditionally — Cargo.lock, poetry.lock and pyproject.toml are completely unparsed on the declared-and-CI-tested minimum Python version, not just on hostile input.
- **[fails]** Project's stated honesty discipline (task framing / docs): "absent evidence is never a pass"; a parse failure must be surfaced, not silently reported as a clean/empty result _(출처: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md framing, bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/dpkg.py and rpm.py docstrings)_  
  근거: pip.py's requirements.txt parser silently drops extras (`pkg[extra]==ver`), `===` pins and unfollowed `-r other.txt` includes with zero disclosure (confirmed by execution, see defects). Deeply-nested JSON/TOML manifests silently empty an entire manifest's component list via a swallowed RecursionError (confirmed by execution). Neither case appends anything to parse_failures/notes for the affected content; the file is reported as a normal, fully-parsed source.
- **[partially]** osvscanner.py:208-226's _probe_manifest_integrity + pip.py/dpkg.py-style 'never raises' parsers together guarantee that a parse failure is always surfaced, never silently indistinguishable from an empty manifest _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:185-226 vs bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py:535-562, bts_sbom/sbom.py:233-245)_  
  근거: _probe_manifest_integrity's _MANIFEST_FORMAT table covers 10 basenames (package.json/package-lock.json/npm-shrinkwrap.json/composer.lock/pipfile.lock/packages.lock.json/cargo.lock/cargo.toml/poetry.lock/pyproject.toml) and does catch the RecursionError-on-deep-nesting case for those; it omits vcpkg.json (also JSON-based, same vulnerable `except Exception: return []` pattern in extra.py:539-543), and sbom.py's sbom_from_manifest() calls manifests.parse_file directly with no probe at all for any format, so the mitigation is partial and caller-specific rather than a fix in the parser layer itself.

### core-reachability

- **[fails]** reachability.py docstring: "REACHABLE ... A found path is real code regardless of graph completeness, so it is never downgraded." _(출처: bts_sbom/reachability.py:14-17)_  
  근거: Two executed counterexamples produce REACHABLE with empty unknown_reasons/assumptions for code that never actually executes the target: (1) a call to the vulnerable function placed inside '#ifdef NEVER_DEFINED_FEATURE / #endif' is parsed as a live call (parser has no preprocessor awareness), yielding REACHABLE main->do_work->vulnerable_func with zero caveats; (2) a function parameter that shadows a global function's name ('void run(fn_t vulnerable_func)') is resolved by name against the GLOBAL defined-function map with no scope awareness, so calling the parameter inside run() is reported as a direct call to the real global vulnerable_func (path main->run->vulnerable_func, REACHABLE, zero caveats) even though main only ever passes a different function (safe_func) to run().
- **[partially]** callgraph.py vendor docstring: a 'Coverity-class' call-graph engine with indirect-call/function-pointer resolution, nominal C++ virtual/override dispatch, SCC detection and reverse-topo summaries (lines 3-14), consumed by reachability.py's 'real callgraph engine (CallGraph)' (reachability.py:188-195). _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py:3-14; bts_sbom/reachability.py:188-224)_  
  근거: These features exist and work when exercised via CallGraph.from_source() (confirmed virtual_dispatch/points_to wiring in code), but bts_sbom.reachability._build_graph_from_sources only calls the bare CallGraph(all_fns) constructor (reachability.py:224), which never runs _scan_cpp_classes/_build_virtual_dispatch/PointsToAnalysis. Executed C++ virtual-dispatch test confirms the adjacency used for BFS has no dispatch edge at all (unresolved_callees=['handle']); the sophisticated resolution never influences a verdict in this slice.
- **[fails]** README implementation table: reachability.py 'Tests: pkg suite'. _(출처: README.md:129)_  
  근거: grep across tests/ for ReachabilityEngine/enrich_sca_vulns/CallGraph/PointsToAnalysis/parse_functions finds exactly one hit: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py:90, which only asserts the names are importable/callable (part of a generic 'import surface' test). No test ever calls .analyze(), parses a file, or exercises BFS/collision/callback logic; callgraph.py and points_to.py have zero references anywhere in tests/.
- **[partially]** README: '`reachability` ... with graph-completeness evidence attached to every verdict' (README.md:110-111) and reachability.py ReachabilityResult docstring (graph_completeness/root_completeness fields). _(출처: README.md:110-111; bts_sbom/reachability.py:111-128)_  
  근거: Evidence is attached to every result object (confirmed in all test runs: graph_completeness/root_completeness/build_model/provenance always populated). However the evidence is advisory-only for REACHABLE: as shown by the shadowing and #ifdef counterexamples, a REACHABLE verdict is asserted unconditionally whenever BFS finds a path, with no check of unresolved_callees/collisions/parse-gap indicators beyond the single same-name-collision check — the attached completeness evidence does not gate or qualify REACHABLE the way it gates UNREACHABLE.

### core-vex-supply

- **[fails]** "a match must be a genuine near-miss ... with a minimum name length so short names don't create noise" (supply_chain.py docstring lines 17-18) _(출처: bts_sbom/supply_chain.py module docstring)_  
  근거: MIN_LEN=4 is applied only to the candidate name, not to entries in the popular-name seed sets. _POPULAR_CRATES contains 3-letter entries ('nom','log','aes'); analyze_one('logs','cargo') and analyze_one('nomp','cargo') are both flagged as typosquats of these short, unrelated-in-intent names (confirmed by execution) -- exactly the 'noise' the docstring says MIN_LEN prevents.
- **[partially]** "VexIndex ... only not_affected and fixed statements suppress a finding; affected and under_investigation never do" (vex.py docstring lines 373-376) _(출처: bts_sbom/vex.py VexIndex class docstring)_  
  근거: The status-gating itself holds (_SUPPRESSING = {not_affected, fixed}, enforced in is_suppressed/apply_to_findings). However the implied guarantee that suppression only applies to the product the statement names does not hold: the product-matching bug (see defect at vex.py:394) lets a not_affected statement for one package version suppress a finding for a different version of the same package, so a 'this statement suppresses' decision can be correct on status but wrong on product scope.

### core-licenses

- **[fails]** license_categories.py docstring: 'Here we classify each canonical SPDX id into ONE category...' positioned as the single source of truth replacing the prior overlapping knowledge bases, explicitly including 'license_utils._LICENSE_MAP'. _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/license_categories.py lines 6-15 (module docstring))_  
  근거: Executed: license_categories.category('CC-BY-SA-4.0')='strong-copyleft' but license_utils.classify_license('CC-BY-SA-4.0')='Weak Copyleft'; category(atom.ident) for 'GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0' = strong-copyleft while license_utils.classify_license on the same string = 'Weak Copyleft'. Grep shows license_utils._LICENSE_MAP is still live: sca_report.py (lines 36,70-71,410,416) and osvscanner.py (line 1243) call license_utils.classify_license/analyze_licenses/compute_compliance_risk_score directly; license_categories is reached only via license_norm.py. The 'replaced' knowledge base is still the dominant production path and disagrees with its replacement.
- **[fails]** bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py docstring: 'License fields are canonicalized through common.license_norm... an unresolved license is emitted as NOASSERTION — never guessed' and 'Validity is proven in benchmark/spdx_sbom by round-tripping the output through the official spdx-tools parser + full validator.' _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py lines 2-9 (module docstring))_  
  근거: Executed: license_norm.to_spdx_field('BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)') returns 'BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT' (missing closing paren) which fails to re-parse as valid SPDX ('unexpected end of expression'); this value is written straight into spdx_sbom.py's licenseDeclared field (line 121) with no validator in the call path (sca_report.py line 461 calls build_spdx and emits the doc with no validate_spdx call). `find . -ipath '*benchmark*spdx*'` found no such directory anywhere in the repository, so the claimed round-trip benchmark does not exist in this codebase to have caught it.
- **[partially]** license_norm.py module docstring: preserves 'status vocabulary (ok\|deprecated\|invalid\|none)' and flags 'DEPRECATED SPDX ids (e.g. GPL-2.0 -> GPL-2.0-only) via an explicit rename table.' _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py lines 6-19 (module docstring) and _DEPRECATED_RENAMES table lines 44-62)_  
  근거: Executed: normalize('GPL-2.0') correctly returns status='deprecated', normalized='GPL-2.0-only'. But normalize() on 5 of 6 sampled table entries whose canonical rename target is not all-uppercase ('wxWindows','bzip2-1.0.5','StandardML-NJ','Nunit','BSD-2-Clause-FreeBSD') all returned status='ok' (undetected), because the check compares the tokenizer's forcibly-uppercased atom.ident against the dict's mixed-case keys.
- **[partially]** license_scan.py module docstring: 'Honesty: a file with no recognizable license text yields NO license finding — it is counted as "no license detected", never guessed.' _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/license_scan.py lines 18-20 (module docstring))_  
  근거: Executed: a genuine UTF-16-encoded LICENSE file containing real MIT license text produces zero findings (scan_file returns {'findings': [], 'copyrights': []}), because _is_binary() (NUL-byte heuristic over the first 8192 bytes) returns True for UTF-16 text. The premise 'no recognizable license text' is false here -- the text is present and recognizable once decoded -- yet the result is indistinguishable from a file that truly has no license text, and no truncation/skip note is raised for this specific case.
- **[fails]** docs/CORE_ISSUES.md Issue #2 documents the `.strip("()")` outer-parenthesis-stripping defect as confined to `bts_sbom.sbom._valid_expression`, with an BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 workaround keeping results correct 'meanwhile'. _(출처: docs/CORE_ISSUES.md lines 10-17)_  
  근거: Executed: the identical `expression_string(node).strip("()")` defect exists independently in bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py line 84 (normalize()), a different function in a different module than the one CORE_ISSUES.md names. sbomplus never imports license_norm (grep found no match), so the documented 'BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 workaround... so results stay correct meanwhile' does not cover this second, undocumented instance that feeds bts_sbom's own spdx_sbom.py output.
- **[fails]** license_utils.build_compat_matrix() docstring: implies a complete pairwise grid ('Unknown ↔ anything returns None', checks 'known incompatible pairs') built from the same _INCOMPATIBLE_PAIRS table used by analyze_licenses(). _(출처: bts_sbom/_internal/license_utils.py lines 780-793 (function docstring) and 819-827 (single-license-flag branch))_  
  근거: Executed: build_compat_matrix(['SSPL-1.0','MIT']) and build_compat_matrix(['CC-BY-NC-4.0','Apache-2.0']) both return matrix=[[True,True],[True,True]] and incompatible_pairs=[]. The `not set_b` branch (single-license-flag entries: SSPL-1.0, CC-BY-NC family, PolyForm-NonCommercial, CERN-OHL-S/TAPR-OHL) always hits an unconditional `continue` regardless of its own if-check, so these entries can never set found_incompat; analyze_licenses() (same file, lines 627-637) handles the identical table correctly, so the two functions diverge on the exact same data.

### plus-core-adapter

- **[fails]** engine.py docstring: 'tests/test_engine_contract.py pins the contract' (so a core upgrade only needs this file re-verified) _(출처: sbomplus/engine.py:4)_  
  근거: No file named test_engine_contract.py exists anywhere in the repository (find/grep over tests/ returns nothing). The adapter's regression safety net is only exercised indirectly by tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py and test_engine_licenses.py, which do not claim to be 'the contract'.
- **[fails]** net.py docstring: 'Every outbound call in BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 goes through here' (centralised User-Agent, 429/5xx backoff, request/failure counting) _(출처: sbomplus/net.py:3-4)_  
  근거: sbomplus/llm.py implements its own independent urllib.request-based POST client (llm.py:148-159) with its own retry (1 retry on 429/5xx), its own usage/error counters (self.usage, self.errors), and its own TRANSPORT hook -- it never calls net.http_json/http_text and is not counted in net.STATS. tests/sbomplus/conftest.py itself patches net.TRANSPORT and llm.TRANSPORT separately, confirming the two paths are independent. The claim holds only for osv.py and licenses.py, which do use net.py.
- **[partially]** workspace.py module docstring: 'Cloned code is untrusted data: ... symlinks are not materialised ... nothing in the clone is ever executed' (applies to the isolated work copy in general) _(출처: sbomplus/workspace.py:5-6)_  
  근거: True for the git-clone path (core.symlinks=false makes git write symlinks as plain text). False for the local-folder ingestion path: prepare() calls shutil.copytree(p, dest, ignore=_IGNORE, symlinks=True) (workspace.py:121), which preserves real OS symlinks. Verified experimentally: a symlink inside a local source folder pointing to a file outside that folder is copied as a live symlink into the work copy, and reading the copied path returns the external file's contents.
- **[fails]** osv.py query_batch docstring: '[] = answered, none known; None = the query FAILED (never read as clean)' _(출처: sbomplus/osv.py:21)_  
  근거: osv.py:33-38: when a coordinate's batch answer carries next_page_token and the completeness follow-up to /query fails (returns non-dict), the code keeps the partial id list from the batch call (line 37, else-branch of the conditional expression) and stores it at out[c] (line 38) -- not None -- even though it is known to be incomplete. tests/sbomplus/conftest.py's FakeWorld never sets next_page_token, so this path is untested.
- **[partially]** docs/CORE_ISSUES.md #3: 'BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 ignores go.sum-only entries when a sibling go.mod exists' (go.sum MVS filter) _(출처: docs/CORE_ISSUES.md:25-29)_  
  근거: True for the straightforward case covered by tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py::test_go_sum_only_version_is_not_an_installed_package. But _go_selected() (engine.py:171-179) parses only `require` lines and ignores `replace` directives; a module whose actual build version comes from a `replace X => Y vZ` directive is never in the parsed set, so its real go.sum entry gets wrongly dropped as go_sum-only (verified experimentally).

### plus-licenses-sbom-sectors

- **[partially]** 'Resolution chain per component (first hit wins ...): 1. lockfile 2. deps.dev 3. registry:* (only when deps.dev has no answer)' (licenses.py module docstring) _(출처: sbomplus/licenses.py docstring)_  
  근거: lockfile/cache ordering matches exactly; but look() (lines 451-462) does not stop at deps.dev's first response — if deps.dev answers with evidence that fails to normalize, it keeps the raw value aside and still tries registry, picking registry only if deps.dev's own evidence was unreadable. So the real rule is 'first READABLE hit', slightly stronger/different than the literal 'first hit wins' wording; not a bug, but the docstring understates the behaviour.
- **[fails]** sbom.spdx()'s CORE_ISSUES#2 workaround 'restores verbatim' the original expression because it came from sbomplus's own structure-preserving parser (sbom.py comment, line 158; SP-AC-47) _(출처: sbomplus/sbom.py comment + specs/sbomplus_features.md SP-AC-47 + tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py::test_parentheses_survive_and_spdx_validates)_  
  근거: Holds for the one nesting direction the existing test covers ('A AND (B OR C)'); fails for symmetric both-sided nesting ('(A OR B) AND (C OR D)' and its OR/AND mirror) because the restoration guard (cur.count('(')!=cur.count(')')) cannot see corruption when both ends lose the same number of characters. Reproduced live (see defect above).
- **[partially]** 'every vulnerability affects reference must resolve to a component bom-ref (checked by validate_cyclonedx)' (sbom.py module docstring honesty rule) _(출처: sbomplus/sbom.py docstring)_  
  근거: validate_cyclonedx does confirm every 'affects' ref is a KNOWN bom-ref (test_validator_catches_dangling_reference), but 'known' does not mean 'correct': under the purl-less cross-ecosystem collision defect above, the ref resolves to a real bom-ref that belongs to a different, unrelated component. Reproduced live.
- **[fails]** CycloneDX 1.5 / SPDX 2.3 outputs are 'valid' per their respective validators, implying the validators are strict enough to back that claim _(출처: sbomplus/report.py ('CycloneDX valid=%s, SPDX valid=%s'), sbomplus/batch.py cyclonedx_valid_rate/spdx_valid_rate)_  
  근거: validate_spdx re-parses license expressions through bts_sbom's real grammar and correctly rejects invalid ones; validate_cyclonedx never parses the 'expression' string at all, so an arbitrary invalid SPDX expression is reported valid=0 errors (reproduced live). The two 'valid' flags therefore certify different things while being presented side by side as comparable metrics.
- **[fails]** Project-wide honesty discipline: 'absent evidence is never a pass' / VEX is fail-closed, not_affected needs a valid justification (task framing, mirrored by bts_sbom/vex.py's own tested rule and sectors.py's own _HONESTY string: 'a requirement whose evidence is absent is not_evidence - never ... a fabricated pass') _(출처: sbomplus/sectors.py _HONESTY string; bts_sbom/vex.py; tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py::test_vex_fail_closed_on_bare_not_affected)_  
  근거: True for literally-absent inputs (no VEX document, no eol dict, 0 components all correctly yield not_evidence, per test_maritime_without_vex_or_eol_is_not_evidence). But maritime_report's MAR-05 treats a present-but-unjustified not_affected statement as valid evidence that closes a critical/high finding -> a 'pass' is produced from evidence that the rest of the project (bts_sbom/vex.py) explicitly considers invalid/absent. Reproduced live.
- **[partially]** sbomplus/sectors.py avoids duplicating bts_sbom/sectors.py's regulatory logic _(출처: task framing comparison request)_  
  근거: 6 of 7 industries are a clean delegation with essentially no duplicated logic (good design). The 7th, maritime_report(), is new logic (the core has no maritime profile, so it isn't 'duplicated' from the core) but it reimplements the same kind of VEX-closing check pattern the project already solved once, correctly, in bts_sbom/vex.py, and reimplements it without the fail-closed justification check -- plus a schema-tag / missing-'industry'-key inconsistency versus the core-delegated reports (see low-severity defect above).

### plus-patch-deliver

- **[partially]** deliver.py docstring + SBOMPLUS.md honesty contract: "원본은 바뀌지 않는다(트리 해시로 증명). push, 원본 폴더 쓰기, PR 은 각각 명시 플래그가 있어야만 한다" (the origin is never changed; push / writing the origin folder / PR each need their own explicit flag) and "Outward-facing steps (inplace, github, PR) are never taken implicitly". _(출처: sbomplus/deliver.py lines 1-9; docs/SBOMPLUS.md '정직 계약' section)_  
  근거: Holds for deliver.py's own gating (confirmed by test_inplace_and_github_need_explicit_flags and by reading: target=='inplace'/'github' do check allow_origin_write/allow_push before touching origin/remote). But patch.apply() itself (which runs before deliver() is ever called) performs its file write with zero path-containment check on plan['files'] (patch.py:332,372); executing apply() with a files entry containing '../' writes outside the work-copy root with no flag at all (confirmed, see t4.py experiment). If root and origin are related by a predictable relative path (exactly the layout sbomplus's own code and tests use: workspace.prepare(repo, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(repo), 'work'))), this can reach the origin folder without allow_origin_write ever being checked, and deliver.py's inplace target additionally runs 'git apply --unsafe-paths' (deliver.py:71,77), which deliberately disables git's own last-line-of-defense against a path-escaping patch.
- **[partially]** apply()'s own 'digest_mismatch' note text: 'approval token does not match the current plan (plan changed since it was reviewed)', implying the digest is a meaningful commitment to 'the plan that was reviewed'. _(출처: sbomplus/patch.py line 318)_  
  근거: approval_digest() (patch.py:300-303) hashes only (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version); it ignores plan['files'] and plan['verification']. Confirmed by execution (t3.py): two plans with identical digest-relevant fields but different 'files' (different manifest targets) and different verification.clean states produce byte-identical digests, so a token approved against one plan state will silently pass against a materially different one.
- **[partially]** SP-AC-18 / pipeline.py comment: 'If a re-scan after patching shows more vulnerabilities, the system shall reset the work copy to the pre-patch commit and report regression_reverted.' _(출처: specs/sbomplus_features.md SP-AC-18; sbomplus/pipeline.py lines 180-184)_  
  근거: Holds in the tested scenario (test_regression_after_patch_is_reverted passes, commit and file content are restored). But pipeline._patch_loop (line 181) calls patchmod.revert(path, base) and discards its boolean return value before unconditionally setting status='regression_reverted' (line 182); if the underlying 'git reset --hard' fails (e.g. no .git directory, a locked file, or an invalid base commit), the pipeline still reports the revert as having happened. Confirmed by reading only; not exercised by any test with a failing revert().

### plus-arch

- **[partially]** arch.py docstring (line 10): "Honesty: every analysis that cannot run (no .git, unsupported language) is recorded in notes; nothing is guessed." _(출처: sbomplus/arch.py:10)_  
  근거: Confirmed gaps that run but are not disclosed: Kotlin edges ARE computed via _java_edges yet graph.languages_analysed/languages_without_import_analysis report Kotlin as NOT analyzed (reverse of the claim - something that ran is reported as not run); Rust's use-based cross-module cycles and C's ambiguous/angle-include gaps get zero notes entry (unlike Ruby); a non-shallow repo whose real history exceeds max_commits is silently windowed with no note (only git-level shallow clones are flagged).
- **[partially]** SP-AC-30: hotspot ranking lists the more frequently changed file first. _(출처: specs/sbomplus_features.md:71)_  
  근거: Holds for the literal non-renamed fixture in the passing test. But in a repo where the current file is the result of a `git mv` or has a non-ASCII name, _git_history's numstat parser fails to match the renamed/quoted path against the current file list, so real edits are dropped and the 'more frequently changed' ranking is computed over undercounted (sometimes zero) commit counts - demonstrated experimentally (file with 2 real edits reported with 0 commits, entirely absent from hotspots).
- **[partially]** Module docstring line 9: 'runtime_eol runtime/toolchain versions found in manifests vs end-of-life data (online when allowed)' implies a meaningful EOL assessment. _(출처: sbomplus/arch.py:9,413-442)_  
  근거: Both pipeline.run_project (arch_online default False, pipeline.py:49) and the `arch` CLI subcommand (--arch-online defaults False, cli.py:55) leave online lookups OFF by default, so in normal use the module never calls endoflife.date and relies solely on the embedded _EOL_STATIC table, which is itself sparse (e.g. "go": {} is completely empty - any Go version is always reported 'unknown') and carries no as-of/last-updated marker, only a per-row 'embedded-table (UNVERIFIED)' string.
- **[fails]** Module docstring lines 6-7: cycle detection and layering work uniformly across the supported graph languages. _(출처: sbomplus/arch.py:6-8)_  
  근거: For Rust, only `mod` declarations (a tree, acyclic by construction) are turned into edges; `use` statements, the actual dependency mechanism, are never parsed, so experimentally a genuine mutual a.rs<->b.rs `use` dependency produces cycle_count: 0 with no disclosure, unlike the analogous Ruby gap which IS disclosed.

### plus-ai-rag-server

- **[partially]** llm.py module docstring: 'The key is never written to evidence, logs, caches or errors.' _(출처: sbomplus/llm.py line 7)_  
  근거: All raised exceptions (lines 125, 134) redact api_key via .replace(self.api_key,'***'); test_api_key_never_surfaces_in_errors confirms this for the exception text. But line 124, self.errors.append("HTTP 429: " + text[:160]), stores the raw provider body with NO redaction, unlike the exception built two lines later from the same text. Currently nothing in the repo reads ChatClient.errors, so this is latent, not exploited, but it is the one place the stated contract isn't literally implemented.
- **[partially]** docs/SBOMPLUS.md: 'RAG 신뢰도 게이트가 정상 질문의 67%를 "모름" 처리... 절대 BM25 임계값... test 셋 응답률 0.333 -> 0.833' / rag.py comment: 'Threshold chosen on golden+dev, verified on the untouched test set.' _(출처: docs/SBOMPLUS.md line 66; sbomplus/rag.py lines 173-176)_  
  근거: Executed: tokenize('해사 분야 규정') yields {'해사':2,'분야':2,'규정':2} - every 2-character Hangul token (and every 3-char-stem-after-1-char-particle-stripping token) is counted twice by _hangul_tokens (rag.py:51-59), systematically inflating BM25 term frequency for very common Korean words. The min_bm25=11.0 threshold and the recall/mrr/ndcg/found_rate figures the tests assert on (all 17 tests in test_ai_rag_loop.py currently pass) were necessarily measured against this already-inflated scorer, so the 'measured on golden/dev/test' claim is true as a description of the tuning process but the underlying signal is not a clean lexical-overlap score as the docstring implies.
- **[partially]** chat.py module docstring: 'A question that no tool matches and whose retrieval confidence is below the gate returns found=False - the bot says it does not know instead of improvising.' / specs/sbomplus_features.md SP-AC-40. _(출처: sbomplus/chat.py lines 5-8; sbomplus/chat.py lines 141-148 (_pick_project))_  
  근거: Executed: Chatbot({'demo': synthetic_project()}).ask('CVE-2018-18074 ...', project='this-project-does-not-exist') returns found=True, tools=['vuln_lookup'], a confident, specific CVE answer - sourced from the unrelated 'demo' project, with no indication the requested project id was unknown. The 'say it does not know' guarantee holds for topic-level unknowns (confirmed by evals.chat_eval and verify_claims tests) but does not cover project misidentification.
- **[fails]** specs/sbomplus_features.md SP-AC-15: 'If the approval token does not match the plan digest, the system shall apply 0 changes and report digest_mismatch.' / docstring in task framing: 'patching needs explicit approval'. _(출처: specs/sbomplus_features.md line 45; sbomplus/server.py lines 100-122 (do_POST /api/scan))_  
  근거: Executed in isolation: patch.apply(d, plans, approved=True, token=None) immediately returns status='applied' and rewrites requirements.txt with no prior digest step. server.py's /api/scan only reads source/patch/approved/use_llm from the JSON body (per its own docstring, line 3-9) and never collects or forwards an approval_token to pipeline.run_project (server.py:112-115), so pipeline._patch_loop always calls patch.apply(..., token=None) on round 1 (pipeline.py:153), which skips the 'token is not None and token != digest' check entirely (patch.py:316). A single POST {source, patch:true, approved:true} therefore applies and commits an edit with zero digest review, unlike the CLI's two-step --approve --token=<digest> flow.

- **[partially]** pipeline.py module docstring: 'a failing stage is recorded in stage_errors and the rest of the run continues, so a partial result is always visible instead of an exception that hides everything' _(출처: sbomplus/pipeline.py lines 4-6 (docstring))_  
  근거: Holds at the inter-stage granularity (verified by reading every S.run call site: analyze/licenses/sbom/arch/patch/proposals are independently isolated). Does not hold at the intra-stage granularity for the patch loop specifically: a mid-round exception inside _patch_loop discards all earlier rounds' already-applied/committed progress because the whole multi-round function is one S.run-wrapped unit with default {} (pipeline.py line 113; see defect above).
- **[fails]** docs/ARCHITECTURE.md: 'loop -> golden batch + RAG/chat eval -> gate -> accept \| rollback'; loop.py docstring: pass accepted only if no blocking metric regressed by more than delta _(출처: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md line 22; sbomplus/loop.py lines 1-8, 43-63)_  
  근거: Demonstrated by direct execution (see critical defect) that loop.gate() returns pass=True for a candidate whose components/vulns collapse to 0 relative to a healthy baseline, because those counts are not gated and the rate metrics derived from them become None (skipped) or trivially 1.0 rather than being flagged as regressed.

### lens-honesty

- **[fails]** workspace.py: 'Cloned code is untrusted data: ... symlinks are not materialised, ... nothing in the clone is ever executed.' _(출처: sbomplus/workspace.py lines 1-9 (module docstring))_  
  근거: Executed workspace.prepare() on a local folder containing a symlink: shutil.copytree(..., symlinks=True) at workspace.py:121 materialises a REAL OS symlink inside the 'isolated' work copy; reading the copied file returned content of a file located entirely outside the work copy. The claim holds only for the git-clone path (core.symlinks=false is set there), not for the documented local-folder input path.
- **[fails]** osvscanner.py: 'A package the scanner could NOT authoritatively query ... is NOT reported as clean' / THE SUBSTITUTION RULE: 'no data is never reported as no vulnerability.' _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py module docstring lines 24-35, 51-81)_  
  근거: Executed OsvScanner.scan() with a mocked OSV answer (200/empty) for a component whose ecosystem defaults to the OSV catch-all 'OSS-Fuzz' (as _parse_sbom does for any SBOM component with no purl, osvscanner.py:753,774). Result: query_status='queried_online' (an ANSWERED status), data_status='complete', vulnerability_presence='none', gate_exit_code()==0, and the table renderer prints 'No known vulnerabilities found (all components authoritatively queried)' -- the exact false-clean pass the docstring says cannot happen.
- **[fails]** reachability.py: 'An UNKNOWN verdict is never converted into a safe/downgraded state here' / verdicts are 'never risk-downgraded'. _(출처: bts_sbom/reachability.py lines 24-25, 587)_  
  근거: Executed enrich_sca_vulns() with two vuln dicts sharing one CVE id across two different packages, one genuinely REACHABLE (proven call path from main()) and one not. by_cve={r.cve_id:r for r in results} at reachability.py:591 collapses both ReachabilityResult objects under the shared key; both vulns' entries ended up REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN, silently discarding the proven REACHABLE verdict for the first package.
- **[fails]** vex.py load_vex_file(): 'Raises VexError on a spec violation (fail-closed -- a malformed VEX suppresses nothing).' CLI: '--check FILE' is described as validating a VEX file. _(출처: bts_sbom/vex.py lines 552-561 (load_vex_file docstring); bts_sbom/cli.py:309)_  
  근거: Executed load_vex_file() on a CycloneDX VEX document asserting analysis.state='not_affected' with no justification and no detail. _parse_cyclonedx_vex() (vex.py:514-549) never calls _validate_statement; the function returned a VexStatement(status='not_affected', justification=None, impact_statement=None) with no error raised -- exactly the 'bare not_affected' the module elsewhere says must be rejected.
- **[partially]** SBOMPLUS.md honesty contract: 'absent evidence is never a pass', applied project-wide including the loop's own regression gate. _(출처: docs/SBOMPLUS.md lines 95-100; sbomplus/loop.py gate() docstring line 44)_  
  근거: Executed loop.gate(base, cand) where cand simply omits two quality metrics (citation_accuracy, rag_recall_at_5) that existed in base. gate() skips any metric where b is None or c is None (loop.py:49-50) -- result: pass=True, decision='accept', with no indication the metric was never measured this pass. Holds for the scanner's documented not_evidence vocabulary; fails for the loop's own self-regression gate, which is itself inside the honesty-discipline scope the docs claim covers everything.
- **[fails]** osvscanner.py docstring: sca_gate_check() is a 'Drop-in for release_gate(extra_checks=[...])'. _(출처: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py line 346)_  
  근거: grep -rn 'release_gate' over the whole repository only matches this one docstring reference; no `release_gate` function/definition exists anywhere in the repository (likely a leftover from the 'vendored from sca_guard' origin noted at the top of the file).

### lens-security

- **[partially]** workspace.py docstring: 'Cloned code is untrusted data: hooks are disabled, submodules are not fetched, symlinks are not materialised, ext:: style transports are refused, and nothing in the clone is ever executed.' _(출처: sbomplus/workspace.py lines 1-9)_  
  근거: True for the git-clone path: core.hooksPath points at an empty temp dir, --no-recurse-submodules, core.symlinks=false, ext::/fd::/http://leading-dash refused (workspace.py:41,99-105), and no code anywhere executes a file from the work copy (repo-wide grep for subprocess/eval/exec found none touching work-copy content). False for the 'local' source kind: prepare() uses shutil.copytree(p, dest, ignore=_IGNORE, symlinks=True) (workspace.py:121), which preserves real symlinks. Reproduced live: os.symlink()+shutil.copytree(symlinks=True) in a scratch temp dir produced a copy whose symlink, when opened, transparently returned the content of the file outside the copy.
- **[partially]** llm.py docstring: 'The key is never written to evidence, logs, caches or errors.' _(출처: sbomplus/llm.py lines 1-13, 124-134, 161-164)_  
  근거: Every path that is actually surfaced (raised LLMError/LLMUnavailable messages, describe()) redacts/omits the key. But llm.py:124 appends raw, unredacted provider-response text to self.errors (unlike the redacted raise two lines later at 125 and at 134); a repo-wide grep shows nothing currently reads ChatClient.errors back into output, so the contradiction is latent, not actively exploited today.
- **[partially]** ai.py: 'a proposal must pass all four [validation] layers, otherwise it is dropped'; chat.py: 'only claims whose quotes survive verification are kept' (i.e. grounded, hallucination-resistant answers) _(출처: sbomplus/ai.py lines 1-13, 154-201; sbomplus/chat.py lines 1-9, 30-32)_  
  근거: The described mechanics (schema/rule/semantic/cross-field checks on refs; verbatim-substring check on quote) are implemented exactly as documented. But they validate textual PROVENANCE, not factual correctness, and never check the free-text rationale/title (ai.py) or answer (chat.py). Reproduced live: a package.json dependency key containing an invented 'CVE-2024-9999 ... Verdict: COMPLIANT' sentence is parsed by bts_sbom with zero validation, rendered verbatim by chat.py's _component(), and chat.verify_claims() reports it 'verified' with accuracy 1.0 - the grounding guarantee is defeated whenever the attacker controls the underlying evidence text, which it does here.

### lens-architecture

- **[fails]** Implicit packaging claim: bts_sbom/pyproject.toml correctly packages the standalone core for its stated 'future public repo root' split. _(출처: bts_sbom/pyproject.toml:1,7,34-37)_  
  근거: bts_sbom/pyproject.toml:7 declares version "0.1.0" while the actual code (bts_sbom/__init__.py:59) and the root pyproject.toml both say "0.1.1" — building from this file today would ship a mislabeled, stale-versioned distribution, the same class of drift the project itself documents as dangerous in docs/CORE_ISSUES.md #4 for a different (external) copy.

## 부록 F. 분석 영역별 요약

### core-sbom-sector — bts_sbom: SBOM builders, sector engine, NTIA, CLI

**목적**: This slice is the public, stdlib-only entry surface of bts_sbom: the CLI (cli.py/__main__.py), the package's re-export surface (__init__.py), the two public SPDX 2.3 builder functions plus validator in sbom.py, the 6-sector regulatory report engine (sectors.py) and the NTIA minimum-elements scorer it reuses (ntia_check.py), and the small shared helpers (models, supplier_resolve, timeutil, pathutil) that feed them. Together they turn a manifest/lockfile or a resolver dependency graph into a validated SPDX 2.3 document, and turn an SBOM (+ optional VEX/EOL) into a per-sector pass/gap/not_evidence compliance report with inline evidence.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/__init__.py`: Public package API: re-exports sbom/vex/osvscanner/sca_report/supply_chain/reachability/sectors symbols and __version__.
- `bts_sbom/__main__.py`: `python -m bts_sbom` entry point, delegates to cli.main().
- `bts_sbom/cli.py`: argparse CLI: sbom/sector/scan/vex/policy/selfsbom subcommands, JSON load/emit helpers, exit-code contract (0/1/2).
- `bts_sbom/sbom.py`: Public SPDX 2.3 builder API: build_spdx_document, sbom_from_manifest, sbom_from_resolver_graph, validate_spdx, and the SPDX license-expression validity glue (_valid_expression).
- `bts_sbom/_internal/sbom.py`: CycloneDX 1.6 builder (deterministic, version-aware bom-ref dedup); consumed by bts_sbom/sca_report.py, outside this slice.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py`: A second, independent SPDX 2.3 builder with a single explicit root-package convention and license_norm-based license handling; consumed by sca_report.py's embedded 'spdx_sbom' output.
- `bts_sbom/sectors.py`: 6-sector (cra/fda/r155/dod/finance/robot) regulatory report engine: per-requirement check functions, SECTOR_PROFILES data, sector_report(), write_evidence().
- `bts_sbom/_internal/ntia_check.py`: NTIA minimum-elements scorer for SPDX/CycloneDX documents; reused by sectors.py for ntia_minimum/supplier_coverage/supplier_and_version checks.
