- `bts_sbom/_internal/models.py`: Stdlib dataclass replacements (DetectedPackage, CveVulnerability) for the platform's pydantic state models; pure data, no logic.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/supplier_resolve.py`: Curated (ecosystem, namespace) -> organisation supplier resolution with an explicit provenance basis (declared/curated-table/namespace-derived).
- `bts_sbom/_internal/timeutil.py`: UTC time helpers that reproduce the pre-3.12 naive-datetime.utcnow() wire format.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/pathutil.py`: Cross-platform relative-path normalization shared by manifest parsers (backslash/slash, './' prefix, duplicate-slash collapsing).
- `bts_sbom/README.md`: User-facing claims: zero deps, platform support matrix, 6-sector matrix, honesty discipline.
- `bts_sbom/CHANGELOG.md`: Version history / claimed feature list for 0.1.0 and 0.1.1.

**데이터 흐름**: cli.py parses argv and dispatches to a cmd_* handler. cmd_sbom calls sbom_from_manifest -> _internal.manifests.parse_file -> build_spdx_document (bts_sbom/sbom.py), then re-checks the result with validate_spdx before printing. cmd_sector loads --sbom/--vex/--eol JSON via cli._load_json (plain utf-8) and calls sectors.sector_report(), which builds a ctx dict (format, flattened components, and ntia_check.evaluate(sbom)) and runs each sector's check functions against it, returning pass/gap/not_evidence rows with inline evidence; --evidence additionally persists via sectors.write_evidence(). A separate, parallel SBOM path (bts_sbom.sbom.sbom_from_resolver_graph) builds the same SPDX shape from a dependency-resolver node/edge graph, optionally calling supplier_resolve.resolve() per node, and feeds into the same build_spdx_document — this is the path where the resolver-graph-specific defects below live. _internal/spdx_sbom.py and _internal/sbom.py are a second, independent pair of builders (SPDX/CycloneDX) reached only through sca_report.py, outside this slice but sharing the same ntia_check/supplier_resolve/pathutil helpers. All JSON output goes through cli._emit (json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=False) then print or utf-8 file write).

**강점**:
- The not_evidence/absent-input discipline is genuinely implemented (not just claimed) for the cases exercised: missing VEX/eol/empty-component-list paths in sectors.py correctly return not_evidence rather than pass or a fabricated gap.
- validate_spdx provides a real, if partial, self-check and (ironically) is the thing that actually catches the _valid_expression corruption when both are exercised together.
- The CycloneDX builder's bom_ref-based dedup (bts_sbom/_internal/sbom.py) is version-aware and does not share the SPDX resolver-graph builder's name-only dedup defect.
- supplier_resolve.py is a thoughtful, pure-function design that records provenance basis (declared/curated-table/namespace-derived) rather than silently guessing a supplier.
- Sector requirement rows are genuinely traceable: each carries its regulation 'basis' string and a measured 'evidence' dict (percentages/counts), not just a boolean.
- pathutil.norm_rel_path/rel_suffix_matches correctly handle the common single-backslash and single-duplicate-slash cross-platform cases that the test suite exercises.

**설계상 약점**:
- Two independent, structurally different SPDX 2.3 builders exist (bts_sbom/sbom.py's multi-root build_spdx_document vs bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py's single-explicit-root build_spdx) with no shared invariant enforced; the NTIA 'described product' exclusion heuristic was written for the single-root shape and silently misbehaves against the multi-root one.
- validate_spdx is described by its own docstring as 'a local judge, not a substitute for a certified validator' and is not wired into every document-producing path (_internal/spdx_sbom.py's output is never validated anywhere in this repository).
- The sector-check verdict vocabulary (pass/gap/not_evidence) has no partial/warn state, so a check like ntia_minimum collapses NTIA's own richer PASS/WARN/FAIL per-element grading into a binary 'no FAIL -> pass', losing information the underlying checker already computed.
- Console/stdout encoding safety (cp949) is handled entirely by an external environment variable (PYTHONUTF8=1 in CI) rather than defended in code, even though the project's own CORE_ISSUES.md records a working in-house fix (ASCII-only text + errors='replace') that was never ported into this package.
- Substring-based text matching (eol/eos token scan in sectors.py, used to fall back when no explicit --eol input is given) has no word-boundary protection, making it fragile to unrelated property names that happen to contain the trigram.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py (633 lines) is the only test file exercising this slice (tests/sbomplus/* targets the separate sbomplus layer). It covers: CLI --help/--version/bare-invocation, usage-error exit code 2, a CRA sector round-trip plus a smoke pass over all 6 sectors, the absent-VEX/eol -> not_evidence honesty invariant, SBOM build from a minimal requirements.txt lockfile (asserting validate_spdx(doc)==[]), an unsupported-manifest honest failure, evidence write round-trip, and pathutil's single-duplicate-slash case. Confirmed via grep: there is zero test coverage anywhere in tests/ for sbom_from_resolver_graph, supplier_resolve.py, _valid_expression/nested-parenthesis license expressions, _check_ntia_minimum's partial-compliance 'pass' behavior, _spdx_described_products' root-exclusion logic, _embedded_eol's token matching, or BOM-prefixed --sbom/--vex/--eol/--findings/--policy inputs (only lockfile BOM handling is tested). The cp949 console-encoding regression is handled only by setting PYTHONUTF8=1 in CI, not asserted by any test that forces a non-UTF-8 stdout encoding.

**열린 질문**:
- Is sbom_from_resolver_graph (bts_sbom.sbom) actually invoked by sbomplus/engine.py or any other real resolver-graph producer in this repo, making the name-only dedup / relationship-misattribution defect live in production rather than theoretical?
- Is the single-root-vs-multi-root documentDescribes convention difference between bts_sbom/sbom.py and bts_sbom/_internal/spdx_sbom.py an intentional two-use-case split, or an unnoticed drift from a shared vendored origin that the NTIA checker's _spdx_described_products was only ever written against?
- Was the 'benchmark/spdx_sbom' validation referenced in _internal/spdx_sbom.py's docstring ever part of this OSS extraction, or does it only exist in the internal platform repository (in which case the docstring claim should be qualified for this package)?
- Is _check_ntia_minimum's 'no FAIL means pass' bar a deliberate, if undocumented, choice (sector-level pass is meant to be looser than a full NTIA grade), or should it require fail_count==0 AND warn_count==0 (or a score threshold) to match its own title?

### core-scanner — bts_sbom: OSV scanner pipeline, SARIF, SCA report

**목적**: This slice is bts_sbom's OSV-Scanner-parity SCA engine: osvscanner.py discovers dependency manifests/lockfiles/SBOMs, queries OSV.dev (or an offline sqlite DB) per component, computes CVSS v3.1/v4.0 severity, groups aliased advisories, and renders results while enforcing a strict 'no data is never reported as no vulnerability' completeness contract with CI-gate exit codes. sca_report.py layers a full composition report (SBOM + license + EPSS/KEV + supply-chain + VEX-status) on top of OsvScanner. _internal/sarif.py exports both MISRA and SCA/CVE findings as SARIF 2.1.0 for GitHub/GitLab/IDE ingestion.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py`: Core scanner: manifest/lockfile/SBOM discovery (discover/_packages_from/_parse_sbom), OSV.dev querying with offline/online degrade (_query_package/_prefetch), CVSS v3.1 and v4.0 base-score math, alias grouping, the completeness/data_status/vulnerability_presence honesty contract (_completeness_block, gate_exit_code, sca_gate_check), guided-remediation hookup, and table/json/sarif/markdown/vertical rendering.
- `bts_sbom/sca_report.py`: End-to-end composition report: wraps OsvScanner + license/deps.dev/EPSS-KEV/supply-chain/NTIA enrichments into one build_report() call, derives per-finding VEX status, and renders a combined markdown report.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/sarif.py`: SARIF 2.1.0 document builders: build_sarif (MISRA/static-analysis findings), build_sca_sarif (CVE/OSV findings, reused by OsvScanner.render('sarif')), build_combined_sarif (multi-run merge), plus the shared _to_uri/file-location helper.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/__init__.py`: (outside slice, read for causality) parse_file() dispatches to a per-ecosystem parser but only wraps the file read in try/except, not the parser call itself — the root cause that lets a single parser exception propagate into osvscanner.py uncaught.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py`: (outside slice, read for causality) legacy lockfileVersion-1 'walk' fallback assumes every dependency value is a dict with no validation — the concrete throw site for the confirmed crash.
- `bts_sbom/vex.py`: (outside slice, read for context) separately builds OpenVEX documents from a scan result, including its own 'UNQUERIED:*' synthetic statement IDs that sca_report.py's VEX-status code appears written to recognize but never actually sees.

**데이터 흐름**: OsvScanner.scan()/discover() walks a target (file or directory) with os.walk, routes each file through _packages_from() to either _parse_sbom() or bts_sbom._internal.manifests.parse_file(), producing package dicts; _prefetch() resolves distinct (ecosystem,name,version) keys in parallel via OsvClient or osv_local_db, then the serial loop in scan() re-queries (hitting the prefetch cache), splits withdrawn advisories, builds the native results + completeness block, and optionally calls fix_planner/license_utils. OsvScanner.render('sarif') and sca_report.py's SBOM/finding pipeline both feed CveVulnerability-shaped objects into bts_sbom._internal.sarif.build_sca_sarif. sca_report.build_report() re-discovers packages independently, re-scans via OsvScanner.scan(), and layers deps.dev/license/EPSS-KEV/supply-chain/NTIA enrichment on top, emitting its own markdown report and VEX-status-per-finding, duplicating (and in one place diverging from) osvscanner.py's own honesty disclosures.

**강점**:
- The data_status/vulnerability_presence substitution rule (no data != no vulnerability) is implemented thoroughly and correctly inside osvscanner.py itself: traced through _completeness_block, _verified_clean, gate_exit_code and sca_gate_check and found internally consistent for every pipeline stage (parse failure, unsupported manifest, version-unresolved, network failure, stale DB, dry-run).
- CVSS v3.1 base-score math (AV/AC/PR/UI/CIA weights, ISS, impact/exploitability formulas, scope-changed coefficients, and the integer-math roundup) matches the published FIRST CVSS v3.1 specification exactly, verified term-by-term.
- Withdrawn-advisory handling (_split_withdrawn) correctly keeps retracted OSV records out of the active vulnerability count while still surfacing them for VEX not_affected emission.
- Encoding handling (utf-8-sig/BOM, errors='replace') is applied consistently across every file-reading path in this slice.

**설계상 약점**:
- The honesty/completeness contract is implemented twice (osvscanner.py's own renderers and sca_report.py's separate _markdown()) with no shared helper, so the two have already drifted (version_unresolved omission) and will drift further as the contract evolves.
- No ordering normalization is applied anywhere between filesystem discovery (os.walk, OS-dependent order) and the final JSON/markdown/SARIF output, so output ordering is not reproducible across machines/filesystems even though the project clearly cares about reproducibility enough to sort source_snapshot_digest's own canonical form.
- Error handling is inconsistent: enrichment steps (lockfile_facts, EPSS/KEV, license scan, supply-chain) are all defensively wrapped in try/except with disclosed fallbacks, but the single most load-bearing call — the actual manifest/lockfile parse — is not.
- The CVSS v4.0 implementation is a partial, undocumented approximation of the spec (macrovector lookup only, no severity-distance interpolation), which is a materially different algorithm from what the docstring and FOCUS claim, not just a rounding nuance.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py never imports osvscanner.py, sca_report.py or _internal/sarif.py directly; its only coverage of this slice is indirect, through CLI smoke tests (test_cli_scan_dry_run_is_honest, test_cli_scan_remediation_block_and_flag, test_cli_sector_*, test_cli_sbom_unsupported_manifest_is_honest) that invoke `bts-sbom scan`/`sbom` as a subprocess and assert on a few substrings of stdout (e.g. 'NOT a verified clean pass', 'query_missing'). test_import_surface_and_version only asserts gate_exit_code/OsvScanner/build_report are importable/callable — it never calls them with real data or checks an actual 0/1/2 exit value. There is no test anywhere that: computes or asserts a CVSS v3.1 or v4.0 score, calls OsvScanner.render('sarif') or build_sca_sarif and checks the SARIF structure, drives build_report() directly, exercises --gate with real vulnerability/no-data scenarios, or scans a directory containing an excluded-name collision (vendors/, node_modules_backup/) or a malformed lockfile. tests/sbomplus/conftest.py explicitly stubs out OsvScanner with a fake 'reads requirements.txt (name==version)' stand-in, so the sbomplus test suite never touches this slice's real code either. Net: this slice — the largest and most mathematically/contractually sensitive file in the FOCUS — has essentially no direct unit-test coverage.

**열린 질문**:
- bts_sbom/cli.py:160-161 (`if args.gate and not args.dry_run: return gate_exit_code(result)`) means `bts-sbom scan --dry-run --gate` always exits 0 regardless of data completeness, bypassing the gate entirely in the one mode where data_status is guaranteed not 'complete' — cli.py is outside this slice's assigned files, so I flag it rather than count it as a defect here; worth another reviewer's attention since it directly interacts with osvscanner.py's gate_exit_code contract.
- Whether other ecosystem manifest parsers (cargo.py, gomod.py, rpm.py, dpkg.py, extra.py — outside this slice) ever emit a non-exact version (making 'version_unresolved' a live, frequent status rather than a rarely-reached one, since both npm.py and pip.py in this repo deliberately filter ranges before they become components) was not verified; this affects how often the sca_report.py footer gap (defect 4) actually fires in practice.
- Whether SARIF's absolute file:// artifactLocation URIs (no uriBaseId) actually cause GitHub/GitLab code-scanning uploads to silently drop location annotations was not verified against a live upload — flagged as a claim_check 'unverifiable' rather than a confirmed defect.

### core-vuln-data-version — bts_sbom: OSV client/local DB, version logic, remediation, policy, enrichment

**목적**: This slice is bts_sbom's vulnerability-data and version-math layer: it talks to OSV.dev (online and via an offline sqlite mirror), decides whether an installed version falls inside an advisory's vulnerable range under the right ecosystem ordering (PEP 440 / SemVer / Maven / Go / generic), suggests the minimal upgrade that clears a CVE, groups per-component fix plans, gates CI on a JSON policy plus VEX suppression, and enriches CVEs with EPSS/KEV, deps.dev license/metadata and npm/PyPI registry author data. It is the layer whose output decisions (affected/not-affected, fixed-version, pass/fail) the rest of the toolkit treats as ground truth.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py`: urllib-based OSV.dev client: retry/backoff POST, ecosystem name mapping, CVSS v2/v3/v4 vector->score, OsvVuln parsing incl. containing-window fixed-version selection
- `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_local_db.py`: sqlite offline mirror: exact-query cache + imported ecosystem dumps, OSV all.zip ingestion, version-range affected-check for offline/degraded lookups
- `bts_sbom/_internal/version_cmp.py`: tolerant generic version tokenizer/comparator plus format dispatch for RPM epoch, Go pseudo-version, CalVer
- `bts_sbom/_internal/version_ranges.py`: OSV range-event-to-interval conversion and ecosystem-aware (semver/PEP440/Maven) interval containment; advisory range-string parsing
- `bts_sbom/_internal/version_match.py`: specifier-string matcher (pip ~=/==/.*, npm/cargo/go ^/~/x-range) with per-constraint explain_match, documented public API
- `bts_sbom/_internal/remediation.py`: per-advisory minimal-fixed-version suggestion from OSV affected[].ranges[].events, honest no-fix rows
- `bts_sbom/_internal/fix_planner.py`: per-component fix plan aggregation (safe_version, major_bump, priority ranking); text-only manifest patch rendering
- `bts_sbom/_internal/policy.py`: JSON policy validation/evaluation (severity_min, max_severity_score, licenses_deny) and VEX-at-decision-point suppression
- `bts_sbom/_internal/epss_kev.py`: live FIRST.org EPSS + CISA KEV feed fetch, in-process KEV cache
- `bts_sbom/_internal/epss_offline.py`: curated offline EPSS/KEV snapshot table, disclosed as 'offline-table' source
- `bts_sbom/_internal/depsdev.py`: deps.dev license + release-metadata enrichment, honest unresolved accounting
- `bts_sbom/_internal/registry_meta.py`: npm/PyPI registry author + download-location enrichment, additive-only

**데이터 흐름**: osvscanner.py (outside slice) drives osv_client.post_query / osv_local_db.lookup per package and tags each result with a query_status; remediation.py and fix_planner.py then walk the same raw OSV dict shape to produce fix plans; policy.py re-derives severity from the raw advisories and optionally suppresses findings via vex.py (outside slice) before gating; epss_kev/epss_offline/depsdev/registry_meta are independent enrichment side-channels feeding sca_report.py (outside slice). version_cmp/version_ranges/version_match are pure libraries called by all of the above for ordering decisions; sbomplus/engine.py (outside slice) re-implements some of the same OSV-record logic rather than reusing remediation.py/osv_client.py, and in doing so correctly filters out GIT-type ranges where this slice's core code does not.

**강점**:
- version_cmp/version_ranges/version_match pass an extensive hand-built table of ecosystem-ordering cases I ran directly: semver build-metadata ignored, PEP 440 epoch/pre/post/dev ordering, Maven qualifier ranks, Go pseudo-version-vs-real-tag ordering, multi-window OSV events with last_affected inclusive bounds, npm caret/tilde/x-range and prerelease opt-in edge cases all matched the documented/expected ecosystem semantics.
- osv_local_db.py uses parameterized SQL everywhere (no injection surface found) and ingests OSV's all.zip purely via zf.read() into memory, never zf.extract(), so there is no zip-slip/path-traversal risk from the downloaded archive.
- policy.py's VEX fail-closed guarantee is correctly wired end-to-end: a malformed or bare not_affected statement raises in bts_sbom/vex.py and policy.vex_evaluate's except-clause leaves every finding unmatched rather than silently suppressing anything (confirmed by reading vex.py's VexError checks).
- osv_client.post_query/query_package_raw correctly separate 'OSV answered' from 'OSV unreachable' (ok flag), which is exactly what the production scanner path (osvscanner.py) relies on to avoid treating a network failure as a clean scan.
- remediation.py and fix_planner.py never write to disk; patch rendering (emit_patch/emit_requirements_patch) only returns text diffs, matching their documented 'advisory only' contract.

**설계상 약점**:
- The 'is this version inside the vulnerable range' / 'what is the fixed version' logic is independently re-implemented across osv_local_db.py, osv_client.py and remediation.py instead of funneling through one shared, GIT-range-aware helper; the same class of bug (treating a GIT range's commit-SHA events as ordinary versions) therefore recurs in three places, and sbomplus/engine.py had to write its own correct version specifically because the core does not provide one.
- EPSS/KEV enrichment (epss_kev.py) has no ok/failure signal or as-of timestamp analogous to osvscanner.py's offline-DB staleness plumbing, so a report can never disclose that its EPSS/KEV numbers came from a dead feed rather than a real measurement.
- fix_planner.py mixes two version-comparison entry points from the same version_cmp module (the epoch-stripping compare()/gt() family vs. the epoch-aware compare_versions() dispatcher) with no stated rule for which to use where; this inconsistency is the direct cause of two separate Debian/RPM-epoch defects below.
- OsvClient exposes both an honest per-package method (query_package_raw) and a dishonest one (query_package) with the same name pattern, plus a query_batch that doesn't match OSV's real batch-API contract; nothing marks the dishonest/incorrect ones as deprecated, inviting future misuse even though today's production scan path avoids them.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py has direct, fairly thorough unit tests for version_cmp/version_ranges (via version_match's PEP440 and npm/cargo/go tables, test_version_match_pep440 / test_version_match_semver_npm_cargo_go / test_version_match_fail_closed_and_explain), remediation.py (test_remediation_smallest_fixed, including last_affected-only and no-fixed-ge-current honesty cases) and policy.py (exit codes, license rule, VEX suppression). None of these exercise the npm/cargo/go '==' operator, or an OSV record whose ranges mix ECOSYSTEM and GIT types -- interestingly, tests/sbomplus/conftest.py DOES build exactly that mixed-range fixture (with a literal SHA constant) for sbomplus/engine.py's tests, but bts_sbom core's own osv_client.py/osv_local_db.py/remediation.py are never run against that same fixture shape. fix_planner.py (plan_fixes, _major, _highest/major_bump) has zero direct unit tests anywhere in the repo. osv_client.py's OsvClient class (post_query, query_package, query_batch, _parse_vulns, _cvss_score_from_vector) has no direct unit test; it is only exercised indirectly through osvscanner.py's separate _query_online/post_query path, which doesn't touch query_package/query_batch/_parse_vulns at all. osv_local_db.py's _vuln_affects/lookup_by_package/import_ecosystem_dump have no direct unit tests (only indirect --offline CLI coverage, which would not naturally construct a GIT-range record). epss_kev.py and epss_offline.py have zero tests anywhere in tests/. depsdev.py and registry_meta.py are covered indirectly, with an injectable fetcher, by tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py::test_depsdev_then_registry_provenance.

**열린 질문**:
- Is there any caller (example script, tutorial, or an unmerged CLI flag) of OsvClient.query_batch/query_package or osv_local_db.import_ecosystem_dump/fetch_ecosystems_index outside what repo-wide grep found, which would raise the real-world severity of defects #2/#7/#8/#9 above?
- Is match_range/explain_match (version_match.py) meant to be wired into the OSV-affects decision path in a future round, or is it a standalone utility for not-yet-written callers -- this determines whether the '==' bug is purely latent or will become load-bearing?
- In this project's real OSV corpus (evals/e2e sources, or live osv.dev data), how often do affected[].ranges[] entries mix an ECOSYSTEM range with a GIT range on the same package (as sbomplus's test fixture models) -- if common, the osv_local_db.py false-negative (defect #1) would fire on real advisories today, not just a constructed test case.
- Does sca_report.py's documented 'bulk-intel-db' enrichment tier (between live-api and the curated offline table) ever get consulted, or is it also bypassed by the same truthy-dict check that defeats the offline-table fallback in defect #3?

### core-manifests — bts_sbom: manifest/lockfile parsers

**목적**: This slice is bts_sbom's manifest/lockfile discovery and parsing layer: a basename-keyed dispatcher (manifests/__init__.py) plus one parser module per ecosystem (npm, pip/poetry, go, cargo/gradle raw, dpkg, rpm, and a dozen more in extra.py) that turn a project's lockfiles/manifests into DetectedPackage records (name/version/ecosystem/purl/relationship/depth), feeding the SBOM builder and the OSV vulnerability scanner. lockfile_facts.py is a second-pass enrichment layer that recovers download URLs, artifact hashes and the real dependency graph (download/hash/edges/BFS depth) for the 6 formats it understands, used only by the vulnerability-scan path.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/__init__.py`: Central registry/dispatcher: basename→parser map (_BY_NAME) + one relpath-suffix route for dpkg; parse_file() is the sole public read-a-manifest entry point used by sbom.py and osvscanner.py.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py`: package.json (direct, exact-pin only) and package-lock.json/npm-shrinkwrap.json (v1 nested tree, v2/v3 flat `packages` map) → DetectedPackage with relationship/depth.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/pip.py`: requirements.txt (pinned '==' lines only, single file, no includes) and poetry.lock (tomllib-only, full resolved tree, group/category-based direct/transitive).
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/gomod.py`: go.mod require blocks/single-line requires, direct vs `// indirect`.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/cargo.py`: RAW-dict (non-DetectedPackage) parsers for Cargo.lock/Cargo.toml and build.gradle(.kts), for other supply-chain consumers; the only module in the slice with a genuinely working tomllib-absent regex fallback for both Cargo.lock and Cargo.toml.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/dpkg.py`: dpkg var/lib/dpkg/status RFC822-stanza parser, routed by relative-path suffix; honest relationship='unknown', per-stanza fault isolation.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/rpm.py`: Standalone rpmdb reader (sqlite backend only, bdb/ndb detected-not-parsed); intentionally NOT wired into the parse_file registry — a separate rootfs-scanning API.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py`: The bulk of ecosystem breadth: Cargo.lock/toml wrappers, Gemfile.lock, composer.lock, Pipfile.lock, pom.xml, yarn.lock, NuGet packages.lock.json, go.sum, gradle.lockfile/build.gradle wrapper, pubspec.lock (stub, returns []), mix.lock, pyproject.toml (poetry+PEP621), conanfile.txt/py, vcpkg.json.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/lockfile_facts.py`: Second-pass enrichment (download location, hashes, dependency edges, BFS depth/relationship correction) for 6 formats (npm v1/v2/v3+shrinkwrap, yarn v1, Cargo.lock, poetry.lock, composer.lock); consumed only by osvscanner.py, not by sbom.py's manifest path.

**데이터 흐름**: cli.py/sbom.py/osvscanner.py call manifests.parser_for()/parse_file(), which looks up a parser by basename (or dpkg's path-suffix) and invokes it, producing DetectedPackage objects (name/version/ecosystem/purl/relationship/depth/confidence) or, for cargo.py's raw functions, plain dicts consumed elsewhere. Every parser catches its own decode exceptions internally and returns [] on failure; parse_file()'s own try/except covers only the file-read step (__init__.py:97-102), not the parser call, so each parser is independently responsible for never raising. osvscanner.py additionally runs lockfile_facts.extract()/apply() for the subset of formats it recognizes, backfilling hash/download/graph-edge/relationship fields in place before CVE matching, and separately runs its own duplicate structural probe (_probe_manifest_integrity, outside this slice) that re-decodes JSON/TOML files to catch the class of failure where a parser's blanket except-and-empty would otherwise be indistinguishable from a legitimately empty manifest — but that probe's coverage list and sbom.py's direct call path do not fully overlap with what this slice's parsers actually register (see defects/claim_checks).

**강점**:
- dpkg.py and rpm.py are the standout modules: careful RFC822/binary-format handling, stanza/record-level fault isolation confirmed by execution, honest 'unknown' relationship tagging, and explicit unsupported-format disclosure (unsupported_rpmdb_note) instead of a silent empty list.
- A consistent, well-documented 'never fabricate a version' discipline for range-only specs across cargo.py/extra.py (Cargo.toml path/git deps, pom.xml ${property}, PEP621 non-== ranges, vcpkg.json version>= minimums), each with an explicit docstring rationale.
- Cross-platform path handling (pathutil.norm_rel_path/rel_suffix_matches) and BOM/CRLF-tolerant reads are deliberate, tested fixes for real Windows/POSIX lockfile-path interop, and the existing regression test for it passes.
- lockfile_facts.py only links a dependency edge when exactly one candidate component matches (len(cands)==1), correctly refusing to guess when a name is ambiguous, honoring its own 'no name-similarity edges' rule.
- go.sum's duplicate module/go.mod-hash lines are correctly deduplicated to one entry per (name, version) via the '/go.mod' suffix strip plus a seen-set.

**설계상 약점**:
- The 'never raise' discipline is implemented ad hoc per parser via a blanket `except Exception: return []/None` around the whole-document decode, rather than a shared helper — this is what turns a CPython implementation detail (RecursionError on deep JSON/TOML nesting) into a codebase-specific silent-data-loss bug rather than a handled, disclosed limitation.
- extra.py's parse_cargo_lock duplicates cargo.py's tomllib/regex logic instead of reusing cargo.py's own (already more robust) implementation, which is how the Python-3.10 Cargo.lock gap (defect 1) was introduced while cargo.py's parallel function already works correctly.
- requirements.txt parsing is a single-pass, single-file design with no notion of -r/-c includes, extras, or PEP 440 '==='; nothing downstream can distinguish 'this file truly has only N pinned deps' from 'M lines were silently unparseable'.
- extra.py's parse_yarn_lock and lockfile_facts.py's _yarn_v1 are two independent re-implementations of the same yarn.lock v1 grammar that have drifted apart (only the latter guards against Yarn Berry); duplication without a shared source of truth invites exactly this kind of divergence.
- No manifest parser enforces an input-size or nesting-depth cap before handing the whole file to json.loads/tomllib.loads/ElementTree — acceptable for ordinary lockfiles, but is the structural reason a trivially small adversarial file can reach the interpreter's recursion limit at all.
- Gradle's modern, now-default 'version catalog' dependency style (libs.versions.toml + libs.someAlias) is entirely unparsed (only literal group:artifact:version string literals are matched); not a violation of its documented scope, but a real coverage gap for current Gradle projects.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py is the only test file touching this slice, and it exercises only 4 narrow scenarios: npm package-lock v3 nested transitive classification, Cargo.lock direct/transitive via sibling Cargo.toml (on this runtime's Python 3.12, where the Python-3.10 tomllib gap above is invisible), UTF-8-BOM tolerance for requirements.txt and a package-lock.json, and parser-routing path-normalization for dpkg's var/lib/dpkg/status suffix match. All 4 currently pass (verified by running `pytest tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py`, 26/28 passing; the 2 unrelated failures are a Windows cp949-console encoding issue in CLI help text, outside this slice). Zero tests exercise: pip.py's requirements.txt edge cases (extras/markers/hashes/-r/===), poetry.lock, gomod.py (no test at all), dpkg.py's actual stanza-parsing logic (only routing is tested, not parse_dpkg_status's output), rpm.py (zero tests anywhere in the repo), or any of extra.py's dozen other parsers (Gemfile.lock, composer.lock, Pipfile.lock, pom.xml, yarn.lock, NuGet, gradle, mix.lock, pyproject.toml/poetry+PEP621, conanfile.txt/py, vcpkg.json) or lockfile_facts.py's npm-v1/v2/yarn/cargo/poetry/composer graph-edge and roots logic. tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py does not exercise this slice despite one test name containing the word 'manifests'.

**열린 질문**:
- Does the project's own 3-OS x {3.10,3.12} CI matrix (.github/workflows/ci.yml) currently pass on the 3.10 legs, given test_cargo_lock_direct_transitive_from_sibling_manifest should fail there per defect 1 — worth having a maintainer check actual CI history for the 3.10 jobs.
- Is vcpkg.json's absence from osvscanner.py's _probe_manifest_integrity _MANIFEST_FORMAT table (bts_sbom/osvscanner.py:189-195) an oversight or intentional, given every other JSON-based manifest basename this slice registers is listed there?
- Is Yarn Berry (v2+) considered in-scope at all for bts_sbom? If so, should extra.py's parse_yarn_lock gain the same '__metadata:' detect-and-decline guard lockfile_facts.py::_yarn_v1 already has, to avoid emitting the fabricated '__metadata' package?
- Is there a reason extra.py's parse_cargo_lock does not delegate to cargo.py's already-tomllib-optional parse_cargo_lock (the way extra.py's parse_cargo_toml and parse_gradle_build do delegate to cargo.py), rather than re-implementing tomllib-only logic from scratch?

### core-reachability — bts_sbom: call graph, points-to, reachability

**목적**: This slice implements bts_sbom's C/C++ "function-level reachability" analysis: a self-contained (no external toolchain) regex+brace-matching parser (callgraph.py) that extracts functions and call sites from source text, an inclusion-based points-to/virtual-dispatch precision tier (points_to.py) wired only into CallGraph.from_source(), and reachability.py, which builds a flat call graph from the parser's output and BFS-searches from configured entry points to CVE-affected function names, classifying each vulnerability as REACHABLE, UNREACHABLE, or REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN with attached completeness evidence, per the project's "honest verdict" contract.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/reachability.py`: Public reachability API (ReachabilityEngine, enrich_sca_vulns). Builds a flat adjacency map from the parsed functions (via callgraph.CallGraph's bare constructor), computes completeness evidence (collisions, unresolved callees, callback candidates, build-model attestation), BFS-searches from configured entry points to each vulnerability's affected function names, and classifies REACHABLE / UNREACHABLE / REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py`: Self-contained regex+brace-matching C/C++ parser (no external toolchain): strips comments/strings, splits class/function bodies, extracts ParsedFunction/CallSite objects, resolves direct calls against a flat global name map (no scoping), detects address-taken/function-pointer multi-target callbacks, builds a nominal C++ class/inheritance model with override-aware virtual dispatch, and provides Tarjan SCC detection + iterative reverse-topological ordering. from_source() additionally wires in PointsToAnalysis, but reachability.py never calls from_source().
- `bts_sbom/_internal/points_to.py`: Inclusion-based (Andersen-style) flow-insensitive points-to analysis plus a secondary flow-sensitive/sequential precision tier and heap-shape/SCC summarization; iterates copy/load/store/offset constraints to a capped fixed point (max_iterations=100). Only reachable via CallGraph.from_source(), which bts_sbom.reachability never invokes.

**데이터 흐름**: source_files -> each file opened/read -> parse_functions() (regex+brace matching, no preprocessor, no scoping) -> List[ParsedFunction] per file -> CallGraph(all_fns) bare constructor populates fn.calls via _extract_calls against a single GLOBAL flat defined-name set (ignores file/scope boundaries) -> reachability.py unions edges per function name across all files into adjacency: name->set[name], plus collisions (name defined in >1 file), unresolved_callees (callee text absent from defs), and callback_candidates (address-taken ∩ defined, via a second regex pass that re-reads every file) -> ReachabilityEngine.analyze(): effective_entries = configured entry points ∩ adjacency keys; for each vuln, BFS (_reachable_from, plain list-based queue) from effective_entries to each affected-function name; REACHABLE if a path is found and neither the path nor target touches a collision; otherwise UNKNOWN if any completeness blocker exists, else UNREACHABLE -> enrich_sca_vulns merges verdict/path/detail/completeness/assumptions/unknown_reasons back into the caller's vuln dicts. CallGraph.from_source()'s virtual-dispatch/points-to precision tiers, and CallGraph.resolve()/reverse_topo()/sccs(), sit downstream of parse_functions but are never called from this data flow (confirmed by repo-wide grep: nothing outside callgraph.py/points_to.py calls from_source/resolve/points_to).

**강점**:
- Same-name collision downgrade is correctly implemented and verified by execution: when a BFS path or its target traverses a function name defined in more than one translation unit (e.g. two files each with a `static helper`), the verdict is correctly downgraded from REACHABLE to REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN rather than asserting a possibly-wrong binding.
- Every tested completeness blocker (unresolved indirect-call targets, address-taken callbacks, absent effective entry points, incomplete build model, same-name collisions, C++ virtual dispatch left unresolved) was confirmed by execution to force REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN rather than ever producing a false UNREACHABLE — the core 'UNKNOWN is never treated as safe' claim holds under all scenarios tried.
- reverse_topo() and sccs() use explicit iterative worklists/stacks (not native Python recursion) specifically to avoid recursion-limit failures on deep or mutually-recursive call graphs — a sound engineering choice, independent of whether it is on reachability.py's critical path.
- callgraph.py's nominal C++ class model (multiple-inheritance path enumeration, virtual/ambiguous-base detection, cast refinement in _receiver_types/_build_virtual_dispatch) is genuinely substantial, correct-looking engineering for a regex-only parser, even though it is currently disconnected from the one safety-critical consumer examined in this slice.

**설계상 약점**:
- Architectural disconnect: reachability.py's call graph is built with CallGraph's bare constructor, never CallGraph.from_source(), so none of callgraph.py's virtual-dispatch resolution, function-pointer multi-target resolution, or points_to.py's entire points-to analysis can ever contribute to a REACHABLE verdict — around 1,200 lines of precision-tier engineering have zero effect on the product the honesty contract governs.
- The regex/brace-matching parser has no preprocessor model (no #ifdef/#if handling) and no scoping model (parameters/locals can shadow global function names); both gaps directly violate the 'a found path is real code' assumption REACHABLE verdicts are built on, and neither gap is listed among the documented UNREACHABLE assumptions (which only mention macro-generated calls being invisible, i.e. the opposite failure direction).
- Complete/incomplete evidence (callback_candidates, unresolved_callees, files_failed) is only loosely correlated with the actual parse state: plain function-pointer assignment without '&' is invisible to callback_candidates, and a source file that parses successfully but yields zero recognizable functions (e.g. K&R-style definitions) is counted in neither files_parsed nor files_failed. The top-level complete/UNKNOWN classification still happens to be safe in the cases tested, but the attached diagnostic evidence a human reviewer would read can misstate why.
- CallGraph.__init__ has an unconditional O(functions x call_sites) cost (the constraint_joins scan) that triggers on any C/C++ input regardless of template usage, making the engine impractical on realistically large codebases (the stated target domain includes automotive/embedded firmware with many thousands of functions).

**테스트 커버리지**: Repo-wide grep (tests/ and tests/sbomplus/) shows exactly one reference to this slice's public surface: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py:90 asserts `ReachabilityEngine` and `enrich_sca_vulns` are present/callable as part of a generic "import surface" smoke test — it never instantiates ReachabilityEngine, never calls .analyze(), and exercises no C/C++ parsing. bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py and bts_sbom/_internal/points_to.py have zero references anywhere under tests/. Consequently none of the following are covered by any test in the repo: BFS path-finding, the same-name-collision downgrade, callback/address-taken detection, build_model declared-complete verification, virtual dispatch, points-to analysis, SCC/reverse-topo ordering, or any of the defects above. All verification in this review was performed by executing ad hoc scripts against the public API from a temp directory (no repo files modified).

**열린 질문**:
- Is the missing `if site.template_args:` guard around callgraph.py:544 an accidental omission (matching the pattern on lines 536 and 546) or intentional? If accidental, gating it would likely remove most of the measured quadratic blowup.
- Is there a reason _build_graph_from_sources (reachability.py) deliberately avoids CallGraph.from_source() / cg.resolve(), rather than it being an oversight — e.g. a correctness concern about from_source()'s heuristics being too permissive for the 'never relax UNKNOWN' contract? If so, is that concern documented anywhere?
- Should a call-site whose textual callee name matches the vulnerable function only through an unqualified, unscoped match (no qualifying owner/no type checking) ever be allowed to produce an unconditional REACHABLE with empty assumptions, or should name-shadowing risk be added to the UNREACHABLE/REACHABLE caveat machinery the same way same-name-collision risk already is?
- Does any downstream consumer (sbomplus/ or the VEX generator) rely on REACHABLE/UNREACHABLE from this engine to auto-generate VEX `not_affected`/`affected` statements, and if so, is it aware that REACHABLE here is not gated by graph completeness at all?
- What happens when PointsToAnalysis.solve() hits max_iterations=100 before reaching a fixed point — is there any plan to detect and surface non-convergence if/when a future caller wires points_to into the reachability verdict path?

### core-vex-supply — bts_sbom: OpenVEX and supply-chain attack detection

**목적**: bts_sbom/vex.py produces and consumes OpenVEX v0.2.0 documents: it builds honest VEX statements from OSV scan results, triaged CVE objects, and function-level reachability analysis (never asserting not_affected without real justification), validates every statement against the OpenVEX spec, indexes a parsed VEX document for suppression lookups, and can also parse/round-trip CycloneDX-embedded VEX. bts_sbom/supply_chain.py is a deterministic, offline heuristic detector (SupplyChainAnalyzer) for dependency-substitution attacks: typosquatting (edit distance, transposition, affix-injection), homoglyph/confusable-character impersonation, namespace/case confusion (npm scopes, crates.io case-sensitivity, Maven groupId:artifactId), and policy-declared dependency confusion, plus a transitive-dependency walker and a simple risk-score aggregator.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/vex.py`: OpenVEX statement/document builders (statement, build_document, from_scan_result, from_triaged, from_cve_reachability, withdrawn_statements), spec validation (_validate_statement, _is_valid_vuln_id), ingestion/indexing for suppression (VexIndex, parse_document, apply_to_findings), and CycloneDX VEX interop (load_vex_file, _parse_cyclonedx_vex, generate_openvex).
- `bts_sbom/supply_chain.py`: SupplyChainAnalyzer: per-ecosystem popular-name seed sets, _edit_distance_le (bounded Levenshtein), _confusable_fold (homoglyph folding), analyze_one/_analyze_crates/_analyze_maven detection logic, get_transitive_deps traversal, compute_risk_score aggregation.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/policy.py`: Caller: vex_evaluate() builds a VexIndex via parse_document() and calls index.status_for() per finding to drive the pass/fail policy gate; documents its own fail-closed contract for malformed VEX.
- `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py`: Caller: OsvScanner.to_openvex() drives vex.from_scan_result()/vex.withdrawn_statements() to emit a standalone OpenVEX document from a live or offline OSV scan.
- `bts_sbom/cli.py`: Caller: `vex` subcommand (--check -> load_vex_file, --from-scan -> from_scan_result) with no per-item exception handling around either call.
- `bts_sbom/sca_report.py`: Caller: uses SupplyChainAnalyzer.compute_risk_score() to fold typosquat/confusion findings into the report's risk score; does not otherwise exercise SupplyChainAnalyzer's detection paths.
- `tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py`: Covers a handful of vex.py paths (bare not_affected rejection, CLI --check roundtrip, vex_evaluate grouping, policy VEX suppression) but contains no functional test of SupplyChainAnalyzer.

**데이터 흐름**: An OSV scan result (or a list of triaged/reachability-annotated CVE objects) flows into vex.from_scan_result/from_triaged/from_cve_reachability, which build and validate OpenVEX statements and assemble a document via build_document(); this document is either emitted to disk/CLI or round-tripped back in through vex.parse_document()->VexIndex, which bts_sbom/_internal/policy.py's vex_evaluate() queries per-finding (status_for/is_suppressed) to decide the suppressed/active partition that feeds the compliance policy pass/fail verdict and sector reports. Independently, dependency names+ecosystems (from manifest/lockfile parsers elsewhere in bts_sbom) flow into SupplyChainAnalyzer.analyze()/analyze_one(), whose findings are merged into report findings and folded into a risk score by sca_report.py via compute_risk_score(); this path never touches vex.py.

**강점**:
- _edit_distance_le verified correct by fuzz-testing against a reference O(n*m) Levenshtein implementation (0 mismatches over 20,000 random trials, maxd 1-3).
- _validate_statement is applied uniformly at every production point (statement(), build_document(), generate_openvex()) and at ingestion (VexIndex.__init__), giving one enforcement point for the 'not_affected needs justification/impact_statement' rule; confirmed to actually raise via execution and by tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py::test_vex_fail_closed_on_bare_not_affected.
- from_scan_result/from_triaged/from_cve_reachability consistently refuse to downgrade unqueried/unknown-reachability packages to a clean not_affected, matching the module's stated honesty discipline (confirmed by reading).
- supply_chain.py's exact-match short-circuit (norm in pop -> None) correctly never flags a popular package against itself, and dependency-confusion is strictly opt-in via caller-declared internal_names rather than guessed.
- get_transitive_deps has an explicit depth cap and a cycle guard, so a malicious/cyclic dependency graph cannot cause unbounded recursion (see defect about its correctness trade-off, though).

**설계상 약점**:
- VexIndex's product-matching strips the version on both the statement side and the query side symmetrically, so 'no version asserted' and 'any version' become indistinguishable by construction rather than by an explicit, documented choice -- version granularity in a not_affected/fixed statement is effectively decorative.
- _is_valid_vuln_id hardcodes a CVE/GHSA/internal-prefix allowlist that is narrower than the actual OpenVEX spec (vulnerability.name is unconstrained free text) and narrower than real OSV.dev id space (e.g. MAL- malicious-package ids, distro advisory ids); every new id family requires a source-code regex update, and the failure mode for an unrecognised id is all-or-nothing for the whole batch/document rather than per-item.
- supply_chain.py's ecosystem coverage is limited to npm/pypi/crates.io/rubygems/maven; Go modules, NuGet, Packagist/composer, and OS-package ecosystems get no popular-name set at all, so typosquat detection silently does nothing for those ecosystems (empty pop set, not an error).
- Popular-name collections are plain set/frozenset objects consumed by first-match-wins loops, making the reported 'resembles' attribution sensitive to Python's per-process string-hash randomization rather than to any de terministic ranking (e.g. closest distance, lexicographic order).
- supply_chain.py has zero functional test coverage despite being described in its own docstring as a 'headline feature' comparable to Snyk/Socket.dev/JFrog; all of its detection logic (typosquat, homoglyph, dependency-confusion, crates/maven handlers, transitive traversal, risk score) is untested.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py covers a thin slice of vex.py: test_vex_fail_closed_on_bare_not_affected (statement() raises on bare not_affected), test_cli_vex_roundtrip (CLI --check on a single-statement, already-'fixed' OpenVEX fixture), test_policy_cli_vex_suppression (CLI policy --vex suppression, including a structurally-invalid VEX fails-closed case), and test_vex_evaluate_grouping (status grouping across not_affected/fixed/affected/under_investigation/unmatched). None of these tests use a VEX statement whose product purl carries a version different from the finding's version, so the critical version-collapsing defect (vex.py:394) is untested; from_scan_result, from_triaged, from_cve_reachability, load_vex_file/_parse_cyclonedx_vex, and generate_openvex have no direct unit tests at all (only exercised indirectly via CLI/OsvScanner paths with well-formed CVE ids). supply_chain.py / SupplyChainAnalyzer has zero test coverage anywhere in tests/ -- the only reference is a name-existence check in an unrelated list at tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py:90; none of analyze_one, _analyze_crates, _analyze_maven, get_transitive_deps, or compute_risk_score is exercised by any test.

**열린 질문**:
- Is the version-collapsing behavior in VexIndex (defect at vex.py:394-395, 406) an intentional design trade-off (treat VEX suppression as package-level, not version-level) that is simply undocumented, or an unintended bug? The module's own framing ('an auditable, never silent' suppression) suggests the latter, but this should be confirmed with the maintainers before treating it as a fix candidate.
- Does any downstream sbomplus/ or bts_sbom caller rely on get_transitive_deps() today, or is it genuinely dead code reachable only through direct unit tests/future features? (Repo-wide grep in this session found no caller besides its own definition and tests directory was not checked for it.)
- What is the actual, spec-correct CycloneDX justification string for 'inline mitigations' (protected_by_mitigating_control vs protected_by_mitigations used at vex.py:104)? This needs verification against the official CycloneDX JSON schema, which was not accessible in this offline, network-disabled review.
- Is bts_sbom/supply_chain.py expected to gain test coverage, or is testing of this module deferred to a higher-level integration/e2e layer in sbomplus/ that this slice did not include?

### core-licenses — bts_sbom: license subsystem

**목적**: This slice implements the bts_sbom core's license subsystem: an SPDX license-expression grammar (parser + AST), two independent and not-mutually-consistent SPDX-id-to-risk-category classifiers, normalization/validation of declared license expressions (with deprecated-id handling feeding the SPDX SBOM's licenseDeclared field), and an offline source-tree license/copyright evidence scanner. It is the component responsible for turning a package's license string (or a file's license text) into both a canonical SPDX expression and a compliance risk verdict (copyleft/permissive/proprietary/etc.) used by the sector report engine.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_spdx.py`: Pure-syntax SPDX license-expression tokenizer + recursive-descent parser: produces a LicenseAtom/And/Or AST with documented OR<AND<WITH precedence, parens, LicenseRef-/DocumentRef-/AdditionRef- tokens, '+' legacy suffix, NOASSERTION/NONE sentinels. No semantics; consumed only by license_norm.py in this slice.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_norm.py`: Normalizes/validates SPDX expressions on top of license_spdx (status ok\|deprecated\|invalid\|none), an explicit deprecated-id rename table, compound-expression leaf decomposition + copyleft detection (delegates to license_categories.category), SPDX-License-Identifier file/directory header scanner, and verify_packages() summary. Feeds spdx_sbom.py's licenseDeclared field and sca_report.py's license_verification.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_categories.py`: Newer canonical SPDX-id -> category classifier (public-domain/permissive/weak-copyleft/strong-copyleft/network-copyleft/proprietary/unknown), GPL/AGPL version-direction compatibility, and a category-pair compatibility table. Declared in its own docstring as the slice's single source of truth, but reached only through license_norm.py.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_scan.py`: FOSSology-inspired (clean-room) source-tree license EVIDENCE scanner: SPDX-tag extraction ('ojo'), an ordered ~70-entry required/excluded phrase signature matcher ('nomos'), copyright-holder extraction, binary/size/skip-dir bounds, and tree aggregation. Delegates categorization/policy to license_utils.classify_license/analyze_licenses, not to license_categories.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_utils.py`: Older, still-production, independent SPDX-id -> policy-category classifier (Permissive/Weak/Strong/Network Copyleft/Proprietary/Commercial Restricted/Unknown) plus a combinatorially-generated ~680-entry obligations table, ~14 hand-curated FSF/OSI incompatible-pair rules, analyze_licenses() findings/report, a 0-100 compliance risk score, and a pairwise compatibility matrix builder. Called directly by sca_report.py and osvscanner.py (the actual production SCA report path).

**데이터 흐름**: A raw license string (from a manifest, deps.dev, or a detected file) enters either (a) license_norm.normalize()/parse_compound_licenses(), which parses it with license_spdx into an AST, re-renders it canonically, and asks license_categories.category() per leaf for copyleft detection -- this path feeds spdx_sbom.py's licenseDeclared and sca_report.py's deprecated/invalid verification; or (b) license_utils.classify_license()/analyze_licenses()/get_license_obligations(), a self-contained string-splitting + substring-heuristic engine with its own category table, called directly by sca_report.py's main report and osvscanner.py's experimental_licenses -- this is the actual production categorization path and does not go through license_spdx or license_categories at all. Independently, license_scan.scan_tree() walks a source tree, extracts SPDX tags / phrase-signature matches / copyrights per file, and hands the detected license strings to license_utils.classify_license/analyze_licenses (not license_categories) to build its policy summary. The two categorization engines (license_categories vs license_utils) never call each other and are not kept in sync, so the same input can receive different compliance verdicts depending on which call path a caller takes.

**강점**:
- license_spdx.py's tokenizer/recursive-descent parser is clean and correctly implements the documented OR<AND<WITH precedence with a clear separation between syntax (this module) and semantics (license_categories/license_utils), and it fails closed (SpdxParseError) rather than guessing on malformed input.
- Both license_norm.py and license_scan.py embed an explicit, testable honesty discipline in the code itself: NOASSERTION/unresolved licenses are never fabricated, scan truncation and skipped directories are reported rather than hidden, and per-license findings in license_scan.py always carry a file:line + snippet trace.
- analyze_licenses() in license_utils.py isolates per-package failures (bad encoding, wrong type, None license) with try/except that demotes to UNKNOWN and logs, so one malformed package cannot abort classification of the rest of the set -- a genuinely good resilience pattern for a batch compliance tool.
- license_scan.py's layered ojo (SPDX tag) / nomos (phrase signature) / copyright design, with required+excluded phrase lists per signature, is an auditable, dependency-free way to get reasonable file-level license detection without any third-party license-detection library.

**설계상 약점**:
- The exact 'five overlapping knowledge bases' problem that license_categories.py's own docstring says motivated its creation still exists in practice: license_utils.py's independently-maintained _LICENSE_MAP/_SUBSTRING_RULES/_INCOMPATIBLE_PAIRS was never retired, remains the actual engine wired into the production sca_report.py/osvscanner.py paths, and now disagrees with license_categories.py on real licenses (see defects).
- Three separate license category/normalize implementations now exist for related purposes across the codebase (license_categories.category, license_utils.classify_license, and sbomplus/licenses.py's own normalize/category outside this slice), each with its own vocabulary (e.g. 'weak-copyleft' vs 'Weak Copyleft') and substring heuristics -- a maintenance-debt pattern that has already produced concrete, user-visible contradictions.
- Unanchored substring matching (`needle in low`) is used as the fallback classifier in both category engines; it is inherently unsafe for a compliance tool because short license abbreviations ('mpl', 'gpl', 'isc') can accidentally appear inside unrelated English words, and there is no word-boundary guard anywhere in either fallback list.
- Several fields look like they encode real pairwise-compatibility analysis but are effectively stubs: parse_compound_licenses()'s 'compatibility_issues' is always an empty list (the OR/permissive-filter logic that touches it is consequently dead code), and build_compat_matrix()'s handling of single-license-flag entries is unreachable dead code -- a caller reading either field's name would reasonably assume more rigor than exists.
- Minor dead code in a module whose own docstring emphasizes precision: _canonical_token() in license_norm.py is a no-op (`return tok`) never called anywhere in the repository.

**테스트 커버리지**: Grep across the entire tests/ tree found zero imports of any of the five target modules (license_spdx, license_categories, license_norm, license_scan, license_utils) by name. The handful of existing 'license' test functions (tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py, test_sbom_sectors_arch.py) exercise sbomplus's own independent licenses.py module (a separate reimplementation outside this slice), and tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py's test_policy_license_rule_over_sbom exercises bts_sbom._internal.policy's exact-string deny-list, not this slice's classification/normalization logic. None of the parser's precedence/paren/exception edge cases, the two category engines' agreement, the deprecated-rename table, or the file-scanner's signature/encoding/binary handling have any direct unit test in this repository.

**열린 질문**:
- Should license_categories.py actually be wired into sca_report.py/osvscanner.py (replacing license_utils._LICENSE_MAP as its own docstring claims), or is license_utils.py now the intended long-term engine? The repo gives no indication either engine is scheduled for removal.
- Is parse_compound_licenses()'s always-empty 'compatibility_issues' field and build_compat_matrix()'s dead single-license-flag branch intentional stubs awaiting a future pairwise-compatibility feature, or an unfinished migration left over from the license_categories.py introduction?
- Is folding 'proprietary' into _NONE in license_norm.py deliberate (e.g. because some downstream policy gate outside this slice treats NOASSERTION and Proprietary identically on purpose), or an oversight? Nothing in the slice or its direct callers documents this choice.
- Was docs/CORE_ISSUES.md's strip('()') entry (#2) written before license_norm.py's own identical defect was noticed, or is the project aware of it and simply hasn't logged it yet?

### plus-core-adapter — sbomplus: workspace, engine adapter, OSV/net/util/cvss/versions

**목적**: This slice is sbomplus's thin layer that (a) turns a user-given source (git URL/shorthand/local folder) into an isolated, never-written-to work copy (workspace.py), (b) is the SOLE adapter through which every other sbomplus module talks to the bts_sbom core scanner, normalising its native OSV-Scanner result into deduplicated components/vulnerabilities with CVSS scoring and ECOSYSTEM/SEMVER-only fixed-version extraction (engine.py), and (c) provides the shared low-level plumbing those two depend on: a direct OSV.dev re-verification client (osv.py), a retrying stdlib HTTP helper (net.py), small io/hash/subprocess utilities (util.py), a from-spec CVSS v3 calculator (cvss.py), and an ecosystem-agnostic version comparator (versions.py).

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/__init__.py`: Package marker; declares sbomplus __version__ = 0.1.0 (independent of bts_sbom core's own 0.1.1).
- `sbomplus/__main__.py`: `python -m sbomplus` entry point; delegates to sbomplus.cli.main (cli.py is outside this slice).
- `run_sbomplus.py`: Repo-root launcher meant for `python -I` isolated-mode runs; inserts the repo root onto sys.path then calls sbomplus.cli.main.
- `sbomplus/workspace.py`: Source acquisition: classifies a src string as git URL / GitHub shorthand / local path (normalize_source), refuses ext::/fd::/http:// and option-like strings, then clones (git, hooks/submodules disabled) or copies (shutil) into an isolated work_root copy (prepare); git() wrapper disables hooks/prompts per invocation; tree_hash-adjacent head()/git() helpers.
- `sbomplus/engine.py`: THE single adapter to bts_sbom core: _import_core() (BTS_SBOM_PATH override + sys.path insert), scan()/analyze() wrapping core.OsvScanner, normalize() turning the core's native OSV-Scanner-shaped result into flat components/vulns with alias dedupe (_merge_vulns), CVSS scoring (via cvss.severity_of), fixed-version extraction restricted to ECOSYSTEM/SEMVER ranges (fixed_versions), Ruby platform merge (_canonical_version), and go.sum MVS filtering (_go_selected); records every correction in adjustments.
- `sbomplus/osv.py`: Direct OSV.dev client used to re-verify candidate 'safe' versions: query_batch (batched coordinate lookup with pagination follow-up) and get_vuln/get_vulns (cached advisory document fetch), consumed by sbomplus/remediate.py.
- `sbomplus/net.py`: Shared stdlib-urllib HTTP helper (http_text, http_json) with retry/backoff, request/failure counters (STATS) and a TRANSPORT monkeypatch point for tests; intended as the single outbound-call chokepoint (claim checked above).
- `sbomplus/util.py`: Shared helpers: time/hash/safe-name utilities, atomic write_json/read_json/read_text, subprocess run() wrapper, tree_hash() (origin non-modification proof, used by pipeline.py/deliver.py), code_fingerprint(), ascii_safe(), clip(), uniq().
- `sbomplus/cvss.py`: Stdlib-only CVSS v3.0/3.1 base-score calculator from a vector string plus severity_of() combining vector score, advisory label fallback, and malware-advisory override; used by engine.py's alias-group severity selection.
- `sbomplus/versions.py`: Small ecosystem-agnostic version comparator (PEP 440/semver-ish tokenisation + qualifier ranking) used throughout engine.py (range containment, max/min-greater-or-equal, major()) and by remediate.py for bump/verification logic; explicitly NOT a full resolver.

**데이터 흐름**: A source string flows into workspace.normalize_source/prepare, producing an isolated work-copy path + lineage dict. engine.scan() lazily imports bts_sbom (via engine._import_core(), honoring BTS_SBOM_PATH) and runs OsvScanner.scan() on that path; engine.normalize() turns the core's native result into components/vulns, calling cvss.severity_of() per alias-merged vulnerability group and versions.compare()/is_version() inside fixed_versions() for every ECOSYSTEM/SEMVER range. The normalized vulns/components feed sbomplus/remediate.py (outside this slice), which calls osv.query_batch()/get_vulns() (net.http_json under the hood) to re-verify candidate safe versions, again calling engine.fixed_versions()/versions.compare(). util.tree_hash() is used elsewhere (pipeline.py, deliver.py) to prove the origin directory was never modified; util.run()/write_json()/read_json() back workspace.py's git() wrapper and (outside this slice) evidence persistence. net.py's TRANSPORT hook is the single point every test monkeypatches to keep the suite network-free, except sbomplus/llm.py which bypasses net.py entirely with its own urllib client.

**강점**:
- cvss.py is a faithful, numerically-verified implementation of the CVSS v3.1 base-score formulas (roundup, impact, exploitability, scope-changed branch all match the FIRST spec), and correctly refuses to score v2/v4 vectors rather than guessing.
- engine.fixed_versions() correctly and deliberately restricts itself to ECOSYSTEM/SEMVER range events (skipping GIT ranges), which matches and fixes a real, documented bts_sbom core defect (docs/CORE_ISSUES.md #1) where the core's guided remediation returned raw git commit SHAs as 'safe versions'.
- workspace.py's git-clone path layers multiple real defenses together (hooksPath override to an empty dir, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT/ASKPASS disabling, --no-recurse-submodules, core.symlinks=false, protocol.ext.allow=never, a `--` separator before the url/dest positional args, and an explicit string-level refusal of ext::/fd::/leading-dash sources) rather than relying on just one.
- net.py and osv.py are explicit and conservative about failure semantics by design (404 vs. failure, None meaning 'unknown' rather than 'clean'), and the test suite (conftest.py FakeWorld) forces every test through a fake transport so no test silently depends on network access.
- engine.normalize()'s union-find alias merge (_merge_vulns) and the three independently-switchable corrections (dedupe, Ruby-platform merge, go.sum MVS filter) are each covered by a focused unit test in tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py, with the switch's effect asserted both on and off.

**설계상 약점**:
- Deliberate duplication of core logic (sbomplus/versions.py vs bts_sbom/_internal/version_cmp.py, and implicitly cvss.py has no core counterpart to diverge from) is a standing maintenance risk: the core module was hardened against a documented real-world bug that sbomplus's own reimplementation does not share, and there is no shared test asserting the two comparators agree on any corpus of versions.
- sbomplus/versions.py's dedupe/component keys in engine.py use the raw, un-canonicalised version string (e.g. comps key = (eco, name, ver)); two manifests that declare the semantically-equal but differently-formatted versions ('1.0' vs '1.0.0') for the same package are not merged by dedupe/platform-merge even though versions.compare() would treat them as equal.
- net.py is advertised as the single outbound chokepoint but sbomplus/llm.py bypasses it entirely with an independent, less battle-tested urllib client and its own ad hoc retry/stat bookkeeping -- a second place to keep in sync if backoff policy or UA/stat requirements change.
- _hooks_dir() creates one process-wide temp directory the first time any prepare() runs and never removes it; harmless but an unbounded (if small) per-process resource leak, and a second disabled-hooks temp dir is independently created per git() invocation's -c flag resolution path only once, so this is consistent but still untracked by any cleanup.
- versions.py's _norm() contains a trailing `.replace('+incompatible', '')` that is unreachable in practice: the preceding unconditional `s.split('+', 1)[0]` already strips anything from the first '+' onward, so the explicit incompatible-suffix handling never has anything left to match -- dead code, correct output, but a stale artifact of an earlier version of the function.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py directly tests engine.normalize()'s three toggled corrections (dedupe, Ruby-platform merge, go.sum MVS filter) and the malware-advisory CRITICAL override, each with the switch both on and off. tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py and conftest.py's advisories_for()/_in_range() helper exercise engine.fixed_versions() indirectly, but only with plain numeric versions (e.g. '2.20.0','4.17.11','1.0','5.4') -- no qualifier-suffixed version is used anywhere in the test fixtures, so the versions.py regression found here is completely untested. tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py exercises workspace.normalize_source's ext::/leading-dash/http:// refusals and workspace.prepare()'s git-clone and tree_hash-based origin-unchanged invariants, but never a local-folder source containing a symlink, and never 'fd::' or the scp-like dash-host pattern. osv.py, net.py, cvss.py, and util.py have no dedicated test file or test function anywhere under tests/ -- they are only reached incidentally through engine.py/remediate.py call paths in other tests, and conftest.py's FakeWorld never simulates OSV pagination (next_page_token), so query_batch's pagination/fallback branch (the osv.py defect above) is never executed by the suite.

**열린 질문**:
- Does the SBOM/CVE scanning path in this repository ever actually encounter OSV ECOSYSTEM/SEMVER ranges with single-letter-suffixed versions in practice (e.g. via the Debian dpkg.py manifest parser or the C/C++ reachability feature), or is sbomplus/versions.py's qualifier table only ever exercised against the cleaner PyPI/npm/Go/RubyGems/Composer ecosystems its own docstring names? This determines whether the versions.py regression found here is a live, exploitable-today gap or a latent one.
- Why does sbomplus/versions.py duplicate bts_sbom/_internal/version_cmp.py's tokeniser/ranking instead of importing/reusing it through the single engine.py adapter, given the core module is explicitly documented as having fixed the exact bug sbomplus/versions.py reintroduces?
- Is tests/test_engine_contract.py (named in engine.py's own docstring) planned but not yet written, or was it renamed/removed without the docstring being updated?
- Should sbomplus/llm.py route through net.py (shared retry/backoff/UA/stats) rather than maintaining a second, independent urllib client, given net.py's docstring claims to be the single outbound chokepoint?
- Is the next_page_token / completeness-retry path in osv.query_batch ever exercised against the real OSV.dev API in practice (i.e. do real package/version lookups actually paginate), or is it dead code in practice because OSV.dev rarely returns more ids than fit on one page?

### plus-licenses-sbom-sectors — sbomplus: licenses, sbom writers, sectors, report

**목적**: This slice turns resolved components+vulnerabilities into deliverables: licenses.py discovers per-component license evidence (lockfile → deps.dev → registry) with provenance and SPDX-expression normalisation/categorisation/policy checks; sbom.py writes CycloneDX 1.5, delegates SPDX 2.3 to the bts_sbom core (with a documented workaround for a core bug), writes OpenVEX, and offers structural validators for both SBOM formats; sectors.py maps one SBOM/VEX/EOL evidence set onto 7 regulatory profiles (6 delegated to bts_sbom's core engine, 1 — maritime — implemented locally); report.py renders the resulting per-project and batch records as Markdown/HTML.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/licenses.py`: SPDX alias/category tables, a real recursive-descent AND/OR/WITH parser (normalize/category), lockfile + deps.dev + PyPI/npm/Maven registry resolution with a raw-evidence cache, source-tree LICENSE/SPDX-header scanning, and distribution-aware policy_findings().
- `sbomplus/sbom.py`: CycloneDX 1.5 builder+validator, SPDX 2.3 builder (thin wrapper over bts_sbom core via engine._import_core()) with a paren-stripping-bug workaround, validate_spdx/validate_cyclonedx/ref_integrity structural checks, OpenVEX builder.
- `sbomplus/sectors.py`: industry_reports()/matrix() dispatch 6 industries straight to bts_sbom.sectors.sector_report via the core; maritime_report() is a locally-authored IACS UR E26/E27 draft checklist, the only sector logic duplicated/reimplemented in sbomplus.
- `sbomplus/report.py`: Pure rendering layer: project_md() (per-project Markdown), batch_md()/batch_html() (aggregate batch summaries); no business logic, consumes dicts produced by pipeline.py/batch.py.

**데이터 흐름**: pipeline.py calls licenses.resolve()/scan_tree() to annotate rec['components'] and rec['license'] in place; sbom.cyclonedx()/spdx()/openvex() turn components+vulns into the three documents (rec['_docs']); sbom.validate_cyclonedx()/validate_spdx()/ref_integrity() populate rec['sbom_check']; sectors.industry_reports() consumes the CycloneDX doc + VEX (never the SPDX doc) to populate rec['sectors']; batch.py aggregates many recs and report.py renders both individual recs and the aggregate into Markdown/HTML for evidence/ output. Within sectors.py, 6/7 profiles flow through engine._import_core() into bts_sbom.sectors.sector_report (which itself reuses bts_sbom._internal.ntia_check); only maritime_report() computes its own verdicts directly from the CycloneDX dict.

**강점**:
- licenses.py's normalize()/category() is a genuine structure-preserving parser (parens, AND/OR/WITH, exception demotion, OR-picks-best/AND-poisons-on-unknown) backed by solid unit tests (test_license_spellings_map_or_stay_unmapped, test_wellformed_unknown_ids_resolve_and_nonspdx_spellings_do_not).
- The raw-evidence license cache (store raw, normalize at read time) is a deliberate, tested design that makes normaliser improvements retroactive without re-querying the network (test_cached_raw_evidence_is_reinterpreted_without_network).
- sectors.py is a genuinely thin adapter for 6 of 7 industries — it delegates to bts_sbom.sectors.sector_report rather than re-implementing ~600 lines of regulatory-check logic, and engine.py is confirmed (by grep) to be the only file in sbomplus that imports bts_sbom, matching the project's stated single-touchpoint design.
- Core honesty invariants are correctly implemented in the paths I traced: sbom.cyclonedx() omits the 'licenses' key entirely when there is no evidence (verified by test_no_license_evidence_means_no_license_entry) rather than inventing NOASSERTION; validate_cyclonedx catches dangling vulnerability 'affects' refs (test_validator_catches_dangling_reference).
- The documented CORE_ISSUES.md #2 workaround in sbom.spdx() is real, deliberate, tested for its primary case, and the module honestly discloses it inline rather than silently papering over the core bug.

**설계상 약점**:
- sbom.cyclonedx()/spdx() never populate a component's supplier/publisher/author/manufacturer field anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep across all of sbomplus/*.py) — so every 'supplier coverage' check across all 7 sector reports (maritime MAR-03 and the NTIA-based CRA-03/FDA-03/DOD-03/FIN-04 the core computes from these same documents) is permanently and deterministically 0%, not because real supplier evidence was checked and found lacking project-by-project, but because the writer has no mechanism to carry it at all; this contradicts the sbom.py docstring's implication that supplier evidence is conditionally present ('absent unless real evidence exists').
- validate_cyclonedx() and validate_spdx() give very different guarantees for the same underlying concept ('is this license expression valid SPDX'): validate_spdx re-parses the expression with the real grammar, validate_cyclonedx only checks that a 'license'/'expression' key is present, never that its value parses — so 'SBOM valid' means two different things depending on format, which the batch/report metrics (cyclonedx_valid, spdx_valid) present side by side as if comparable.
- Two independent SPDX alias/category/parsing implementations exist in the same repository (sbomplus/licenses.py's own CATEGORY/_ALIASES/_one()/normalize() vs bts_sbom._internal.license_spdx/license_norm) with no shared source of truth; docs/CORE_ISSUES.md#2 shows the core's own normaliser has exactly the bug sbomplus's parser was built to avoid, which is direct evidence of how easily the two can drift further apart.
- sectors.py's maritime_report() reimplements vulnerability-closing semantics (MAR-05) that none of the 6 core-delegated profiles attempt (those only check VEX *presence*, never try to resolve which findings are closed) — and in doing so it does not reuse bts_sbom/vex.py's carefully built, separately tested fail-closed justification discipline, which is exactly where the gap below was found.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py thoroughly covers licenses.py's normalize/category/policy/resolve/scan_tree and caching (including the deliberate CORE_ISSUES#2 parens test), but only for the single nesting direction, not the symmetric AND/OR case shown broken above. tests/sbomplus/test_sbom_sectors_arch.py covers sbom.cyclonedx()/validate_cyclonedx()/ref_integrity()/spdx() happy paths, the 'no license -> no licenses entry' rule, a dangling-ref negative test, and that all 7 industries produce a report plus maritime's not_evidence branch -- but no test ever feeds maritime_report() (or any sector) an actual VEX document with fixed/not_affected/affected statements to exercise MAR-05's closing logic (exactly where the critical VEX defect lives), and no test exercises the purl-less cross-ecosystem collision path in cyclonedx(). sbomplus/report.py has zero test coverage anywhere under tests/ (confirmed by grep for 'report' imports) -- project_md/batch_md/batch_html are entirely untested.

**열린 질문**:
- How often does engine.py actually hand sbom.cyclonedx() a component with purl=='' in real repositories (which ecosystems/manifest parsers in the bts_sbom core produce no purl), i.e. is the cross-ecosystem bom-ref collision a frequent real-world risk or a rare corner case? This requires reading bts_sbom's native scanner/identity code, outside this slice.
- Is sectors.maritime_report() ever invoked in production with a VEX document that did NOT come from sbomplus's own sbom.openvex() (which never emits not_affected)? If the current pipeline always self-generates the VEX it feeds in, the not_affected/justification gap is a live defect in the public API but currently dormant in the shipped pipeline wiring -- worth confirming against cli.py/pipeline.py call sites beyond this slice.
- Is the docs/README 0.990 license-resolution figure reproducible today against the same repositories, or tied to a frozen evidence/batch/latest.json snapshot that may already be stale?
- Should _ref()'s generic-purl fallback include the ecosystem (e.g. 'pkg:generic/<ecosystem>/<name>@<version>') to remove the cross-ecosystem collision risk entirely?

### plus-patch-deliver — sbomplus: remediation, approval-gated patching, delivery

**목적**: This slice is BTS-SBOM 앱 계층's approval-gated remediation pipeline: remediate.py turns (component, vulnerability) data into a per-package 'lowest version that clears everything' plan and re-verifies each candidate against OSV.dev; patch.py gates that plan behind an explicit approval + digest check and then performs pure per-ecosystem text/JSON edits of dependency manifests (never lockfiles) on an isolated work copy, committing the result to a fix branch; deliver.py routes an applied patch to one of four destinations (branch/folder/inplace-origin/github-push+PR), each outward-facing target gated behind its own explicit boolean flag. Together with pipeline.py's _patch_loop (adjacent orchestrator), this implements the project's 'approval-gated patching with re-scan verification and revert-on-regression' claim.

**구성 요소**:
