- `sbomplus/remediate.py`: plan(): groups vulnerabilities by (ecosystem, canonical name, installed version) and computes safe_version = max of each advisory's own fixed version (the lowest version that, assuming monotonic fixes, clears every advisory for that component@version). verify(): re-queries OSV.dev for the (package, safe_version) candidate itself, up to max_rounds times, bumping safe_version further if the candidate is itself still affected; records clean=True/False/None (never silently treats a failed query or an unfixable residual as clean). summary(): aggregates counts for reporting.
- `sbomplus/patch.py`: Per-ecosystem pure text/JSON editors (edit_requirements, edit_package_json, edit_go_mod, edit_cargo_toml, edit_pom_xml, edit_composer_json, edit_gemfile) that rewrite a single dependency's version in a manifest; _targets_for()/_lock_flavor() pick candidate manifest files per plan from plan['files']; approval_digest()/apply() implement the approval gate and drive the actual read-edit-write-commit sequence on the work copy's fix branch; revert()/diff() support regression rollback and reporting.
- `sbomplus/deliver.py`: Takes an apply() result and routes it to one of branch (no-op, default)/folder (export files+fix.patch+PATCH_REPORT.md)/inplace (git apply onto the real origin folder, gated by allow_origin_write)/github (git push + optional `gh pr create`, gated by allow_push/open_pr).
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: (adjacent, direct caller/orchestrator) _patch_loop() repeatedly calls patch.apply(), rescans with engine.analyze(), scopes the before/after vulnerability comparison to the files that were actually edited, calls patch.revert() on regression, and implements the 'spinning guard' against repeating an identical plan set; run_project() forwards only the LAST round's apply() result (rec['patch']['last']) into deliver.deliver().
- `sbomplus/engine.py`: (adjacent, callee) _canon()/fixed_versions() supply the canonical-name matching and range-aware fixed-version computation that remediate.py groups/plans on; normalize() builds each component's accumulated 'files' list (via a root-relative os.path.relpath) that ends up as plan['files'], the untrusted-ish input patch.py trusts as edit targets.
- `sbomplus/workspace.py`: (adjacent, callee) prepare()/git()/head() create and operate on the sandboxed work copy that patch.py edits; local-folder sources are copied with shutil.copytree(..., symlinks=True), preserving real symlinks (unlike the git-clone path which disables core.symlinks), a plausible route for a files-path to resolve outside the work copy.
- `sbomplus/versions.py`: (adjacent, callee) Ecosystem-agnostic version comparator (compare/max_version/major) used throughout remediate.py and patch.py's blocking/floor-bump logic.
- `tests/sbomplus/conftest.py`: Fakes: FakeScanner (only recognizes files literally named 'requirements.txt', narrower than patch.py's own requirements*.txt matching), FakeWorld (fake OSV/deps.dev/PyPI/npm/Maven HTTP), make_repo (git fixture builder).
- `tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py`: Acceptance tests (AC-1,2,3,14-22,45,59,66,68) for workspace safety, approval gate, digest mismatch, upper-bound block, a single-file requirements patch+commit, regression revert (single round, via pipeline._patch_loop), lockfile-only manual fallback, transitive-gem manual fallback, scoped-regression (untouched lockfile doesn't cause a false revert), delivery flag gating, github push, folder export, and work-copy replacement.

**데이터 흐름**: engine.analyze() (bts_sbom core scan + engine.normalize) produces components/vulns, each vuln/component carrying a root-relative 'files' list built via os.path.relpath against root (no containment check). remediate.plan() groups vulns per (ecosystem, canonical name, version) into one plan per component with a computed safe_version and copies 'files' from the matching component. remediate.verify() re-queries OSV.dev for each plan's (package, safe_version) candidate, up to max_rounds, possibly bumping safe_version. pipeline._patch_loop() calls patch.apply(root, plans, approved, token): apply() recomputes approval_digest(ups) from (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version) only, checks it against token, then for each upgrade plan calls _targets_for(plan, root) (built straight from plan['files']) and tries each candidate file in order via _edit(), stopping at the FIRST file that gets a non-zero, non-blocked edit; all successful edits accumulate in a per-call 'pending' dict, get written to disk, and are committed on a fix branch. _patch_loop then reruns engine.analyze() on the patched work copy, scopes the before/after vuln comparison to files actually in that round's changed_files, reverts via patch.revert() on regression (return value unchecked), or continues to another round with freshly-planned/verified plans, or stops; it forwards only the LAST round's apply() result into deliver.deliver(), which reads res['base_commit']/res['changed_files']/res['results'] (all per-call, not cumulative) to build the branch/folder/inplace/github delivery artifact, including an unescaped-component-name PATCH_REPORT.md / PR body.

**강점**:
- apply() genuinely changes 0 files and returns a digest without approved=True (verified by reading and by test_no_approval_changes_nothing); the approval gate's negative case is solid.
- Upper-bound / module-path-incompatible upgrades are detected and reported as 'blocked' rather than silently forced, for both requirements.txt (</<=) and go.mod (major version needs /vN path + import rewrite).
- edit_requirements correctly round-trips CRLF vs LF per line (captures the exact line ending via line[len(body):]) and preserves extras, environment markers, inline comments and trailing continuation characters when rewriting a matched line.
- Every ecosystem editor that interpolates a package/crate/module name into a dynamically-built regex (go.mod, Cargo.toml, package.json, Gemfile) consistently wraps it in re.escape(), avoiding regex-injection corruption from unusual package names.
- github push uses '--' before the user-supplied remote_url/refspec and never passes --force, so it can't be coerced into an option-injection or silently clobber a pre-existing remote branch; inplace/github are both cleanly gated behind allow_origin_write/allow_push (confirmed by test_inplace_and_github_need_explicit_flags).
- Regression detection is deliberately scoped to files the current round actually edited (with a documented real-world rationale and a dedicated regression test, AC-66) rather than naively diffing whole-repo vulnerability counts.

**설계상 약점**:
- patch.apply() has no containment check that an edited file stays under 'root'; it fully trusts plan['files'] (itself built by engine.normalize()'s os.path.relpath against root with no bounds check), so any upstream path that resolves outside root (a symlink in a locally-copied untrusted project, or a cross-directory manifest reference such as pip's '-r ../x/requirements.txt', go.mod 'replace' with a filesystem path, or an npm/yarn workspace 'file:' dependency) becomes a target patch.py will happily read and write.
- approval_digest() is a thin 4-field commitment (ecosystem, component, current_version, safe_version); it does not bind to which files will be edited or to the plan's verification state, so its own error text ('plan changed since it was reviewed') overstates the protection it actually gives.
- apply() treats a plan's candidate target files as alternatives to try until one succeeds, not as a set that must all be patched; a vulnerable (package, version) declared in more than one manifest (prod+dev requirements files, multiple workspace package.json files) is only ever fixed in the first one.
- Per-round apply() outputs (changed_files, results, base_commit) are not aggregated across pipeline._patch_loop's rounds; only the last round's result is ever handed to deliver(), so any multi-round run silently loses earlier rounds from delivery artifacts.
- The 'spinning guard' in pipeline._patch_loop (identical plan-set detection) looks largely dead code for the dominant real scenario (lockfile not actually changed), since the earlier, more specific rescan_fixed==0 check already breaks the loop first; it is unclear what realistic input would ever exercise it, and nothing tests that it does.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py covers (with AC tags): the approval gate (not-approved changes nothing), digest mismatch, upper-bound-blocked (requirements.txt only), a single happy-path requirements.txt patch across 3 pins with a fix-branch commit, one regression-revert scenario (single round, via pipeline._patch_loop), lockfile-only manual fallback, transitive-gem manual fallback (not blocked), scoped regression (an untouched lockfile's new findings don't trigger a false revert), inplace/github refused without their flags, github push producing exactly one remote branch, folder delivery's 3 artifacts, and work-copy replacement of read-only leftovers. tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py covers remediate.plan/verify in isolation (fixed-version purity, OSV-failure-is-unverified, bump-to-later-fix within max_rounds). NOT covered by any test: a component declared in more than one manifest file (the only-the-first-file-gets-patched defect), any path outside the work-copy root in plan['files'], a BOM-prefixed manifest, an idempotent/no-op re-apply of edit_package_json (the CRLF-destroying reformat path), a true happy-path version bump for package.json/Cargo.toml/pom.xml/Gemfile (only their lockfile-only/transitive 'manual' fallbacks are tested), a multi-round pipeline run followed by deliver() (the last-round-only delivery gap), the open_pr=True / `gh pr create` code path, and markdown/content escaping in _report_md.

**열린 질문**:
- Can engine.normalize()'s per-component 'files' list (consumed verbatim by patch.py as edit targets) ever legitimately contain a path outside the scanned root in a real (non-test) run - via symlinks in a locally-copied untrusted project, or via pip -r / go.mod replace / npm-yarn workspace 'file:' references the core scanner may report with an out-of-root source.path? This determines how exploitable defect #1 (path traversal) is in ordinary use versus only via adversarial input.
- Is apply()'s 'try candidate files until one succeeds, then stop' behavior in the per-plan target loop (patch.py:331-346) an intentional design choice, or an oversight that should instead patch every file in plan['files']? Nothing in the docstring or specs states that only one declaration needs fixing.
- Should approval_digest() incorporate plan['files'] and plan['verification']['clean'] so a stale or materially different plan cannot replay an old approval token?
- Is the pipeline._patch_loop 'spinning guard' (identical plan-set detection) ever actually reachable given that the more specific rescan_fixed==0 check already breaks the loop first in the common 'manifest changed but lockfile still pins the old version' case? No test exercises the spinning_stopped status.

### plus-arch — sbomplus: legacy architecture analysis

**목적**: sbomplus/arch.py performs read-only, deterministic "legacy architecture" analysis of a source tree already materialized as an isolated work copy: language/LOC inventory, git-history-driven hotspot and change-coupling (hidden-dependency) detection, a regex/AST per-language internal import graph with Tarjan SCC cycle detection and condensation-DAG layering/fan-in-out/instability metrics, directory-based domain clustering with extraction-candidate flagging, and a manifest-driven runtime/toolchain EOL check (hard-coded table, optional online endoflife.date). It funnels all of this into one `analyze()` result plus a short list of evidence-citing `modernization()` proposals, consumed downstream by the AI/RAG layers.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/arch.py`: Whole slice: inventory (collect_files/stack), git-history hotspot/coupling, per-language import-graph builders, Tarjan SCC + layering/instability metrics, directory clustering, runtime EOL lookup, and the analyze()/modernization() orchestrator.
- `sbomplus/workspace.py`: git() subprocess wrapper used by _git_history() to run `git log --numstat` and `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` against the isolated work copy.
- `sbomplus/net.py`: http_json() used by eol_lookup() for the optional online endoflife.date call; also the TRANSPORT test-hook used in my experiments to simulate network failure without any real network access.
- `sbomplus/util.py`: read_text() - bounded-size UTF-8 file reader used by every per-language edge extractor and by stack().
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: Caller: run_project() invokes archmod.analyze(path, online=arch_online) as the 'arch' stage (default arch_online=False), wrapped in a generic try/except that records stage_errors and defaults rec['arch'] to {} on any exception (including RecursionError).
- `sbomplus/cli.py`: Caller: `sbomplus arch` subcommand calls archmod.analyze() directly with no try/except (cli.py:172), so an exception inside analyze() crashes the CLI invocation outright.
- `sbomplus/ai.py`: Consumer: reads arch['hotspots']/arch['extraction_candidates'] as grounding evidence for LLM/RAG proposals, so silent gaps in arch.py propagate into EXAONE proposals and chatbot answers.
- `tests/sbomplus/test_sbom_sectors_arch.py`: Only test file exercising this slice: 5 tests covering Python cycle detection, hotspot ranking (no renames), no-.git skip, Dockerfile EOL, and Ruby cycle+partial-note.

**데이터 흐름**: pipeline.run_project (or the `arch` CLI subcommand) hands arch.analyze() the path of an already-materialized, isolated work copy (never the origin repo). analyze() walks the tree once (collect_files), computes per-file LOC/complexity (stack), builds the import graph per detected language (one regex/AST pass per file), feeds the edge set into Tarjan SCC + condensation layering (graph_metrics) and into clusters(); separately shells out via workspace.git() to `git log --numstat` for commit/file churn consumed by hotspots()/coupling() (coupling cross-references the same edge set to flag 'hidden dependencies'); optionally calls net.http_json() against endoflife.date for runtime EOL, else falls back to the embedded table. Everything is merged into one dict under rec['arch'], summarized into rec['metrics'] (arch_edges/arch_cycles), and read downstream by ai.py/chat.py/rag.py to ground EXAONE proposals and RAG chatbot answers - so any silent gap found here (misreported languages_analysed, dropped hotspot commits, undetected Rust cycles, inflated JS complexity, stale/absent EOL data) is passed on as if it were solid evidence.

**강점**:
- Entirely stdlib-only and read-only; every file read is size-capped (read_text limits) and AST-parse failures are caught narrowly (SyntaxError/ValueError/MemoryError/RecursionError), so malformed or huge files cannot crash the pipeline by themselves.
- os.walk's default followlinks=False means a symlink loop inside the analyzed tree cannot cause infinite traversal - verified empirically with a self-referential directory symlink.
- The iterative Tarjan SCC implementation (sccs()) was verified correct against several nontrivial multi-SCC synthetic graphs, including nested cycles and cross-cycle cross edges.
- Python import resolution (including multi-level relative imports) is accurate and conservatively ambiguity-safe: a bare name only resolves when exactly one dotted-path candidate matches; ties resolve to no edge rather than a guess - verified with a 3-level package using `.`, `..`, and named-sibling imports.
- Several explicit honesty mechanisms already exist and work as specified where applied (no-.git skip, git-level shallow-clone flag, Ruby partial-analysis note, EOL embedded-table-is-UNVERIFIED tag) - the gaps found are places that same discipline was not carried through consistently, not an absence of the mechanism.
- modernization() proposals cite concrete evidence (commit counts, LOC, complexity, cohesion/inbound numbers) rather than freeform text.

**설계상 약점**:
- The disclosure mechanism (`notes`) is opt-in per code path rather than structural - only Ruby and the two global cases (no .git, zero edges overall) are wired to add a note, so new or unnoticed gaps (Rust, C, Kotlin, git-log edge cases) fall outside it by default rather than being caught by a general safety net.
- _git_history's numstat parser treats the filename field as always a plain, currently-valid relative path, with no awareness of git's own rename or path-quoting conventions - both are default git behaviour, not obscure edge cases, so this is a structural gap rather than a rare corner case.
- The only "this view is incomplete" signal surfaced is git-level shallow-clone detection; the module's own max_commits windowing (its single most load-bearing parameter for scaling to large repos) has no equivalent signal.
- Import-edge extraction rigor varies sharply by language (full AST for Python vs. single-pass line regex for everything else) but the output does not reflect that difference in confidence - all resolved edges, and the cycles/metrics built from them, are presented with equal weight regardless of language.
- The complexity "proxy" is pure token/regex matching over raw source text with no comment/string stripping, so it is sensitive to superficial lexical patterns (e.g. modern JS/TS syntax) rather than actual control flow, while still driving which files get flagged as refactor-worthy hotspots.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_sbom_sectors_arch.py has 5 tests touching this slice: Python 2-module import cycle (SP-AC-29), hotspot ranking on a simple non-renamed history (SP-AC-30), no-.git skip (SP-AC-31), Dockerfile-declared EOL for python 3.7 (SP-AC-32), and a Ruby require_relative cycle + partial note (SP-AC-58). All 5 pass against the current code (re-ran them). Entirely untested: coupling()/hidden-dependency detection, clusters()/extraction candidates, modernization() proposal generation end-to-end, the JS/TS/Java/Go/C/C++ import-edge builders (6 of 9 supported "graph languages" have zero tests), eol_lookup's online code path and its caching behaviour, runtimes() detection for every manifest type except Dockerfile (.python-version, .nvmrc, go.mod, package.json, pyproject.toml/setup.py/setup.cfg, composer.json), max_files/max_commits capping, and - unsurprisingly, since they are exactly the defects found above - git rename/quote handling, the Kotlin languages_analysed mislabeling, Rust's mod-vs-use gap, and deep-chain recursion.

**열린 질문**:
- Is renamed/non-ASCII-named file history meant to be an accepted blind spot of hotspots/coupling, or should _git_history special-case git's `a => b` / `{a => b}/c` rename syntax and pass `-c core.quotepath=false` (or use `-z`) to get literal, parseable paths?
- Should max_commits truncation of a non-shallow repo be surfaced as its own note (distinct from is-shallow-repository), given it is the default, common case for any sizeable legacy repo?
- Is Kotlin import-graph support intentionally reusing the Java regex (with its "as Alias"/semicolon-free syntax gaps), and if so should _GRAPH_LANGS simply be corrected to include "kotlin" so the honesty metadata matches reality?
- Should Rust's `use`-based cross-module edges be added (so cycle detection is meaningful for Rust), or is the mod-tree-only edge set an intentional simplification that just needs a Ruby-style disclosure note?
- Is there an intended refresh cadence or "as of" stamp for _EOL_STATIC, given entries like python 3.10's 2026-10-31 EOL date are now determinative very close to "today", and "go" has zero entries at all?

### plus-ai-rag-server — sbomplus: LLM (EXAONE), validator, RAG, chat, evals, HTTP server

**목적**: This slice is sbomplus's AI/retrieval/serving layer: an EXAONE LLM client with budget and safety controls (llm.py), a validated LLM-or-deterministic remediation-proposal lane (ai.py), a bilingual hybrid-retrieval engine over the project's own knowledge base (rag.py), a grounded chatbot combining deterministic project-fact tools with that retrieval (chat.py), an offline evaluation harness for both (evals.py), and a minimal localhost JSON/HTML server exposing scan and chat over HTTP (server.py). Together they let a human (via CLI or the HTTP UI) ask an EXAONE-backed or purely deterministic system for remediation guidance and ask natural-language questions about an analysed project or the tool's own documentation, with every LLM-touching output supposed to be truthfully labelled and evidence-grounded.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/llm.py`: Stdlib-only OpenAI-compatible chat-completions client for LG EXAONE (or a local/compatible server): config resolution, per-day call ledger/budget, single-flight locking, retry/backoff on 429/5xx, response parsing and key redaction.
- `sbomplus/ai.py`: AI proposal lane: builds a size-bounded evidence JSON from a project record, prompts EXAONE, runs a 4-layer validator (schema/rule/semantic/cross-field) over the JSON response, caches validated responses by prompt hash, and falls back to rule-based deterministic proposals when EXAONE is unavailable or its output doesn't validate.
- `sbomplus/rag.py`: Hybrid retrieval: bilingual (Korean/English) tokenizer with synonym expansion, heading-aware markdown chunker, BM25+TF-IDF index fused with Reciprocal Rank Fusion, an absolute-BM25 confidence gate, and a golden/dev/test-set evaluator (Recall@k, MRR, nDCG, OOD rejection).
- `sbomplus/chat.py`: Grounded chatbot: deterministic 'tools' that render facts straight out of an analysed project's record (overview/vulns/license/patch/arch/sector), combined with rag.Index retrieval over knowledge+evidence markdown; every answer is built from quote-verified claims, with an optional EXAONE rephrase pass gated by re-verification.
- `sbomplus/evals.py`: Offline evaluation harness: builds the knowledge index, runs rag.evaluate on the golden/dev/test query sets, and defines a fixed synthetic project + chat test cases used to score retrieval and chatbot grounding without any network access.
- `sbomplus/server.py`: Stdlib ThreadingHTTPServer exposing a minimal JSON API (POST /api/scan, GET /api/jobs/<id>, POST /api/chat, GET /api/projects, GET /api/health) plus a single hardcoded HTML page, wiring pipeline.run_project (scan jobs) and chat.Chatbot (Q&A) to HTTP, intended for localhost-only, unauthenticated use.

**데이터 흐름**: An HTTP POST to /api/scan (server.py) validates the source string via workspace.normalize_source, spawns a background thread that calls pipeline.run_project with only {patch, approved, use_llm} forwarded from the request body; that pipeline clones/copies the analysed (untrusted) source, runs SBOM/vuln/license/arch analysis, builds a remediation plan, optionally applies patches gated solely by the caller-supplied 'approved' boolean (no approval_token is ever obtained or forwarded by server.py), and calls ai.propose(). propose() turns the project record into a bounded evidence dict (vuln ids, component/package names, license categories, architecture hotspot file paths - all ultimately sourced from the untrusted repo) and sends it as the user prompt to llm.ChatClient.complete(); the raw JSON response is run through ai.validate()'s four layers before being trusted, with any failure/unavailability producing ai.deterministic() output labelled source='deterministic'. Results are persisted as evidence (markdown + JSON) readable by any later caller of GET /api/projects. POST /api/chat builds a chat.Chatbot over the same evidence plus the knowledge/ markdown corpus (rag.Index): deterministic 'tool' text for the matched project is combined with rag.Index.search() hits over knowledge/evidence chunks, every resulting claim is checked as a verbatim substring of its cited source, and only then (optionally) replaced by an EXAONE-rephrased answer that itself must pass a second verify_claims pass at >=0.8 accuracy. evals.py drives rag.evaluate()/chat.Chatbot end to end offline against fixed fixtures to produce the regression-gate metrics asserted by tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py.

**강점**:
- The deterministic/LLM source boundary in ai.py is consistently and verifiably honest: propose() only ever labels output source='exaone' when a response both parsed and survived all four validation layers; every other path is truthfully 'deterministic' with the cause recorded in llm_note (confirmed by code and by tests SP-AC-33/34/35/36/64).
- API-key redaction is applied to every exception message llm.py raises (one gap found only in the unused self.errors list), and a dedicated test (test_api_key_never_surfaces_in_errors) locks this in.
- Claim-level grounding in chat.py (verify_claims: exact, whitespace-normalised substring match) is simple, cheap, and well exercised by tests; it correctly rejects forged/translated quotes even when an EXAONE rephrase is used.
- The 4-layer proposal validator closes off hallucinated refs/actions/versions effectively for all structured fields (action enum, refs-must-name-real-evidence, upgrade target must equal the verified plan's safe version), matching its own docstring and tests precisely.
- server.py's source validation rejects dangerous git transport strings (ext::, fd::, leading '-', http://) before any subprocess call, and the HTTP API intentionally exposes no deliver_target/allow_push knob, so no HTTP-triggered job can push to a remote origin.

**설계상 약점**:
- The HTTP server's patch-approval surface is a single trusted boolean with no digest/token step, so exposing /api/scan at all collapses the CLI's two-phase 'review digest, then approve with matching token' design into a one-shot action - a deliberate simplification that silently drops the SP-AC-15 protection rather than surfacing that gap to callers.
- No CSRF/Origin defense anywhere in server.py, combined with Content-Type-agnostic body parsing, means the 'no auth, localhost only' threat model implicitly assumes network isolation is the only attacker model, ignoring that any webpage opened in the same browser while the server runs can reach it.
- Free-text LLM output (title/rationale/summary, and the risk label) is validated only for type/length/enum membership, never cross-checked against the evidence it is supposed to describe - the validator's 'cross-field' layer only polices action/refs/target_version, leaving the human-readable narrative unconstrained.
- Chatbot project selection (_pick_project) has no explicit 'unknown project' outcome: an unmatched project id is treated identically to 'no project specified', silently falling back to the single loaded project or a name-matched project instead of surfacing the mismatch.
- The per-day LLM call ledger is a plain read-modify-write JSON file with no locking, so it is only correct for a single process at a time, despite the server and CLI both being able to point at the same ledger path concurrently.
- The RAG confidence-gate threshold (min_bm25=11.0) was tuned empirically against the shipped tokenizer; because that tokenizer has a term-frequency-doubling bug for common 2-character Korean tokens, the threshold and the golden/dev/test metrics it was tuned against describe the buggy scorer's behaviour rather than a clean lexical-overlap signal, making future re-tuning (if the tokenizer is fixed) necessary but currently undocumented as a risk.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py exercises most of this slice end to end against a fake LLM transport (conftest.py's `world` fixture, no network): llm.py's 429 day-limit short-circuit, retry/ledger counting, key redaction, and single-flight locking (SP-AC-33/36/37/62/63); ai.py's 4-layer validator, ref-repair, prompt-hash caching, and truncated-response handling (SP-AC-34/35/64/65); rag.py's golden and held-out test-set thresholds (SP-AC-38/39/61); chat.py's tool-citation accuracy, forged-claim rejection, and EXAONE-claim bracket/translation handling (SP-AC-40/41/67). tests/sbomplus/test_server_cli.py exercises server.py's unsafe-source rejection, /api/chat happy path, /api/projects listing, and `cli.py evalrag` (SP-AC-55/56/57) against a real ThreadingHTTPServer on an ephemeral port with a FakeScanner. evals.py has no dedicated test file but is driven directly by the above. None of the existing tests exercise: patch:true+approved:true together over the HTTP API (so the approval/digest bypass is untested), any Origin/Content-Type/CSRF handling, a malformed or negative Content-Length, Chatbot.ask with an unmatched project id, or the Hangul-tokenizer duplicate-count behaviour (the golden/dev/test thresholds currently pass with the bug present, so it is invisible to the test suite as written).

**열린 질문**:
- Was the RAG confidence threshold (min_bm25=11.0) and the recall/mrr/ndcg/found_rate figures quoted in docs/SBOMPLUS.md measured with the _hangul_tokens duplicate-count bug already present? If so, fixing the tokenizer will require re-tuning the threshold and re-measuring the golden/dev/test numbers, not just a drop-in fix.
- Is the HTTP API's omission of an approval_token parameter an intentional simplification (treating remote 'approved: true' as sufficient authority) or an oversight relative to the CLI's two-step review flow and SP-AC-15? If intentional, should the docstring/spec say so explicitly rather than implying the same approval-gating applies everywhere?
- Is `sbomplus serve` ever expected to run on a machine where other, less-trusted local webpages/processes are present (the realistic CSRF/DoS threat model), or is 'localhost only' treated as an absolute boundary in deployment? That changes how urgently the CSRF/Origin and negative-Content-Length gaps need fixing.
- Does any planned feature (verbose CLI output, an evidence dump of LLM diagnostics) intend to surface ChatClient.errors to users? If so the redaction gap at llm.py:124 would need to be closed before that ships.

### plus-pipeline-loop-cli — sbomplus: pipeline, batch, loop harness, looplog, speclint, CLI

**목적**: This slice is the orchestration layer of sbomplus: pipeline.py runs one project end-to-end through bts_sbom-backed analysis stages plus the approval-gated patch/re-scan loop; batch.py fans that out over many sources and aggregates metrics; loop.py runs one "loop-engineering" pass (golden batch + RAG/chat eval), gates it against the last accepted baseline, and clusters failures to propose the next variable; looplog.py renders the accumulated loop history as Markdown; speclint.py is a small EARS-shape + two-way traceability linter for specs/sbomplus_features.md; cli.py is the argparse front end wiring all of the above (scan/patch/arch/batch/loop/evalrag/looplog/ragloop/chat/serve) to console output and process exit codes.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: run_project(): per-stage try/except isolation (_Stages), builds components/vulns/license/plans/SBOM docs/arch/proposals, drives _patch_loop() (approval-gated multi-round patch+rescan+revert), computes metrics() and persist()s evidence JSON + cdx/spdx/vex
- `sbomplus/batch.py`: run_batch(): runs pipeline.run_project over a source list (optionally threaded), aggregate(): rolls per-project metrics into rates/counts, writes summary.json/md/html + per-project reports
- `sbomplus/loop.py`: flatten(): batch summary + rag/chat eval -> flat metric dict; gate(): accept/rollback decision vs. last accepted baseline; plateau(): spinning guard; cluster_failures(): groups failure causes; propose_variables(): EXAONE-or-deterministic next-step hypotheses; run_pass()/run_rag_pass(): orchestrate + persist history.json/rag_history.json
- `sbomplus/looplog.py`: render(): turns evidence/loop/history.json + rag_history.json into a Markdown table of passes and metric deltas
- `sbomplus/speclint.py`: parse()/lint(): EARS-shape+'shall'+digit checks on specs/sbomplus_features.md; trace(): two-way AC-id <-> test-docstring reference check; __main__ CLI entry
- `sbomplus/cli.py`: argparse subcommands (scan/patch/arch/batch/loop/evalrag/looplog/ragloop/chat/serve), _settings()/_client() wiring, console printing (_print, ASCII-safe), process exit codes
- `sbomplus/workspace.py (read for context, not in slice)`: source acquisition / work-copy creation; _unique_dest() readonly-safe delete-and-reuse (AC-68 fix location)
- `sbomplus/sbom.py (read for context, not in slice)`: validate_cyclonedx()/ref_integrity() - corroborates the loop-gate blind-spot finding (empty SBOM validates as 'perfect')

**데이터 흐름**: cli.py parses args -> builds a settings dict (_settings) and an optional ChatClient (_client) -> for scan/patch: pipeline.run_project(one source) -> workspace.prepare -> engine.analyze -> licenses/remediate/sbom/arch/patch(_patch_loop)/ai.propose -> metrics()/persist() -> printed + exit code. For batch/loop: cli reads sources_json (read_json with a silent [] default) -> batch.run_batch() calls pipeline.run_project per source (serial or ThreadPoolExecutor) -> aggregate() -> summary dict + per-project records written to evidence/batch/runs/*; loop.py additionally calls evals.rag_eval/chat_eval, flattens everything via flatten(), compares to evidence/loop/history.json's last accepted entry via gate(), clusters failures, asks for next hypotheses, and appends/writes a new pass entry. looplog.render() only reads those history.json files back into Markdown. speclint only reads specs/sbomplus_features.md and tests/ source text; it has no runtime coupling to the pipeline/batch/loop code path.

**강점**:
- Per-stage isolation in pipeline.run_project (_Stages.run) genuinely works at the inter-stage level: a failing licenses/arch/proposals/patch stage is recorded in stage_errors and the rest of the pipeline still runs, verified by reading every S.run call site.
- The approval-gate (patch applied only when approved=True and, after round 1, only with a token matching the plan digest) and the regression-revert path are real: _patch_loop scopes regression detection to files actually edited (documented fix for the 134-package false-revert case) and calls patchmod.revert on real regressions.
- speclint's lint()+trace() genuinely enforce what they claim: verified by executing them against the live specs/sbomplus_features.md and tests/sbomplus - 0 lint problems, 0 untested ACs, 0 dangling test references, 68 ACs.
- The documented AC-68 fixes (workspace._unique_dest force-remove of read-only git objects, and loop/batch correctly threading --work through to pipeline.run_project) are both present in the current code and covered by a passing test (test_existing_workcopy_with_readonly_files_is_replaced_without_leaks).

**설계상 약점**:
- The loop gate only ever compares metrics that are HIGHER/LOWER-listed AND non-None in both base and candidate; absolute volume metrics (components, vulns) are carried as informational-only and never gated, and several rate metrics silently become None (and get skipped) when their denominator is 0 - so there is no metric that reliably fails a pass in which the engine's component/vulnerability detection itself collapses to zero without raising an exception.
- cli.py's exit-code discipline is inconsistent across subcommands: scan/patch (2 on error), arch (2 on error), loop/ragloop (1 on rollback), evalrag (1 on miss) all propagate failure, but batch always returns 0 regardless of how many of its projects errored.
- No cleanup path exists anywhere in pipeline.py/batch.py/cli.py for the work copies pipeline.run_project creates; this is bounded for batch/loop (by-name reuse via workspace._unique_dest) but unbounded for the single-project scan/patch/arch CLI commands, which call tempfile.mkdtemp() fresh on every invocation.
- batch.run_batch's own per-source loop (the `one()` closure) is not exception-isolated the way pipeline.run_project's internal stages are: a malformed entry in the user-supplied sources_json (wrong key name, non-dict item) raises inside batch.py itself and aborts the whole batch/loop run, losing every project's results rather than recording one bad entry.
- loop.plateau() demands byte-identical JSON across the full metrics dict (including the wall-clock analysis_s_avg field) for 3 consecutive accepted passes, which is a fragile basis for 'no more progress possible' given that timing is explicitly called out elsewhere in the same file as advisory/noisy.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_specs.py exercises speclint.lint/trace directly against the real spec and passes. tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py unit-tests loop.gate(), loop.plateau() and loop.propose_variables() in isolation with synthetic dicts (SP-AC-42/43/44), but never with a metrics vector containing None values or an absolute-count collapse, so the gate blind-spot above is untested. tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py and test_server_cli.py call pipeline.run_project directly (not through cli.py or batch.py) and cover the approval-gate, digest-mismatch, regression-revert/scoped-regression, and AC-68 workspace paths reasonably well. Confirmed by grep across tests/: there is zero test coverage for batch.aggregate()/batch.run_batch(), loop.run_pass()/loop.run_rag_pass(), loop.cluster_failures(), loop.flatten(), and looplog.render() - none of these functions are called anywhere in the test suite. cli.main() is exercised by exactly one test (test_cli_evalrag_exits_zero, AC: SP-AC-57) covering only the 'evalrag' subcommand; scan/patch/arch/batch/loop/looplog/ragloop/chat/serve are never driven through cli.main()'s actual argparse + exit-code layer, which is why the batch-always-exits-0 and silent-empty-sources-json defects above have no regression test to catch them.

**열린 질문**:
- Is the loop gate intended to also catch absolute-count collapses (components/vulns dropping to 0), or is that explicitly out of scope because project_success_rate is assumed to always catch it? The author's own comment marks exaone_rate/ai_keep_rate as 'informational, not gated' but gives no equivalent note for components/vulns, so it's unclear whether the omission is deliberate.
- Is the single-project CLI path (scan/patch/arch without --work) expected to be used repeatedly/in CI, where the unreclaimed tempfile.mkdtemp() leak would matter, or is it assumed to be an interactive one-off command where manual cleanup is acceptable?
- Should `sbomplus batch`'s exit code reflect per-project failure the way loop/ragloop/evalrag do, or is batch intentionally meant to always succeed as long as the process itself didn't crash (treating all per-project outcomes as data, not process failure)?
- Is there a planned/expected caller that validates sources_json shape before batch.run_batch sees it (so the one() closure's lack of exception isolation never actually matters in practice), e.g. a schema check in a wrapper script not present in this repo?

### tests-ci-packaging — Tests, CI, packaging, release hygiene

**목적**: This slice is the quality/release-engineering harness around the two-package monorepo: the pytest suites for bts_sbom (28 tests) and sbomplus (69 tests), the two GitHub Actions workflows, the two pyproject.toml build configs, the sbomplus Dockerfile, and the top-level/nested CONTRIBUTING/SECURITY/CHANGELOG docs. It is what proves (or fails to prove) that the honesty-discipline claims made elsewhere in the repo actually hold, and what a contributor or CI runner actually experiences when building, testing, linting and packaging the project.

**구성 요소**:
- `tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py`: bts_sbom core: CLI subprocess + library behavioral tests (import surface, exit codes, SBOM/sector/scan/vex/policy round-trips, version-match/remediation unit tables, cross-platform BOM/path handling) - 28 tests
- `tests/sbomplus/conftest.py`: shared fakes: FakeScanner (offline OSV-shaped results), FakeWorld.http/llm (raise AssertionError on any unmodeled URL), autouse no_network fixture, make_repo git fixture factory
- `tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py`: EXAONE LLM lane (budget/rate-limit/serialization/secret-redaction), RAG retrieval + chatbot grounding/citation, loop-engineering gate/plateau tests
- `tests/sbomplus/test_engine_adjustments.py`: engine.normalize() edge cases: cross-manifest de-dup, Ruby platform-variant merge, go.sum MVS filtering, malware-advisory severity
- `tests/sbomplus/test_engine_licenses.py`: engine.analyze(), cvss scoring, versions.compare, license normalization/provenance/caching tests
- `tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py`: workspace safety (unsafe source rejection), approval-gated patch apply/block/revert, delivery targets (inplace/github/folder)
- `tests/sbomplus/test_sbom_sectors_arch.py`: CycloneDX/SPDX document + industry-report + architecture (hotspots/cycles/EOL) tests
- `tests/sbomplus/test_server_cli.py`: stdlib HTTP API (ThreadingHTTPServer) and CLI entry point tests
- `tests/sbomplus/test_specs.py`: EARS spec lint + bidirectional AC<->test traceability check
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: bts_sbom core CI: 3-OS x 2-Python matrix, ruff, pytest, self-SBOM/SBOM/policy smoke demos
- `.github/workflows/sbomplus_ci.yml`: sbomplus layer CI: 2-OS x 2-Python matrix, pytest tests/sbomplus, speclint, evalrag
- `pyproject.toml`: monorepo build config: both packages, two console-script entry points, ruff config
- `bts_sbom/pyproject.toml`: staged standalone-package build config for a future bts_sbom-only extraction
- `Dockerfile.sbomplus`: containerized sbomplus HTTP service (self-declared unbuilt)
- `.gitignore`: excludes caches, OSV sqlite cache, work copies, LLM ledger, batch run artifacts
- `CHANGELOG.md`: root release notes (BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 Unreleased + 0.1.0; no 0.1.1 entry)
- `bts_sbom/CHANGELOG.md`: nested release notes, correctly includes the 0.1.1 engine-round-2 entry
- `CONTRIBUTING.md / bts_sbom/CONTRIBUTING.md`: identical contributor workflow docs, not updated since before the BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 merge
- `SECURITY.md / bts_sbom/SECURITY.md`: identical vulnerability-disclosure policy, scoped only to the bts_sbom package

**데이터 흐름**: Tests drive the code two ways: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py shells out to `python -m bts_sbom` via subprocess and asserts on stdout/exit codes; tests/sbomplus/* import sbomplus modules directly and monkeypatch sbomplus.net.TRANSPORT / sbomplus.llm.TRANSPORT to FakeWorld callables so no real socket is ever opened (an autouse fixture makes every test fail loudly on an unmodeled URL). CI installs the packages (editable) then runs ruff/pytest/scripted smoke demos; the packaging config (pyproject.toml, bts_sbom/pyproject.toml) is consumed by `pip install`/`docker build` to turn the source tree into installable artifacts - which is where I found the gap: sbomplus's own runtime code (evals.py/rag.py) assumes `knowledge/` and `evals/` sit as siblings of the installed `sbomplus` package, but the packaging config never ships them, so a real (non-editable) install silently breaks that assumption.

**강점**:
- Test assertions are concrete and behavioral throughout (exact version strings, file contents, exit codes, branch names, HTTP status codes) rather than smoke-level 'no exception raised'; README's two checkable test counts (28 core, 69 sbomplus) are exactly accurate, confirmed via `pytest --collect-only`.
- Every sbomplus test cites a specific EARS AC id, and test_specs.py programmatically enforces full bidirectional AC<->test traceability (confirmed passing when run); the 68-AC count in specs/CHANGELOG matches an independent count of distinct SP-AC ids.
- conftest.py's FakeWorld.http/llm raise AssertionError on any URL not explicitly modeled, and an autouse fixture applies this to every test by default - a strong, hard-to-defeat enforcement of the 'no real network' discipline at test time.
- The Windows-specific regression test for read-only git object files during work-copy replacement (AC-68) actually passes when executed on this Windows machine, not just assumed.
- sbomplus_ci.yml's non-pytest steps (speclint, evalrag) reproduce correctly when run directly, and sbomplus itself is genuinely stdlib-only (verified independently with an AST import scan), consistent with the zero-dependency claim.

**설계상 약점**:
- Two pyproject.toml files carry independent, unsynchronized version fields with no CI check tying them together - a structural invitation for exactly the drift found (root at 0.1.1, nested at 0.1.0).
- Root-level CONTRIBUTING.md/SECURITY.md/CHANGELOG.md were copied once from the nested bts_sbom/ originals and then never revisited as the repo grew a second, larger package (sbomplus) with its own attack surface and its own CI workflow; nothing enforces that the root docs cover both packages.
- sbomplus has no CI lint step in either workflow, and no dedicated stdlib-only regression test (unlike bts_sbom, which has both) - its cleanliness is accidental/unverified rather than enforced.
- Packaging (`[tool.setuptools.packages.find]`) is purely Python-package-oriented; it has no mechanism for the data directories (`knowledge/`, `evals/`) that sbomplus's own code depends on via relative-sibling-path convention, so the packaging/runtime coupling is invisible until someone actually builds and installs the wheel.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py (28 tests) covers import surface, CLI exit-code contract, SBOM/sector/scan/vex/policy round-trips, version-matching, remediation, transitive-dependency resolution, and cross-platform path/encoding handling - all with meaningful, value-level assertions. It only existence-checks (never behaviorally exercises) reachability.py, supply_chain.py, and _internal/{callgraph,points_to,sarif,epss_kev,epss_offline}.py. tests/sbomplus/ (69 tests across 7 files + conftest) covers engine normalization/adjustments, CVSS/versions/license resolution, patch/delivery/revert, SBOM/industry-report/architecture analysis, the EXAONE/RAG/chat/loop stack, the HTTP API/CLI, and spec traceability - every test is tied to a specific EARS AC id and verified by a dedicated traceability test. Zero tests (direct or indirect) reference sbomplus/batch.py, sbomplus/looplog.py, or sbomplus/report.py. I independently ran single representative tests (test_specs.py, test_existing_workcopy_with_readonly_files_is_replaced_without_leaks) and the two non-pytest sbomplus_ci.yml steps, all passing on this Windows machine.

**열린 질문**:
- Is README.md's '42 tests' for sectors.py a copy/paste or stale number, or does it refer to a counting method not reflected anywhere in tests/ or bts_sbom/sectors.py?
- Will bts_sbom/pyproject.toml's version and the root CHANGELOG.md's missing 0.1.1 entry be reconciled before any real/standalone publish of bts_sbom, given that release/v0.1.2 (a separate worktree, not yet merged to main) already fixes the version single-sourcing problem and the cp949 crash?
- Is the untracked commit.tar file sitting in the release/v0.1.2 worktree (C:/firebaseprojects/_rel_bts_sbom_012/commit.tar) meaningful leftover or disposable local debris?
- Is sbomplus_ci.yml's 'not yet run on GitHub' status because the branch/repo isn't pushed yet, or a deliberate hold pending review - and will its lint coverage gap for sbomplus/ be addressed before it is enabled?
- Does the project intend root-level CONTRIBUTING.md/SECURITY.md to eventually cover sbomplus explicitly, or is sbomplus expected to grow its own separate contributing/security docs the way bts_sbom has its own nested copies?

### docs-data-evidence — Docs, specs, knowledge corpus, evals, evidence, tutorials

**목적**: This slice is the project's documentation, knowledge/eval corpus and measured-evidence trail for a two-package SBOM/SCA toolkit: README/docs/specs describe and promise an honesty-first design (absent evidence never a pass, VEX fail-closed, every number measured), knowledge/+evals/ feed and grade the grounded RAG chatbot, and evidence/batch+evidence/loop are the raw JSON that the docs' prose numbers are supposed to be transcriptions of. Its job is to let a reader verify the tool's claims against reproducible evidence rather than take them on faith, and tutorials/examples exist to teach the underlying bts_sbom CLI hands-on.

**구성 요소**:
- `README.md`: top-level entry point: quickstart, 6-sector matrix, honesty discipline, implementation-status table, BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 summary with headline e2e numbers
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 pipeline diagram and 5 design rules (single adapter, explicit outward-action flags, absent-evidence-never-pass, raw-evidence caching, untrusted-repo read-only)
- `docs/SBOMPLUS.md`: canonical BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 doc: limitations-first, full 10-project e2e metrics table, loop-engineering summary table, CLI usage, honesty contract, cross-links to spec/tests/knowledge/evals
- `docs/CORE_ISSUES.md`: catalog of 4 unfixed bts_sbom core defects found while building BTS-SBOM 앱 계층, each with its sbomplus-side workaround and owning test
- `docs/LOOP_LOG.md`: pass-by-pass record of 15 e2e loop passes and 4 retrieval-only passes, one variable per pass, metric deltas and accept/rollback decisions
- `docs/LECTURE_MAPPING.md`: traceability table from lecture RAG/agent/SDD techniques to implementation status (구현/부분/미구현/해당없음/적용불가) with code pointers
- `specs/sbomplus_features.md`: EARS-style SDD spec, 68 acceptance criteria (SP-AC-1..68) each with a measured number, grouped by module
- `knowledge/domain/*.md (8 files)`: RAG knowledge base: licenses, vulnerabilities, remediation/patching, legacy architecture, SBOM formats, industries, AI/loop, CLI usage
- `knowledge/lecture/*.md (6 files)`: paraphrased lecture-methodology notes (RAG methods, eval metrics, SDD/requirements, chatbot/agents, dataops/MLOps loop) feeding the same RAG index
- `evals/rag_golden.json, rag_dev.json, rag_test.json`: 25/20/16-query retrieval+OOD fixtures (golden=regression set, dev=tuned on, test=untouched) consumed by evals.rag_eval/rag_sets_eval
- `evals/e2e_sources.json`: the 10 real GitHub sources (+ depth/notes) driving every batch/loop e2e pass
- `evidence/batch/latest.json, evidence/batch/runs/*/summary.{json,md,html}`: 15 historical batch-run snapshots (one per loop pass), each with aggregate metrics and per-project JSON/MD/SBOM/VEX artifacts; latest.json mirrors the final accepted pass p15
- `evidence/loop/pass_NN_*.json, history.json, rag_history.json`: raw per-pass metrics/decisions/misses that LOOP_LOG.md's prose tables are manually transcribed from
- `tutorials/01_sbom_basics.md, 02_vulnerability_scan.md, 03_sector_compliance.md`: Korean workshop tutorials walking through bts-sbom CLI (sbom/scan/policy/sector) using examples/
- `examples/requirements.txt, demo.vex.json, demo.eol.json`: small sample inputs referenced by the tutorials to build and evaluate a demo SBOM

**데이터 흐름**: batch.run_batch/loop.run_pass execute the real pipeline over evals/e2e_sources.json and write per-project + aggregate JSON into evidence/batch/runs/<ts>_<tag>/ (mirrored to evidence/batch/latest.json) and evidence/loop/pass_NN_*.json + history.json; evals.rag_eval/chat_eval run the same loop against evals/rag_*.json and the knowledge/ corpus (chunked by rag.chunk_markdown) and are logged to evidence/loop/rag_history.json. Humans then hand-transcribe selected numbers from these JSON files into the prose tables of README.md, docs/SBOMPLUS.md and docs/LOOP_LOG.md — there is no automated check that the transcription stays correct, unlike specs/sbomplus_features.md's ACs which are mechanically linted and traced to tests/sbomplus/*.py by sbomplus.speclint. tutorials/ and examples/ sit outside this loop entirely: they describe the bts_sbom core CLI directly and are not exercised by batch/loop/tests at all, so they can drift silently from both the code and from each other.

**강점**:
- Nearly every headline number in README/SBOMPLUS.md/LOOP_LOG.md was independently reproduced from evidence/batch/latest.json, evidence/loop/pass_*.json and evidence/loop/rag_history.json by direct inspection/execution (components, vulns, severity split, license source breakdown, plan/patch stats, EXAONE keep rate, golden/dev/test recall and found-rate, and the flask per-industry pass/gap/not_evidence breakdown) — the project's 'measured, not aspirational' claim holds up under spot-checking far better than typical.
- Honesty mechanics are implemented in code, not just asserted in prose: the empty-manifest project (iso14229) is tagged data_status=empty_project/vulnerability_presence=unknown rather than silently reporting zero vulnerabilities, confirmed by reading the actual evidence record.
- EARS spec traceability (specs/sbomplus_features.md <-> tests/sbomplus/*.py) is mechanically enforced by sbomplus.speclint and tests/test_specs.py, not just claimed; running it confirms all 68 ACs are well-formed and bidirectionally traced.
- The docs are unusually self-critical: SBOMPLUS.md's 한계 section, CORE_ISSUES.md and LECTURE_MAPPING.md all volunteer unflattering facts (in-sample keep-rate, optimistic golden recall, 4 unimplemented lecture techniques, maritime profile being a draft) instead of omitting them.
- knowledge/ RAG corpus is small (500 lines across 14 files) and shows no detected verbatim leakage of eval questions, so the reported Recall@5=1.0 on the golden set is not trivially explained by the corpus literally containing the questions.

**설계상 약점**:
- Numeric claims in README/docs/SBOMPLUS.md are hand-transcribed from evidence JSON with no automated cross-check (unlike the spec<->test traceability, which is linted); this has already let two count mismatches ship (CORE_ISSUES.md documents 4 defects vs. SBOMPLUS.md's '3건', and README's 'sectors.py ... 42 tests' vs. an actual 28-test core suite).
- tutorials/ and examples/ are entirely outside the test/loop/spec harness, so nothing guarantees the shipped example inputs (examples/demo.vex.json, demo.eol.json) stay consistent with each other or with the example manifest (examples/requirements.txt) used alongside them in the same walkthrough.
- evidence/ retains full per-project SBOM/VEX/CycloneDX/SPDX output for all 15 historical loop passes (150+ per-project files) with no visible pruning/retention policy, and a stray undocumented dotfile (evidence/loop/.code_p02) sits among the otherwise consistently-named pass artifacts.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py (28 tests) covers the bts_sbom core CLI/sectors/sbom/scan but nothing in docs/specs/knowledge/evidence. tests/sbomplus/test_specs.py mechanically enforces specs/sbomplus_features.md's EARS shape and bidirectional test traceability (68/68 ACs covered, confirmed by running speclint directly). tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py's test_golden_retrieval_thresholds and test_untouched_test_set_answerable_rate_and_ood run the real evals/rag_golden.json and evals/rag_test.json against the real knowledge/ corpus (not mocked), so those fixtures and the corpus are genuinely exercised. Nothing in the test suite checks that README/docs/SBOMPLUS.md/LOOP_LOG.md prose numbers match evidence/batch or evidence/loop, and nothing checks tutorials/ or examples/ against the live CLI or against each other — both gaps are where the confirmed defects above were found.

**열린 질문**:
- Is docs/SBOMPLUS.md's '3건' core-defect count simply a stale/typo count, or was defect #4 (cp949 crash) deliberately excluded because its workaround lives in sbomplus's print layer rather than being a true engine-level bypass like #1-3?
- Was README's 'sectors.py ... 42 tests' ever accurate at some earlier point (e.g. before test consolidation into the single tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py file), and should it be corrected to the actual sector-specific test count?
- The task brief states evidence/batch contains 196 markdown files; the actual count found is 165 (15 summary.md + 150 per-project .md across 15 runs) — was evidence/batch pruned since that figure was computed, or is 196 simply inaccurate?
- Should tutorials/ and examples/ get even a lightweight automated check (e.g. a doctest-style CLI smoke test) given specs/ already enforces this kind of consistency for the BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 layer but not for the bts_sbom core tutorials?

### lens-honesty — Cross-cutting: does the honesty contract actually hold?

**목적**: Cross-cutting audit of whether the repository's own stated "honesty contract" (absent evidence is never a pass; no-data != no-vulnerability; VEX fail-closed; reachability UNKNOWN never treated as safe; offline values stay NOASSERTION; untrusted-repo isolation; explicit flags for outward actions; patch approval-gating) actually holds in code, across both bts_sbom (the core SBOM/SCA/VEX/reachability/sector-report toolkit) and sbomplus (the adapter/orchestration layer built on top of it). The review enumerates every place a verdict/pass/clean/resolved value is produced and tries concrete, executed counterexamples with faked/edge-case inputs (empty ecosystems, duplicate CVE ids, malformed VEX, symlinked work copies, missing loop metrics) rather than relying on the project's own tests or documentation.

**구성 요소**:
- `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py`: Core OSV scan engine: ecosystem normalisation, per-component query_status, completeness/data_status/vulnerability_presence assembly, gate_exit_code(); the single most load-bearing honesty-contract implementation in the repo.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py`: Low-level OSV.dev HTTP client; defines its own (different) OSS-Fuzz catch-all ecosystem map used elsewhere in the core.
- `bts_sbom/vex.py`: OpenVEX generation/consumption: statement validation (_validate_statement, fail-closed), VexIndex suppression lookups, CycloneDX/OpenVEX ingestion (load_vex_file).
- `bts_sbom/reachability.py`: C/C++ function-level reachability engine (REACHABLE/UNREACHABLE/REACHABILITY_UNKNOWN) and enrich_sca_vulns() public API that attaches verdicts to vuln dicts.
- `bts_sbom/sectors.py`: 6-sector regulatory report engine producing pass/gap/not_evidence verdicts per requirement, each with an evidence block.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/policy.py`: Policy gate: findings/components/VEX evaluation, vex_evaluate() fail-closed parsing of VEX documents for CI policy decisions.
- `bts_sbom/cli.py`: bts-sbom CLI: wires gate_exit_code, policy evaluate, and the 'vex --check' validate-and-summarize command.
- `sbomplus/engine.py`: The single documented adapter between sbomplus and bts_sbom core; normalize() carries completeness.data_status/vulnerability_presence through verbatim; fixed_versions() avoids the core's GIT-range remediation bug.
- `sbomplus/workspace.py`: Source acquisition / isolation: clones git sources (symlinks neutralised via core.symlinks=false) or copies local folders (shutil.copytree(symlinks=True) -- the isolation gap).
- `sbomplus/patch.py`: Approval-gated manifest patcher; apply() is the sink that turns a symlinked manifest inside the work copy into an arbitrary-file-write primitive.
- `sbomplus/remediate.py`: Remediation plan + OSV re-verification (plan/verify); verification.clean stays None on OSV-unreachable, never treated as clean.
- `sbomplus/osv.py`: Direct OSV.dev batch client used by remediate.verify(); query_batch()'s single-query pagination fallback does not itself paginate.
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: Per-project pipeline orchestration: patch loop with scoped regression detection (edited-files-only comparison), origin_unchanged tree-hash check.
- `sbomplus/loop.py`: Engineering-quality regression gate (gate()/plateau()/cluster_failures()) comparing a candidate pass's metrics against an accepted baseline; silently skips metrics missing from either vector.
- `sbomplus/licenses.py`: License resolution with provenance (lockfile -> deps.dev -> registry) and TTL'd raw-evidence cache; correctly re-tries on stale/under-powered cache misses.
- `sbomplus/sectors.py / sbomplus/report.py`: BTS-SBOM 앱 계층 wrappers around bts_sbom.sectors for per-industry reporting inside the pipeline record.

**데이터 흐름**: User input (CLI path/URL, or a crafted SBOM/VEX/scan-result JSON) -> bts_sbom.osvscanner.discover()/_parse_sbom ingests manifests/lockfiles/SBOMs into packages with an ecosystem tag (defaulting to the OSV catch-all 'OSS-Fuzz' when unknown) -> OsvScanner.scan() queries OSV.dev or the offline DB per component, producing a per-component query_status that is rolled up into a single completeness block (data_status, vulnerability_presence) -> consumers (gate_exit_code, sca_gate_check, policy.evaluate+vex_evaluate, sectors.sector_report, vex.from_scan_result) read that completeness block as the ultimate source of truth for pass/fail/suppress decisions, without re-deriving whether an 'answered' status was actually meaningful. In sbomplus, workspace.prepare() turns a user-given source into a 'work copy' that engine.scan()/engine.normalize() feed into licenses.resolve(), remediate.plan()/verify(), patch.apply(), arch.analyze() and sectors/report; engine.normalize() passes bts_sbom's data_status/vulnerability_presence straight through unchanged. patch.apply() writes into files addressed by path relative to the work copy root -- if that root contains a real OS symlink (possible only for the local-folder ingestion path), writes land outside the isolated copy. loop.py's gate()/run_pass() sits one level further out, gating the ENGINEERING PROCESS itself (whether a code change is "accepted") by comparing metrics computed from full pipeline runs against a historical baseline.

**강점**:
- The core's completeness/data_status/vulnerability_presence contract (osvscanner.py) is unusually well specified and, for the ordinary online/offline/network-failure/version-unresolved/parse-failure paths actually tested, behaves exactly as documented -- a solid foundation that the task's other reviewers' sector/VEX/report findings likely build on.
- VEX statement construction (vex.statement/build_document/generate_openvex) and the policy-gate's vex_evaluate() are genuinely fail-closed: a malformed or non-OpenVEX VEX document fed to the CI policy path is rejected outright and every finding becomes 'unmatched', never silently suppressed.
- reachability.py's per-verdict completeness reasoning (callback candidates, same-name collisions, declared-vs-analyzed translation units) is unusually rigorous and correctly keeps UNKNOWN instead of asserting UNREACHABLE whenever any completeness assumption is unmet.
- sbomplus/licenses.py's cache correctly re-attempts resolution when the cache entry predates the currently-enabled fallbacks or has expired past ttl_s, rather than trusting a stale 'no evidence' answer indefinitely.
- patch.py and deliver.py gate every outward/irreversible action (approval token/digest, allow_origin_write, allow_push) before doing anything, and never silently force a blocked upgrade.
- The 'only one adapter touches the core' architectural claim is actually true, verified structurally across the whole sbomplus package, not just asserted in docs.

**설계상 약점**:
- The 'meaningless answered-zero' problem with the OSS-Fuzz catch-all ecosystem is explicitly recognised in a code comment (osvscanner.py:1176-1180) but the fix (fw-distro fallback retry) is gated on the component carrying 'osv_ecosystems' candidates; ordinary non-firmware components that merely lack a purl fall through unmitigated into the completeness contract as if genuinely answered.
- Verdict/status vocabularies get reused for materially different situations without a sub-field distinguishing them in the common case: patch._patch_loop's top-level status 'applied' covers both a rescan-verified fix and a manifest-only edit the scanner's own lockfile reading cannot confirm (distinguishable only by also reading rescan_fixed_total/note).
- enrich_sca_vulns()'s per-CVE-id keying (rather than per-(cve_id,package,version)) is a systemic design flaw, not just an edge case: any vuln list with a CVE affecting multiple components (common for widely-shared CVEs) silently corrupts verdicts for all but one of them.
- osv.py's single-query pagination fallback assumes one extra page is always enough; there is no loop to drain further next_page_token values.
- sectors._check_vex_present only checks that a VEX document is non-empty, not that its statements are individually well-formed or correlate with the SBOM's own findings -- a narrower check than its name/placement in a regulatory 'vulnerability handling' row might suggest to a report reader.
- No test anywhere exercises the OSS-Fuzz/no-purl SBOM ingestion path, the local-folder symlink path, or enrich_sca_vulns()'s actual verdict output (only its existence in __all__ is checked) -- exactly the three areas where concrete defects were found.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py only asserts that `enrich_sca_vulns`, `VexIndex`, `load_vex_file`, `parse_document` and `VexError` exist as exported names (via a `__all__`/attribute presence loop) -- none of their actual verdict behaviour is exercised, so the reachability CVE-collision bug and the VEX-validation gap are both invisible to the existing suite. tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py tests loop.gate() only for the identical-metrics pass and a strictly-worse pass; it never exercises a candidate with a metric key missing relative to the baseline. tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py and test_engine_*.py cover workspace.prepare() for git/local sources and patch.apply()'s manifest-editing logic in depth, but no test creates a symlink inside a local source folder, so the shutil.copytree(symlinks=True) isolation gap has zero coverage. No test anywhere constructs an SBOM component with a missing purl / empty ecosystem to exercise the OSS-Fuzz catch-all path in osvscanner.py, and no test checks gate_exit_code()/sca_gate_check() against that scenario. CORE_ISSUES.md's two documented core-defect workarounds (GIT-range fixed_versions, SPDX parenthesis stripping) are each backed by a named, existing regression test, which is the one area where a known honesty-adjacent gap is both disclosed and concretely tested.

**열린 질문**:
- Does any real analysed repository in evals/e2e_sources.json actually ship a committed *.cdx.json/*.spdx.json/bom.json (or does any component in those SBOMs lack a purl)? If so the OSS-Fuzz false-clean defect may already be silently affecting the batch/loop evidence in evidence/batch and evidence/loop and their claimed component/vulnerability counts.
- Is workspace.prepare()'s local-folder path (shutil.copytree(symlinks=True)) actually reachable from the documented CLI entry points (`python run_sbomplus.py scan <folder>`, `patch <folder> --approve`) with attacker-supplied folders, or is local-folder analysis always assumed to be the user's own trusted project in practice? The code offers no guard either way, and the module's own docstring claims otherwise.
- Does any call site actually pass a vuln list with duplicate CVE ids across different packages to enrich_sca_vulns() today (the function is currently unused elsewhere in the repo), or is this a latent defect in an otherwise-dormant public API awaiting a future integration?
- Is the dangling `release_gate(extra_checks=[...])` reference in osvscanner.py a true leftover from the 'vendored from sca_guard' origin noted at the top of several core files, and does the real sca_guard release_gate (if it exists upstream) itself honour the same honesty contract?

### lens-security — Cross-cutting: handling of untrusted input and outward actions

**목적**: This cross-cutting review threat-models bts_sbom + sbomplus as a toolkit whose whole purpose is to point itself at hostile, untrusted repositories/documents (and, for sbomplus, to take gated outward actions like patching, pushing and opening PRs). It examines every place untrusted repo/document content is acquired, parsed, or scanned, and every place the tool reaches outward (subprocess, network, git push, HTTP server, filesystem writes outside the work copy, LLM prompts/outputs), to verify the project's own honesty/isolation claims hold under adversarial input.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/workspace.py`: Source acquisition: classifies/validates a git URL or local path and materializes an isolated work copy (hardened git clone, or shutil.copytree for local folders); origin is never touched
- `sbomplus/util.py`: Shared helpers: no-shell subprocess wrapper (run()), atomic JSON write, tree/content hashing, name sanitization (safe_name)
- `sbomplus/patch.py`: Approval-gated, per-ecosystem manifest text editors + apply()/revert()/diff(); never touches lockfiles, commits only on explicit approval
- `sbomplus/deliver.py`: Outward delivery targets (branch/folder/inplace/github+PR), each outward-facing target individually gated by an explicit boolean flag
- `sbomplus/server.py`: Stdlib HTTP JSON API + single-page UI; localhost-only by default, no authentication, delegates source validation to workspace.normalize_source
- `sbomplus/net.py`: Centralized outbound HTTP helper (urllib) with retry/backoff and request/failure accounting; all network egress funnels through here
- `sbomplus/llm.py`: EXAONE/OpenAI-compatible chat-completion client: config resolution, per-day call budget ledger, key redaction on raised errors
- `sbomplus/ai.py`: Builds a curated evidence JSON for the LLM, sends it, and runs a 4-layer validator (schema/rule/semantic/cross-field) on the response; deterministic fallback when unavailable or invalid
- `sbomplus/chat.py`: Grounded chatbot: deterministic project 'tools' + RAG retrieval produce claims; verify_claims() checks only that a quote is a verbatim substring of its cited document
- `sbomplus/rag.py`: BM25+TF-IDF hybrid retrieval index over knowledge/ markdown and per-project evidence markdown chunks, with a confidence gate for out-of-corpus questions
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: End-to-end per-project orchestration with per-stage error isolation; wires analyze -> license -> plan -> patch(approval-gated) -> deliver(flag-gated) -> proposals -> evidence persistence
- `sbomplus/cli.py`: Argument parsing; every outward-action flag (--allow-origin-write/--allow-push/--open-pr/--approve) defaults to False
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/__init__.py`: Manifest-parser dispatch by basename (and a fixed relpath-suffix allowlist for dpkg); parse_file() reads with no size cap
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/npm.py`: package.json/package-lock.json parsers; dependency NAME is taken verbatim from untrusted JSON keys with no format validation
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/extra.py`: Text/XML lockfile+manifest parsers for many ecosystems, including pom.xml via bare xml.etree.ElementTree.fromstring
- `bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/rpm.py`: Pure-Python RPM header + sqlite-rpmdb parser for extracted rootfs images; opens an untrusted sqlite file directly (not wired into the default scan path)
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_scan.py`: Full-source-tree license/copyright scanner: os.walk + open() on every non-binary-extension file up to a byte cap; transparently follows filesystem symlinks
- `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_local_db.py`: Local sqlite cache/offline mirror of OSV data; all queries are static/parameterized (no string-built SQL from untrusted values)
- `bts_sbom/reachability.py`: Pure regex/text based C/C++ call reachability (no compiler/subprocess invocation on untrusted source)
- `bts_sbom/_internal/callgraph.py`: Regex-driven C/C++ class/function/call-graph extraction over untrusted source text

**데이터 흐름**: A hostile git URL or local folder enters only through workspace.normalize_source()/prepare(), which is meant to be the single chokepoint that turns it into an isolated 'work copy' (hardened git clone, or a shutil.copytree for local folders) without ever touching the origin or executing anything inside it. bts_sbom's engine then walks that work copy: manifest parsers (bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/*) extract component names/versions verbatim from untrusted files with essentially no format validation, and license_scan.py opens and reads every non-binary file's bytes. These attacker-influenceable strings flow into: (a) deterministic remediation plans, which alone can reach patch.apply()/deliver() - itself gated behind explicit, default-False flags at every layer (CLI/pipeline/deliver.py/server.py) - and (b) a separate evidence/report layer (sbomplus/ai.py, chat.py, rag.py) that renders the same untrusted strings into markdown/JSON "evidence", indexes it for retrieval, and optionally sends it to the EXAONE LLM. The chatbot's and AI-proposal's safety nets (verbatim quote verification, 4-layer proposal validation) check that output text can be traced back to an evidence document, not that the document's content is honest - so once an attacker's string is inside the evidence corpus, it can be surfaced back out as a "verified" claim with no LLM required. Evidence/cache file paths are always built from sanitized (safe_name/sha256) components, so the untrusted content does not escape into the filesystem layout itself.

**강점**:
- Single, consistently-used no-shell subprocess wrapper (sbomplus/util.py:run) - repo-wide grep found zero shell=True, os.system, eval, exec, pickle, or yaml.load anywhere in ~23k lines.
- Git-clone hardening is substantial and layered: --no-recurse-submodules, --no-tags, core.hooksPath pointed at an empty temp dir, core.symlinks=false, protocol.ext.allow=never, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0/GIT_ASKPASS=echo to stop interactive credential prompts, and explicit refusal of leading-dash/ext::/fd::/http:// sources.
- LLM/EXAONE output is architecturally firewalled from the auto-patch/outward-action pipeline: patch.apply()/_patch_loop() consume only the deterministic remediate.plan() output, never ai.propose()'s result (confirmed by reading the full call graph in pipeline.py).
- Outward actions (origin write, remote push, PR open) are each individually gated behind explicit, default-False flags at the CLI, pipeline, and deliver() layers, and the HTTP server never exposes those flags at all, so even a mis-exposed server cannot be made to push.
- Evidence/cache file paths are always derived through safe_name() or a sha256 hash of content, not through raw attacker-controlled strings, so no path-traversal-into-the-evidence-tree was found.
- License/source scanning enforces honest, reported bounds (MAX_FILES=6000, 64KB/256KB per-file caps) rather than unbounded reads.
- API key handling is redacted everywhere it is actually surfaced to the user/evidence (one unreachable internal exception noted separately).

**설계상 약점**:
- The 'isolated work copy' boundary is enforced thoroughly for git-clone sources but not for local-folder sources: symlinks are explicitly neutralized during clone (core.symlinks=false) but explicitly preserved during the local-folder copytree (symlinks=True), an inconsistency that undermines the stated safety contract for an entire input modality.
- The chat/AI grounding model verifies textual provenance (is this quote a verbatim substring of a document?) but has no notion of evidence trustworthiness; it implicitly assumes the evidence text itself was produced honestly, which is false by construction once any part of that evidence is copied verbatim from an adversarial repository.
- Dangerous-source detection in normalize_source() is a set of ad hoc string/prefix/substring checks (case-sensitive here, case-insensitive there, whole-string-only leading-dash check) rather than a structural URL parse that validates scheme, authority and path independently - easy to add a new bypass to when the allowlist is next extended.
- No centralized identifier-sanitization layer: every one of the many manifest parsers trusts attacker-supplied map keys (dependency names) as safe, human-readable, display-ready strings; validation (if any) happens ad hoc per-ecosystem rather than once at a shared boundary.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/sbomplus/test_patch_delivery.py exercises normalize_source()'s documented refusal list (ext::, leading-dash/--upload-pack=, http://) and the branch/folder/inplace/github delivery targets, including a push+PR flow against a local bare repo reached via a file:// URI, plus approval-digest/token-mismatch handling. tests/sbomplus/test_server_cli.py checks the HTTP /api/scan handler rejects 'ext::sh -c id' with 400 and exercises basic job lifecycle. tests/sbomplus/test_ai_rag_loop.py (not fully read) appears to cover ai.py's validator layers and rag.py's retrieval quality/OOD rejection on the project's own (trusted) knowledge base and golden/dev query sets. None of the suite: (1) tests a case-variant transport string (EXT::/Ext::) or an ssh://-authority dash-injection URL, (2) exercises the local-folder symlink-preservation path at all (no 'symlink' hit anywhere under tests/), (3) feeds an adversarial/instruction-like dependency name through chat.py/ai.py to check that grounding survives attacker-controlled evidence content, or (4) feeds a crafted pom.xml entity payload or a malformed rpmdb.sqlite file into their respective parsers. bts_sbom's own tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py focuses on CLI/engine behavior over well-formed fixtures, not hostile-manifest-content safety.

**열린 질문**:
- Does any real deployment of sbomplus/server.py (e.g. via Dockerfile.sbomplus or a documented docker-compose) ever bind the HTTP server beyond 127.0.0.1, which would turn the ssh-host-injection and grounding-bypass defects into remotely triggerable issues? Not checked here (out of this review's file-content focus).
- What git/OpenSSH versions does CI/production actually run, and do they include the post-CVE-2017-1000117 mitigation against a dash-prefixed ssh host? workspace.py does not pin or check this itself.
- Is bts_sbom/_internal/manifests/rpm.py's sqlite/binary-header parser slated to be wired into the main manifest-discovery path for a future firmware/container-rootfs feature? If so it will need the same untrusted-input hardening review applied to the rest of bts_sbom before that happens.
- Should cli.py's evidence_chunks() continue to index full per-project markdown reports as generally-searchable 'evidence' RAG chunks (reachable by any unrelated question when no specific project is named), given that is what makes the chat-grounding bypass reachable without the user ever typing the attacker's exact string?

### lens-architecture — Cross-cutting: architecture, boundaries, duplication, maintainability

**목적**: Cross-cutting architecture/boundary/duplication/maintainability review of the whole beyondthesecurity-sbom repository: bts_sbom (regulatory SBOM/SCA core, v0.1.1) plus sbomplus (a stdlib-only layer on top claiming a single adapter touchpoint, engine.py, with approval-gated patching, legacy-architecture analysis, an EXAONE-backed proposal validator, a BM25 RAG chatbot, and a localhost HTTP server). The goal is to verify the stated adapter boundary, quantify duplication/drift between the two packages, find size/complexity hot spots, check public-API vs _internal leakage, check packaging boundaries, and assess error handling/global state/portability.

**구성 요소**:
- `sbomplus/engine.py`: Sole adapter importing/calling the real bts_sbom core; normalizes native OSV-Scanner output into sbomplus's component/vuln records; contains the single highest-complexity function in the repo (normalize(), cx=80).
- `bts_sbom/__init__.py`: Curated public API (__all__) re-exporting sbom/vex/osvscanner/sca_report/supply_chain/reachability/sectors; does not export _internal.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/__init__.py`: Docstring only — no __all__, no access control; 29 'vendored' implementation modules are fully importable despite the underscore convention.
- `bts_sbom/osvscanner.py`: Core OSV-Scanner-parity engine; own independent CVSS v3.1/v4.0 base-score implementation (_cvss_v31_base_score, _cvss_v40_base_score) used on raw OSV JSON.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py`: Vendored OSV HTTP client; post_query() is the only part actually used (by osvscanner.py); query_package/query_batch/_parse_vulns and its own separate CVSS v3/v4 implementation are dead code.
