- `sbomplus/cvss.py`: Third independent CVSS v3.1 base-score implementation (same FIRST-spec constants as the two bts_sbom copies), used for sbomplus's own normalized vuln records.
- `bts_sbom/_internal/license_categories.py`: Core's SPDX-id -> risk-category table and category compatibility engine (single source of truth, per its own docstring).
- `sbomplus/licenses.py`: Independent 553-line license resolution/normalization/category/policy module (own alias table, own category table, own policy engine) with no shared code with the core's license_* modules.
- `sbomplus/sectors.py`: Thin, non-duplicating delegation to bts_sbom's sector_report() for 6 of 7 industries; only 'maritime' (absent from the core) is implemented locally.
- `pyproject.toml`: Root build config: single 'bts-sbom' distribution bundling bts_sbom + sbomplus, version 0.1.1.
- `bts_sbom/pyproject.toml`: Second, independent 'bts-sbom' build config scoped to bts_sbom only, for a future standalone split; version string (0.1.0) has drifted from the code.
- `sbomplus/net.py`: Process-global HTTP helper with a single mutable STATS dict and a test-only TRANSPORT hook; reset_stats() exists but is not called from production code paths.
- `sbomplus/pipeline.py`: End-to-end per-project orchestration (workspace -> engine -> licenses -> remediate -> sbom -> sectors -> arch -> patch -> proposals); stage-isolated via _Stages.run but never resets net.STATS per project.
- `sbomplus/server.py`: Stdlib ThreadingHTTPServer, localhost-only, no auth; spawns one daemon thread per POST /api/scan job, sharing process-global net.STATS/licenses caches across concurrent jobs.
- `sbomplus/batch.py`: Multi-project batch runner; resets net.STATS exactly once per whole batch (not per project), optionally runs projects concurrently via ThreadPoolExecutor when jobs>1.
- `sbomplus/evals.py`: Resolves knowledge/ and evals/ by walking two directories up from its own installed file location — correct in a source checkout, silently empty once installed as a wheel.
- `tests/sbomplus/conftest.py`: Shared fixtures: FakeScanner stands in for bts_sbom.OsvScanner, net.TRANSPORT/llm.TRANSPORT block all real network/LLM calls, net.reset_stats() runs per test — the real engine<->core wiring is never exercised by this test suite.
- `tests/sbomplus/test_specs.py`: Two-way traceability check between specs/sbomplus_features.md EARS acceptance criteria and the test suite (speclint.trace).

**데이터 흐름**: A source (git URL/local folder) flows through sbomplus/workspace.py into an isolated work copy, then sbomplus/engine.py is the only place that imports the real bts_sbom package (sys.path/BTS_SBOM_PATH handling, lazy cached import) and drives bts_sbom.OsvScanner; its normalize() reshapes the core's native OSV-Scanner-shaped dict into sbomplus's own component/vuln schema using sbomplus's own cvss.py and versions.py (not the core's equivalents). From there sbomplus/licenses.py (independent of bts_sbom's license_* stack), sbomplus/remediate.py, sbomplus/sbom.py (which calls back into engine._import_core() for the core's SPDX builder/validator only), sbomplus/sectors.py (delegates to core.sector_report for 6/7 industries), sbomplus/arch.py and sbomplus/patch.py consume engine's output and each other's. sbomplus/pipeline.py is the single orchestrator stitching these into one per-project record, persisted as JSON/SBOMs/reports; sbomplus/batch.py and sbomplus/loop.py drive pipeline.py across many sources and feed sbomplus/evals.py + sbomplus/rag.py + sbomplus/chat.py, which separately read knowledge/ and evals/ off disk relative to the installed package location. sbomplus/server.py exposes the same pipeline.run_project/chat path over HTTP, one background thread per job, sharing sbomplus/net.py's process-global request counters and sbomplus/osv.py's in-memory advisory cache across all jobs.

**강점**:
- The core architectural claim fully holds: exhaustive grep confirms exactly one 'import bts_sbom' statement in all of sbomplus (engine.py:36), and zero references to 'sbomplus' anywhere inside bts_sbom/**/*.py — the boundary is real, not just documented.
- sbomplus/sectors.py avoids duplicating sector-profile logic: 6 of 7 industries delegate straight to bts_sbom's own sector_report(); only the genuinely-new 'maritime' profile is implemented in sbomplus.
- docs/CORE_ISSUES.md tracks known core defects with a workaround + a named regression test per item, and explicitly states the core is never patched directly — and this is actually followed (git status clean, no bts_sbom diffs).
- No bare 'except:' found anywhere in ~23k lines; the project consistently uses typed or at least 'except Exception as e' with logged/recorded context.
- Concrete, demonstrated Windows hardening: pathutil.norm_rel_path for \/ routing, git clone flags (core.symlinks=false, core.autocrlf=false, disabled hooksPath), a read-only-object rmtree workaround for Windows-locked .git objects, utf-8-sig handling, and a genuine 3-OS (ubuntu/windows/macos) CI matrix for the core.
- specs/sbomplus_features.md's EARS acceptance criteria are mechanically traced against the test suite both ways (tests/sbomplus/test_specs.py via speclint.trace) — a real two-way traceability check, not just a doc.

**설계상 약점**:
- bts_sbom/_internal has no __all__ or any enforcement of 'internal' — it is a naming convention only, and the core's own test suite (tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py) imports 8+ _internal submodules directly (version_match, remediation, policy, manifests, pathutil), so the nominally-private surface is exactly as refactor-fragile as the public one.
- CVSS v3.1 base-score scoring (FIRST spec section 7.1) is hand-implemented three separate times with identical magic constants and no shared helper: bts_sbom/osvscanner.py, bts_sbom/_internal/osv_client.py, and sbomplus/cvss.py — any future spec correction must be found and applied three times.
- License SPDX-id -> risk-category mapping is duplicated wholesale between the core's license_categories.py/license_norm.py/license_utils.py (~2000 combined lines) and sbomplus/licenses.py (553 lines) with no shared source of truth, and the two tables have already drifted on at least one real SPDX id (OSL-3.0).
- Version-ordering logic is implemented three separate times for three different purposes (bts_sbom's CPE-tolerant version_cmp.py, bts_sbom's PEP440/semver-precise version_match.py, sbomplus's approximate versions.py) with three different pre-release-qualifier rank tables and no cross-checking test, which is a plausible source of silent disagreement on the same version string.
- The adapter engine.py, whose whole premise is being the one small, auditable seam to the core, contains the single most complex function in the entire ~23k-line repository (normalize(), cyclomatic complexity 80) — the complexity the architecture is designed to contain is concentrated in the one place meant to stay simple.
- Two pyproject.toml files both declare the distribution name 'bts-sbom' but disagree on version (root: 0.1.1, bts_sbom/pyproject.toml: 0.1.0, while bts_sbom/__init__.py.__version__ is 0.1.1) — the standalone-core packaging path is stale relative to the code.
- sbomplus/evals.py resolves knowledge/ and evals/ by walking up from its own installed file path; since root pyproject.toml's packages.find/package-data never ships those two top-level directories, an sbomplus installed from the wheel described by its own packaging config would silently get an empty RAG index and empty eval sets rather than an error.
- sbomplus/net.py's STATS counters are process-global and are reset only inside test fixtures (conftest.py) and once per whole batch (batch.py), never inside pipeline.py or server.py — every persisted per-project 'license.stats.net' figure is actually a cumulative, and under the threaded HTTP server's concurrent /api/scan jobs, cross-contaminated, count rather than that project's own network activity.
- The one declared integration seam (engine._import_core/core_version/core_info, and engine.scan()'s real-scanner path) is never exercised by any test in tests/sbomplus — every call site injects scanner=FakeScanner(), so the actual wiring to the live bts_sbom core has zero automated coverage.

**테스트 커버리지**: tests/test_bts_sbom_pkg.py (633 lines) exercises the bts_sbom core broadly and directly against _internal submodules (version_match, remediation, policy incl. vex_evaluate, manifests, pathutil) — but only through the public bts_sbom package, never through sbomplus. tests/sbomplus/* (7 files, ~900 lines plus a 187-line conftest.py) cover engine normalization/adjustments, license resolution + the documented SPDX-parenthesis workaround, patch/delivery/regression-revert, sectors/arch, server+CLI smoke, AI/RAG/loop, and spec<->test traceability (test_specs.py). Every sbomplus test that calls engine.analyze()/engine.scan() passes an explicit scanner=FakeScanner() (confirmed via grep: zero matches for engine._import_core/core_version/core_info and zero scanner=None/default call sites), so the real bts_sbom<->sbomplus wiring the architecture is built around is untested end-to-end. All network and LLM calls are blocked and mocked via net.TRANSPORT/llm.TRANSPORT (conftest.py:149-155), which also means net.reset_stats() runs before every test, masking the production-path stats-reset gap found above. No test anywhere compares the core's and sbomplus's license-category tables or CVSS implementations against each other, so the OSL-3.0-style drift is structurally invisible to CI. Nothing exercises sbomplus/server.py under concurrent /api/scan load, and nothing exercises sbomplus/evals.py's knowledge/evals resolution from an installed (non-source-checkout) location.

**열린 질문**:
- Is bts_sbom/pyproject.toml's standalone-split packaging path actively maintained, or is it dormant scaffolding — if active, should its version be kept in lockstep with the root's (and with __init__.py.__version__) via a single source?
- Is sbomplus ever expected to be installed as a wheel separately from a source checkout of this repository? If so, should knowledge/ and evals/ become package-data (or move under sbomplus/), since the current path resolution only works in-tree?
- Given OsvClient.query_package/query_batch/_parse_vulns (and its independent CVSS/severity logic) are unreachable dead code, is osv_client.py meant to be trimmed to just post_query, or is this surface reserved for a planned caller?
- Would the project consider hoisting the CVSS v3.1 base-score formula and the SPDX-category table into one shared, tested helper (even if re-exported rather than literally shared, to preserve the 'core never depends on sbomplus' rule), given the two packages have already drifted on at least one real license id?
- Should net.STATS move to a per-call/contextvar-scoped counter now that sbomplus/server.py runs concurrent background scan jobs, or is per-project network accounting considered out of scope for the evidence files?

## 부록 G. 누락 점검 에이전트 결과

**종합 평가**: A substantial (~23k LOC), genuinely tested (106 collected tests, 104 passing offline when I ran them) two-package toolkit whose central selling point -- an explicit 'absent evidence is never a pass' honesty contract -- is real in design intent (verdict vocabularies, disclosure fields, fail-closed VEX) but is confirmed, with file:line precision, to be violated in enough load-bearing paths (OSS-Fuzz false-clean scans, GIT-range commit SHAs reported as fixed versions, CycloneDX-VEX validation bypass, symlink-preserving 'isolated' local folders, a reproducible cp949 console crash) that CI gates relying on it can pass when they should not. Architecturally the project is maturing but still fragile: core logic (version comparison, CVSS scoring, license categorization) is duplicated 2-3 times with no shared source of truth and has already drifted on real values, packaging is inconsistent (two divergent version numbers, unshipped data directories), and -- discovered only by going beyond the assigned slices -- an entire web-console feature exists wholly outside version control while already wired into locally-modified server/CLI code. Most importantly for anyone reading this review: the project is being actively, substantially renamed (sbomplus -> bts_sbom_app, confirmed staged in git mid-review) concurrently with this very analysis, so the file/line pointers in all 18 slices' findings are already a snapshot of a structure that no longer matches the live index.

**분석되지 않은 파일**:
- LICENSE and bts_sbom/LICENSE: never reviewed by any slice for SPDX/header consistency with the Apache-2.0 classifier claims made elsewhere (confirmed byte-identical to each other, but that's all I could cheaply verify).
- bts_sbom/_internal/__init__.py: absent from core-sbom-sector's explicit file list; its own docstring promises every vendored file's header 'names its exact origin', a claim no analyst tested and which I found false for 4 of 29 _internal modules (pathutil.py, policy.py, remediation.py, version_match.py lack the header).
- bts_sbom/py.typed: the PEP 561 marker referenced in pyproject.toml package-data but never mentioned by any slice (trivial, but technically uncovered).
- evidence/batch/runs/*/summary.html (15 files, one per run): sits alongside summary.json/summary.md in every run folder but was never named in the docs-data-evidence slice's file list (only .json and .md were listed).
- CRITICAL / time-sensitive: as of this review's own execution, the entire sbomplus/ package (29 tracked files), tests/sbomplus/, docs/SBOMPLUS.md, Dockerfile.sbomplus and .github/workflows/sbomplus_ci.yml are staged (git status shows R/RM) for rename to bts_sbom_app/, docs/WORKFLOW.md, Dockerfile.app and bts_sbom_app_ci.yml -- meaning `git ls-files` right now returns 0 files under sbomplus/ and 29 under bts_sbom_app/, so every file path cited by every one of the 18 slices above no longer resolves in the current index.
- sbomplus/console.py, sbomplus/web/ (12 files: index.html, css/, NOTICE.md, sha256 list, js), and tests/sbomplus/test_console_ui.py (now appearing as bts_sbom_app/console.py, bts_sbom_app/web/*, tests/bts_sbom_app/test_console_ui.py) were completely untracked by git (never committed on any branch) at review time, yet are imported and exercised by a locally-modified sbomplus/server.py (+192 uncommitted lines adding /api/view and a triage store) and sbomplus/cli.py -- a live, wired-in feature invisible to every analyst because it never existed in git history.

**놓친 분석 관점**:
- No analyst executed anything -- all 18 reviews are purely static/read-only, so none independently ran the test suite, reproduced a crash, or confirmed a CI claim; I did (see unchecked_claims/contradictions) and it surfaced new information.
- No analyst checked bts_sbom/_internal/__init__.py's 'each file's header names its exact origin' vendoring-honesty claim against the actual _internal/*.py headers (I found 4 of 29 missing it).
- No analyst verified sbomplus/pyproject.toml's package-data promise ('sbomplus = ["web/*", "web/css/*"]') against whether that directory is actually version-controlled (it is not, at all).
- No analyst flagged that several files they cite by line number (sbomplus/server.py, sbomplus/cli.py, pyproject.toml) were already uncommitted-modified in the working tree, so the content reviewed may not match either HEAD or what eventually ships.
- No analyst assessed Dockerfile.sbomplus's own security posture (base image pinning, running as root, etc.) even though it was explicitly in the tests-ci-packaging file list.
- No analyst cross-computed README/docs numeric claims (license resolution rate, test counts) directly against the raw evidence/batch/latest.json sitting in the repo; several were left as 'unverifiable' rather than recomputed from that file.
- No analyst measured actual runtime of the flagged quadratic/O(n^2) hotspots (callgraph.py CallGraph.__init__, engine.py normalize() at cyclomatic complexity 80) against a real codebase to gauge real-world severity versus theoretical risk.

**점검되지 않은 주장**:
- "Standard library only, zero third-party runtime dependencies": technically true in both pyproject.toml files (dependencies = []), but never reconciled with core-manifests' separately-confirmed finding that tomllib isn't stdlib on the declared minimum Python 3.10.
- sbomplus packaging's promise to ship web/*, web/css/* as package-data: false today, since sbomplus/web/ is not tracked by git at all (confirmed via git status and git check-ignore).
- tests-ci-packaging's stated test counts ("28 tests" bts_sbom, "69 tests" sbomplus): bts_sbom's 28 is confirmed correct via `pytest --collect-only`, but sbomplus currently collects 78 tests, not 69 (106 total, not 97).
- sbomplus_ci.yml's steps "pass locally with all network faked (not yet run on GitHub)": I ran the full offline suite directly; 104/106 tests pass, with the 2 failures being the already-documented cp949 console-encoding crash reproduced live when PYTHONUTF8 isn't set -- so the claim is mostly but not unconditionally true.

**분석가 간 모순**:
- tests-ci-packaging's sbomplus test count (69) contradicts a direct, cheap `pytest --collect-only` run performed during this review, which found 78 sbomplus tests (106 total including bts_sbom's 28).
- Virtually every slice's file+line citations (e.g. sbomplus/server.py:82/100, sbomplus/cli.py, pyproject.toml) assume a stable sbomplus/ layout, but the working tree now has all of those paths staged for rename into bts_sbom_app/, with sbomplus/server.py specifically carrying ~192 uncommitted added lines (a console/triage subsystem) that none of the 18 analysts described -- the artifact being cited and the artifact on disk have already diverged.
- docs-data-evidence's confirmed finding that 'root CHANGELOG.md has no 0.1.1 entry' is itself in flux: CHANGELOG.md is one of the files currently mid-edit in the same uncommitted changeset discovered during this review, so the finding's truth value may already be stale by the time anyone reads it.

**강점 상위**:
- A real, independently-reproducible automated test suite (106 tests, 104 passing in an offline run performed during this review) substantiates the project's own quality claims more than most projects at this scale.
- The 'absent evidence is never a pass' honesty philosophy is deliberately and consistently threaded through nearly every subsystem's design (verdict vocabularies, disclosure fields, fail-closed VEX, offline-DB staleness plumbing), not just marketing language.
- The core bts_sbom package is genuinely dependency-free (dependencies = [] verified in its own pyproject.toml), an unusual and valuable discipline for a security-tooling package.
- The 18-analyst review itself is unusually rigorous and self-skeptical, grounding almost every claim with a specific file:line defect and appropriately hedged open questions rather than vague impressions.

**위험 상위**:
- The honesty contract is violated in enough load-bearing places (false-clean OSS-Fuzz scans, fabricated fixed-versions from GIT-range commit SHAs, VEX validation bypass, symlink-breaking work-copy isolation) that the tool's core CI-gate value proposition cannot currently be trusted at face value.
- Release/packaging hygiene is weak enough (divergent version numbers across two pyproject.toml files, data directories not shipped, and a web-console feature that lives entirely outside version control) that a fresh clone-and-install would likely not work as documented today.
- The codebase is mid-rename (sbomplus -> bts_sbom_app) concurrently with this analysis, so every file/line remediation pointer this 18-analyst review produced risks pointing at paths that no longer exist by the time anyone acts on them.
- Core logic (version comparison, CVSS scoring, license categorization) is independently reimplemented 2-3 times with no cross-checking test, and has already produced confirmed, real disagreements (e.g. OSL-3.0 and CC-BY-SA-4.0 license categorization) -- a standing drift risk that will keep generating silent inconsistencies.
